# Changelog

All notable changes to NanoClaw will be documented in this file.

For detailed release notes, see the [full changelog on the documentation site](https://docs.nanoclaw.dev/changelog).

## [1.2.121] - 2026-07-21

- Documented the platform-core vs personal-domain boundary (#853): new `docs/CORE-VS-DOMAIN.md` maps the #844 seams (IPC registry, lifecycle hooks, spawn gates, location sinks, sidecar config, host-plugin registration) with the Hubitat/flight-assist extractions as reference examples, a "where does this go?" checklist (tile skill / overlay / host plugin / sidecar / core), and the private-fork rules (host plugins private-by-default, public sync must not grow personal domain). Linked from CLAUDE.md; the "just change the code" (REQUIREMENTS) and "handful of files" (README) claims now carry the core-vs-domain caveat.

## [1.2.120] - 2026-07-21

- Removed the `index.ts` re-export barrel left behind by the #749 split (#852): the compatibility shims for `escapeXml`/`formatMessages`, `wipeSessionJsonl`, `_setRegisteredGroups`, `getAvailableGroups`/`isStaleSessionError`, `hydrateRegisteredGroupTriggerPatterns`, and the gate orchestration helpers are gone. Every test now imports from the owning module (`message-pipeline`, `orchestrator-state`, `session-wipe`, `group-registry`, `gates/orchestrator`); `index.ts` is startup wiring only. No orchestrator behavior change — nanoclaw is a deployed application, not a published library, and nothing consumes `dist/index.js` as a module entrypoint (the package is now marked `"private": true` to make that explicit).

## [1.2.119] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the Hubitat listener + `smart_home_events` accessors into a self-contained host plugin (#848): `src/hubitat-listener.ts` → `src/host-plugins/hubitat/listener.ts`, `src/db-smart-home.ts` → `src/host-plugins/hubitat/db.ts`, registration in `src/host-plugins/hubitat/index.ts`. The lifecycle hooks now load the listener via dynamic import, so with `HUBITAT_HUB_IP` unset the plugin registers no hooks and the listener/accessor modules never load — no connect attempts on an unconfigured install (only the small registration entrypoint is evaluated). The `smart_home_events` schema migration stays in core `src/db.ts` with ownership documented at the CREATE TABLE; the `HUBITAT_*` config knobs stay in `src/config.ts` with the plugin contract documented (three of the four are reserved for the epic:smart-home product phases). Behavior with the env set is unchanged; listener/accessor bodies moved verbatim.

## [1.2.118] - 2026-07-21

- Migrated the ops IPC commands to the #845 registry (slice 6, final): `persist_tz_segments` / `fetch_markdown` / `github_backup` / `persist_global_file` / `run_sidecar` / `promote_staging` / `tessl_update` / `list_installed_tiles` / `push_staged_to_branch` move from the `processTaskIpc` switch to `src/ipc-handlers/ops.ts`, and the git/persona machinery they share (`runGit`, `gitAuthEnv`, `redactGitToken`, `PERSISTABLE_GLOBAL_FILES`, `validateGlobalFilesToPersist`, `ensurePersonaRepo`, `persistGlobalFilesToGit`, `runPersonaPersistTask`, `runSerializedPersonaPersist`, `backupCommitAndPush`) moves to the new `src/git-persist.ts`. Bodies are verbatim transplants — every in-handler isMain gate, result-envelope convention, and outer-boundary catch unchanged; the tz decision helpers (`classifyTzPersist`, `coerceTzSegments`, `MAX_TZ_SEGMENTS`) and `KNOWN_TILE_NAMES` ride along into `ops.ts`. The legacy switch is gone — `processTaskIpc` is now registry dispatch plus an unknown-type warn. Machinery tests split to `src/git-persist.test.ts` and `src/ipc-handlers/ops.test.ts`. The `nanoclaw-host` `persona-persist-direct-push` rule's `src/ipc.ts` references update in lock-step (tile-repo PR). `ipc.ts` 2550 -> 808 lines.

## [1.2.117] - 2026-07-21

- Migrated the chat-admin IPC commands to the #845 registry (slice 5): `nuke_session` / `chat_status` / `inspect_gate_decisions` / `nuke_chat` / `send_message_to_chat` move from the `processTaskIpc` switch to `src/ipc-handlers/chat-admin.ts`. The four admin-tile commands keep their in-handler isMain gate (their error envelopes are written unconditionally, including to the orphan result path on a bad requestId — the dispatcher's `requiresMain` gate only writes for valid ids); `nuke_session` stays self-scoped to the verified sourceGroup. Bodies are verbatim transplants: the chat_id/chat_name disambiguation ladder, pre-nuke status snapshot, pool-vs-direct broadcast routing with phantom-row gating, and both outer-boundary result-envelope catches unchanged. Only the ops slice (named host operations) remains in the legacy switch. `ipc.ts` 3294 → 2550 lines.

## [1.2.116] - 2026-07-21

- Added the generic location-sink registry and moved the flight-assist artifact writer to a host plugin (#849): the channel `onLocation` callback now does `storeLocation` + `runLocationSinks` — core owns location persistence and the fan-out list (`src/location-sinks.ts`, sinks run in registration order, each an isolated outer-boundary catch), while the travel tile's `current-location.json` write (owner-only filtering intact) registers as `src/host-plugins/flight-assist-location-sink.ts`. `registerHostPlugins()` now runs BEFORE channels connect so sinks are registered before the first inbound location. No flight-assist import remains in the core message path; DB location rows unchanged.

## [1.2.115] - 2026-07-21

- Migrated the learned-trigger IPC commands to the #845 registry (slice 4): `list_learned_triggers` / `promote_learned_trigger` / `reenable_learned_trigger` / `delete_learned_trigger` move from the `processTaskIpc` switch to `src/ipc-handlers/learned-triggers.ts`, keeping owner-of-the-bill authorization gated in-handler (the list command implicitly scopes a non-main caller to its own folder and writes the cross-folder-denied envelope). Bodies are verbatim transplants — the {kind, pattern} identity matching, no-trim-on-pattern rule, and snapshot refreshes unchanged. `ipc.ts` 3659 → 3294 lines.

## [1.2.114] - 2026-07-21

- Migrated the config-override IPC commands to the #845 registry (slice 3): `set_agent_model` / `set_maintenance_agent_model` / `set_session_caps` / `set_task_agent_model` / `set_additional_tiles` move from the `processTaskIpc` switch to `src/ipc-handlers/group-config.ts`. The model/cap commands keep owner-of-the-bill authorization gated in-handler; trust-adjacent `set_additional_tiles` is main-only via the dispatcher's `requiresMain` gate. Bodies are verbatim transplants — validation, containerConfig sibling-field preservation, the cadence-registry-row write refusal, and snapshot refreshes unchanged. `ipc.ts` 4181 → 3659 lines.

## [1.2.113] - 2026-07-21

- Made the named-sidecar registry data-driven (#850, first P1 child of #844): sidecar specs move from TypeScript literals in `sidecar-runner.ts` (which hard-coded the audible-backup NAS mount paths) to a gitignored `config/sidecars.json`, loaded and validated per `run_sidecar` call by the new `src/sidecar-config.ts` and exposed to the orchestrator container via a new read-only `./config:/app/config` compose mount. Missing file = empty registry with a discoverable unknown-name error; invalid file (bad JSON, wrong shape, unset `${VAR}` in a mount) = entry-specific actionable error envelope, never a silently-empty registry. `${VAR}` placeholders in mounts expand from the host env plus a `HOST_PROJECT_PARENT` builtin preserving the pre-#850 `.audible` resolution. Trust model unchanged from #750 — image/mounts/flag-allowlist come from trusted host config, never the IPC payload. `config/sidecars.example.json` documents the shape, `config/README.md` the add-a-sidecar-without-a-TS-change workflow.

## [1.2.112] - 2026-07-21

- Added host lifecycle hooks (#847, third P0 child of #844): `src/host-lifecycle.ts` registers named startup/shutdown hooks that run in registration order, each isolated — a hook's throw or rejection is logged with the hook's name and never skips the remaining hooks or the platform teardown that follows. First consumer: Hubitat. `main()` no longer hard-codes `startHubitatListener`/`stopHubitatListener` — `src/host-plugins/hubitat.ts` registers both hooks via `registerHostPlugins()`, gated on `HUBITAT_HUB_IP` so an unconfigured install registers nothing (full listener extraction is #848). No behavior change when no plugins register.

## [1.2.111] - 2026-07-21

- Migrated the group-registry IPC commands to the #845 registry (slice 2): `refresh_groups` / `register_group` / `unregister_group` / `set_trusted` / `set_trigger` move from the `processTaskIpc` switch to `src/ipc-handlers/groups.ts`, using the dispatcher's `requiresMain` gate in place of per-case isMain checks (fire-and-forget commands warn and drop on unauthorized callers, as before). Also isolates the registry between tests — `ipc-registry.test.ts` wipes the module-global handler map and the core-registration once-guard in `afterEach` via test-only `_reset*ForTests` helpers (same testing-standards finding the fleet reviewer raised on #856's spawn-gate tests). `ipc.ts` 4505 → 4181 lines.

## [1.2.110] - 2026-07-21

- Made pre-spawn gates registrable and removed the hard-coded `tessl__flight-assist` entry from core (#846, second P0 child of #844): `src/spawn-gates.ts` now owns only the mechanism — `registerSpawnGate` / `evaluateSpawnGate`, duplicate-name throw, and the shared fail-open/closed fs-errno narrowing helper. The trip-window policy (travel-db.json symlink-contained resolve, 24h lead/trail window arithmetic, fail-open/closed asymmetry) moved verbatim to `src/host-plugins/flight-assist-spawn-gate.ts`, registered at startup through the new idempotent `registerHostPlugins()` entry point (called before `startSchedulerLoop` so gates are in place before the first fire). Behavior preserved: skills without a registered gate spawn unconditionally, and the #754 trip-window semantics are pinned by the tests, which moved with their owner.

## [1.2.109] - 2026-07-21

- Introduced the host IPC command registry (#845, first P0 child of the #844 host-plugin-architecture epic): `src/ipc-registry.ts` owns handler registration (`registerIpcHandler` / `dispatchIpcTask`, with an opt-in `requiresMain` admin gate) plus the shared `IpcTaskPayload` type and `scriptResultPath`, moved verbatim out of `ipc.ts`. First migrated slice: the five scheduled-task lifecycle commands (`schedule_task` / `pause_task` / `resume_task` / `cancel_task` / `update_task`) now register from `src/ipc-handlers/tasks.ts`; `processTaskIpc` dispatches through the registry first and falls through to the legacy switch for not-yet-migrated commands. No behavior change — task-file shape, result-file paths, and authorization gates unchanged (pinned by `ipc-auth.test.ts`, now exercising the migrated handlers through the registry; new `ipc-registry.test.ts` covers dispatch, duplicate registration, and the admin gate). `ipc.ts` 5249 → 4505 lines.

## [1.2.108] - 2026-07-21

- Ended the `db.ts` barrel role (#842, the #751 follow-up): all 40 consumer files now import DB accessors directly from their owning `src/db-<domain>.ts` module, and the re-export blocks are removed from `db.ts` so typecheck enforces the import discipline. `db.ts` is 911 lines — `initDatabase`/`createSchema`, the state-migration runner, and test helpers only. Test plumbing moved with it: `vi.mock('./db.js', …)` targets retargeted to the domain modules the subjects now import (the old mocks silently stopped intercepting), and `vi.resetModules()`-style dynamic imports split per module. No runtime behavior change.

## [1.2.107] - 2026-07-21

- Completed the #751 `src/db.ts` god-file split — seams 6–9 land the four remaining domain extracts (PRs #837–#840), all on the seam-2 `db-connection.ts` live-binding pattern with `db.ts` re-exports so call sites and tests are unchanged, no schema or query changes:
  - **Seam 6** (#837): messages/chats/reactions → `src/db-messages.ts` (21 symbols, `storeChatMetadata` … `getLastFromMeMessages` incl. the Telegram synthetic bot-id two-pass lookup). `db.ts` 3009 → 2477.
  - **Seam 7** (#838): scheduled tasks + run logs → `src/db-tasks.ts` (19 symbols incl. `rebuildCadenceRegistryForGroup`). Also fixes the pre-init guard in `rebuildCadenceRegistryForGroup`, dead since seam 2 made the pre-init handle an always-truthy Proxy sentinel — `db-connection.ts` now tracks registration explicitly (`isDbHandleRegistered()`), with a new `db-tasks.test.ts` pinning the function-specific error (flagged by Copilot). `db.ts` 2477 → 1999.
  - **Seam 8** (#839): timezone/location → `src/db-tz.ts` (`tz_state`/`locations`/`follow_me_tasks`: the #574 location cascade, TripIt segment walking, `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory`, follow-me pending-run lock helpers, `TRANSIENT_SQLITE_CODES`). `tz-resolver.ts` and `db-json-migrations.ts` retarget imports to `db-tz.js` so the pre-existing `walkTzSegments` ↔ `resolveCurrentTz` cycle stays two-module and call-time-only. `db.ts` 1999 → 1126.
  - **Seam 9** (#840): smart-home events → `src/db-smart-home.ts`, plus `db-smart-home.test.ts` covering the previously-untested accessors (insert round-trip, filters, hour-window bounds, frozen-clock retention cleanup, latest-state rollup — coverage gap surfaced by the fleet reviewer's testing-standards check).
  - **End-state reached:** `db.ts` is 1040 lines (from 6545 at issue re-measure) — `initDatabase`/`createSchema`, the state-migration runner, test helpers, and the re-export barrel. Every domain accessor lives in its `src/db-<domain>.ts` module. Full suite 174 files / 3835 tests green.

## [1.2.106] - 2026-07-21

- Flipped the orchestrator's `.env` bind mount to read-only (`./.env:/app/.env:ro`, #835). The mount was RW solely so the in-host Trakt OAuth autorefresh could persist rotated tokens back to `.env`; that last writer was retired when Trakt moved to the OneCLI gateway (#817). Audit confirms every remaining `.env` access goes through the read-only `readEnvFile` in `src/env.ts`, so the write surface on the credentials file is closed.

## [1.2.105] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the session accessors out of `src/db.ts` into `src/db-sessions.ts` (#751 seam 5). Verbatim move of the `sessions` table domain — `getSession`, `setSession`, `deleteSession`, `deleteSessionName`, `deleteAllSessions`, `getAllSessions` — onto the `db-connection.ts` live-binding pattern, re-exported from `db.ts` so call sites and tests are unchanged. No schema or query changes. `db.ts` 3076 → 3007 lines.

## [1.2.104] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the registered-group accessors out of `src/db.ts` into `src/db-registered-groups.ts` (#751 seam 4). Verbatim move of the `registered_groups` domain — the container-config / trigger-pattern parsers (#81/#82 config machinery incl. `parseContainerConfig`, `parseTriggerPatternColumn`, `legacyTriggerToConfig`, serializers) and the nine public accessors (`deriveTriggerString`, `getTriggerPatterns`, `setTriggerPatterns`, `getRegisteredGroup`, `setRegisteredGroup`, `updateGroupTrusted`, `updateGroupTrigger`, `deleteRegisteredGroup`, `getAllRegisteredGroups`) — onto the seam-2 `db-connection.ts` live-binding pattern, re-exported from `db.ts` so call sites and tests are unchanged. `isTriggerPatternConfig` is now exported for the one remaining `db.ts` consumer (the createSchema-time legacy trigger_pattern backfill, which stays with the schema). No schema or query changes. `db.ts` 3651 → 3076 lines.

## [1.2.103] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the session-length cap state accessors (#413 section) out of `src/db.ts` into `src/db-session-length-cap.ts` (#751 seam 3). Verbatim move of `SessionLengthRow` / `SessionResetReason` / `PendingReset` and `getSessionLengthState` / `recordSessionTurn` / `markSessionForReset` / `consumeSessionReset` / `clearSessionLengthStateForGroup` onto the seam-2 `db-connection.ts` live-binding pattern; `db.ts` re-exports everything so call sites (`message-pipeline`, `ipc`, session-cap tests) are unchanged. The section header's stale "`src/index.ts` runAgent" writer attribution is corrected to `message-pipeline.ts` (post-#749). No schema or query changes. `db.ts` 3878 → 3651 lines.

## [1.2.102] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the shared SQLite connection handle and the router-state accessors out of `src/db.ts` (#751 seam 2). New `src/db-connection.ts` owns the `db` live binding: `initDatabase` / `_initTestDatabase` still open the database and register the handle via `setDbHandle`, so single-connection ownership (WAL + busy_timeout, one open handle per process) is unchanged — domain modules read the live binding at call time instead of a module-local `let`. First domain on the pattern: `getRouterState` / `setRouterState` move to `src/db-router-state.ts`, re-exported from `db.ts` so call sites and tests are unchanged. This is the enabler for the remaining #751 seams (session-length cap, registered groups, sessions), mirroring the `orchestrator-state.ts` enabler from the #749 split.

## [1.2.101] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the one-shot JSON→SQL state importers out of the `src/db.ts` persistence god-file into a new `src/db-json-migrations.ts` (#751 seam 1 — the issue's priority-1 cut). Moves `migrateJsonState` (the `initDatabase` dispatch), the nine per-group `migrate*JsonFiles` importers (#293 wave: orders, morning-brief, calendar, heartbeat, task-tz, trusted-session, nanoclaw-state, scheduled-reminders, email-feedback), `emitMigrationStartupSummary`, and their private shape interfaces/helpers (`firstNonEmpty`, `resolveFollowMeTaskShape`, `missingRequiredScheduledReminderFields`). Behavior-preserving: `initDatabase` passes the connection handle explicitly (`migrateJsonState(db)`) so `db.ts` keeps single-connection ownership; the DATA_DIR migrations keep writing through the `db.ts` accessors (call-time circular import, same pattern as the #749 extractions); `SUPPORTED_TZ_STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` is now exported from `db.ts` for the tz-state importer; `db.ts` re-exports `firstNonEmpty` so `orders-json-migration.test.ts` is unchanged. `db.ts` drops **6545 → 3878 lines**. Full suite green (172 files, 3821 tests). Remaining #751 seams: router state, session-length cap, registered groups, sessions, then the high-coupling messages/tasks/tz domains.

## [1.2.100] - 2026-07-21

- Made the message pipeline's best-effort channel calls crash-safe (#826). The two awaited `channel.setTyping?.(...)` calls around the agent run now go through a `setTypingBestEffort` helper (rejection → `logger.warn`, never a throw), so a typing-indicator transport failure can no longer abort processing or skip the `releaseIdleTimerControl` cleanup; the fire-and-forget circuit-breaker trip notification (`mainChannel?.sendMessage(...)`) gets a `.catch` → `logger.warn`, so a channel rejection during an already-degraded state no longer surfaces as an unhandled rejection. These were pre-existing patterns moved verbatim by the #825 extraction and deferred to keep that move behavior-preserving. Adds 4 unit tests for the helper (forwarding, async rejection, sync throw, channels without `setTyping`).

## [1.2.99] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the inline `IpcDeps` object out of `main` into a new `src/ipc-deps.ts` (#749 seam 4b — the final thin-`main` cut). The ~344-line `startIpcWatcher({...})` literal becomes an `export const ipcDeps: IpcDeps`; `main` now wires `startIpcWatcher(ipcDeps)`. Behavior-preserving: every handler already closed over module-level singletons (`queue`/`channels`/`registeredGroups`/`sessions`/`pendingReplyTo`/`nukeTimestamps` from the orchestrator-state/runtime modules) and the extracted pipeline/registry functions — no `main`-local state — so the object moves as a plain import graph, not a DI factory. Adds `src/ipc-deps.test.ts` (3 tests) for the channel-guard handlers (`sendMessage` throws / delegates, `sendReaction` no-ops without a channel). `index.ts` drops **1152 → 788 lines** — now under the ~1k composition-root target. **This closes the #749 epic**: `src/index.ts` is process bootstrap + `startX()` wiring, with the inbound pipeline (`message-pipeline.ts`), gate orchestration (`gates/orchestrator.ts`), shared state (`orchestrator-state.ts`), runtime singletons (`orchestrator-runtime.ts`), group registry (`group-registry.ts`), classifier (`message-classify.ts`), circuit breaker (`circuit-breaker.ts`), session-wipe (`session-wipe.ts`), and IPC wiring (`ipc-deps.ts`) all owned by named modules. `index.ts` went 3533 (issue open) → 788.

## [1.2.98] - 2026-07-21

- Extracted the group-registry helpers out of the `src/index.ts` god-file into a new `src/group-registry.ts` (#749 seam 4a — thinning `main`). Moves `registerGroup` (the hub) plus its startup-maintenance siblings `cleanupOrphanNonMainHeartbeats`, `logRegisteredGroupOrphans`, `hydrateRegisteredGroupTriggerPatterns`, and the internal `chownRecursive`. Behavior-preserving: shared registry state (`registeredGroups`) comes from `orchestrator-state.ts` (a live-binding import the functions read and mutate in place), and `index.ts` re-exports `hydrateRegisteredGroupTriggerPatterns` so `register-group.test.ts` is unchanged while importing the three that `main` / the IPC-deps object still call. `recoverPendingMessages` stays with bootstrap (it drives the pipeline). Adds `src/group-registry.test.ts` (3 tests) covering the previously-untested `cleanupOrphanNonMainHeartbeats` non-main/main/no-task branches. `index.ts` drops 1374 → 1152. Remaining #749: seam 4b — extract the inline `IpcDeps` object to get `main` to bootstrap + `startX()` wiring and `index.ts` under ~1k.

## [1.2.97] - 2026-07-20

- Moved the inbound message pipeline out of the `src/index.ts` god-file into a dedicated `src/message-pipeline.ts` (#749 — the core seam-3 cut). Relocates the three-function loop `startMessageLoop` → `processGroupMessages` → `runAgent` plus their pipeline-local helpers (`installIdleTimerControl`, `messageLoopRunning`, `getAvailableGroups`, `isStaleSessionError`). Behavior-preserving: the pipeline pulls shared state from `orchestrator-state.ts` (cursors/sessions/registry, via #824's live bindings) and the shared runtime singletons — `queue`, `channels`, `pendingReplyTo`, `nukeTimestamps` — from a new `src/orchestrator-runtime.ts` (all `const`, mutated in place, so `index.ts` and the pipeline share one live reference). `index.ts` re-exports `getAvailableGroups` / `isStaleSessionError` so `routing.test.ts` / `stale-session.test.ts` are unchanged, and imports the loop entry points it wires in `main`. `index.ts` drops **2644 → 1373 lines** — now primarily composition root + IPC-deps wiring. Full suite green (169 files, 3812 tests); no functional change (diff is a move + import rewrite). Remaining #749 work: thin `main` (extract the large inline IPC-deps object / lifecycle wiring) if it earns its own cut.

## [1.2.96] - 2026-07-20

- Centralised the shared orchestrator state out of the `src/index.ts` god-file into a new `src/orchestrator-state.ts` (#749 seam-3 enabler, ahead of the message-pipeline move). Moves the four cross-cut singletons (`lastTimestamp`, `sessions`, `registeredGroups`, `lastAgentTimestamp`) plus their persistence helpers `loadState` / `saveState` / `getOrRecoverCursor` and the `_setRegisteredGroups` test helper. The singletons are exported as live bindings so `index.ts`'s existing reads and in-place mutations resolve through the import untouched; the only reassignments (all inside the module: `loadState`, `_setRegisteredGroups`) plus the `startMessageLoop` cursor advance (now via the new `setLastTimestamp` setter) are the exhaustive set that couldn't cross a live-binding boundary. `index.ts` re-exports `_setRegisteredGroups` so `routing.test.ts` / `router-integration.test.ts` are unchanged. Also drops an orphaned `isReplyToBot` doc-comment left stranded by the #823 classifier extraction. Behavior-preserving; `index.ts` 2700 → 2644. Adds `src/orchestrator-state.test.ts` (8 tests) for the previously-untested state helpers (load/save/cursor-recovery/corrupted-JSON paths). Sets up the core-loop extraction (`processGroupMessages` / `runAgent` / `startMessageLoop` → `message-pipeline.ts`) as the next #749 cut, which can now import shared state by name.

## [1.2.95] - 2026-07-20

- Extracted two self-contained concerns out of the `src/index.ts` god-file as #749 seam-3 prep, ahead of the larger message-pipeline move. (1) The inbound "addressed to us" classifier (`isReplyToBot`, `isAddressedToUs`) → new `src/message-classify.ts`. (2) The per-group circuit breaker (failure counter + cooldown, previously the inline `consecutiveFailures` / `circuitBreakerUntil` maps) → new `src/circuit-breaker.ts`, exposing `checkCircuitBreaker` (mutating loop gate), `isBreakerActive` (read-only status for the `chat_status` IPC), `recordFailure`, `recordSuccess`. Behavior-preserving: the trip log + main-group notification stay at the `runAgent` call site (they need the channel/registry singletons); the module owns only the state machine. Both concerns were previously untested inline helpers — this adds `src/message-classify.test.ts` (9 tests) and `src/circuit-breaker.test.ts` (7 tests, frozen clock). `index.ts` drops to ~2700 lines. The core loop (`processGroupMessages` / `runAgent` / `startMessageLoop`) stays tracked in #749 as the next cut.

## [1.2.94] - 2026-07-20

- Extracted the Stage-1 gate orchestration helpers out of the `src/index.ts` god-file into a dedicated `src/gates/orchestrator.ts` (#749 seam 2). Behavior-preserving move of `resolveGatesForGroup`, `evaluateGateChain`, `gateAllowsSpawn`, and their private helpers `buildGateContext` / `stripReplyQuotePrefix` / `REMOVED_GATE_NAMES`, doc comments intact; `index.ts` imports the two it still calls from the message pipeline (`resolveGatesForGroup`, `evaluateGateChain`) and re-exports all three so `src/gates/router-integration.test.ts` and `src/inspect-gate-decisions.test.ts` — which import them from `./index.js` — are unchanged. `isReplyToBot` / `isAddressedToUs` stay in `index.ts`; they belong to the message-pipeline seam (#749 seam 3), not gate evaluation. `index.ts` drops ~231 lines (2980 → 2749) with no functional change; the remaining seams (message pipeline, thin `main`) stay tracked in #749.

## [1.2.93] - 2026-07-19

- Retired the host-side Trakt-access machinery now that Trakt fetch runs in-container through the OneCLI custom-oauth gateway (the gateway owns Trakt auth + refresh), the Trakt half of #748 / #741 (#817). Deletes the `fetch_trakt_history` IPC case (`src/ipc.ts`) and its MCP tool (`container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts`), plus the obsolete host device-auth flow (`scripts/trakt-auth.py` + `scripts/test_trakt_auth.py`) — auth is now the OneCLI consent, so the host no longer runs a device-code flow, reads the personal tokens, or refreshes the grant. The scrub/classification scripts drop their Trakt hooks: `sync-to-public.sh` loses the `trakt-auth.py` exclude, the `fetch_trakt_history` scrub entries, and the vestigial `fetch_trakt` replace; `tile-repo-lib.sh` drops `fetch_trakt` from its `admin_pattern` regexes. The CI `Python tests (trakt-auth)` step is removed (it invoked the now-deleted `scripts.test_trakt_auth`). Stale references to `fetch_trakt_history`/`trakt-auth` are cleared from `docs/SECURITY.md` (Trakt moves from the host-scripts row to a gateway-injected row, no container credential), the #544 container-runner comment + `container-runner.maintenance-blocklist.test.ts` comments (generalized off the deleted handler to `mcp__nanoclaw__*`), the `docker-compose.yml` RW-`.env`-mount comment, and `.env.example`. The container carries no Trakt config: the OneCLI gateway injects both the OAuth Bearer (custom-oauth) and the `trakt-api-key` header (a vaulted header-injection secret holding the client id) on `api.trakt.tv` requests, so `TRAKT_CLIENT_ID` is not forwarded into the container. Kept: the historical #748 CHANGELOG entry, and `TRAKT_CLIENT_ID`/`TRAKT_CLIENT_SECRET`/`TRAKT_ACCESS_TOKEN`/`TRAKT_REFRESH_TOKEN` in `.env.example` pending the separate ops-side NAS `.env` strip (all now live in the OneCLI vault). Gated on `jbaruch/nanoclaw-media#57` (in-container gateway fetch shipped, media@0.1.38) AND the gateway `trakt-api-key` header-injection secret being live — must not deploy before both or Trakt breaks.

## [1.2.92] - 2026-07-19

- Fixed `persist_global_file` failing with `git add: fatal: not a git repository`, which made every approved persona self-improvement non-durable — the #393 failure mode via a new cause (#471). The handler ran its `git add`/`commit`/`push` in the orchestrator's cwd (`process.cwd()` → `/app`), the built image directory, which is not a git worktree: `docker-compose.yml` bind-mounts `./groups`, `./data`, etc. but never the repo's `.git`, so there was no repo to commit into. PR #700 shipped this from day one and only ever passed because the unit tests inject a real temp repo — the runtime path was never exercised until an operator approved soul-searching edits. Persist now operates on a dedicated, self-provisioning clone of the deploy-source repo (new `ensurePersonaRepo`) under `data/persona-repo` (gitignored, mounted, persistent): first run shallow-clones `main`, later runs fetch + hard-reset + clean to `origin/main` so a stranded prior state can't ride along. The handler overlays the approved persona files from the live runtime mirror (`groups/global/<file>`) onto that clean clone, then commits and pushes `HEAD:main` through the unchanged `persistGlobalFilesToGit` boundary (its push-time allowlist gate still bounds the direct-push to the persona files), so `deploy.sh`'s `git pull origin main` keeps the edit across deploys. "No repo" now surfaces as a distinct `git clone`/`git fetch`/`git reset` stage error instead of being conflated with persist's "No changes" no-op (issue ask #2). Clone URL is configurable via `ORCHESTRATOR_REPO_URL` (default: the private deploy source). New coverage exercises the fresh-clone, existing-clone-refresh, clone-failure (token-redacted), and end-to-end overlay-then-push paths.

## [1.2.91] - 2026-07-19

- Moved the `audible_backup` handler out of the orchestrator core behind a generic privileged-sidecar runner (#750, part of #741). A new `src/sidecar-runner.ts` holds a named-sidecar registry — `name → { image, mounts, baseArgs, allowedFlags, timeout, maxBuffer }` — seeded with the `audible-backup` entry; a plugin references a sidecar by name and may append only registry-allowlisted flags (`--dry-run`), while the image and host bind-mount paths live in trusted host config and never come from the IPC payload, so a compromised container cannot request `-v /:/…` or mount the host `.env`. `processTaskIpc`'s `audible_backup` case becomes a generic `run_sidecar` case (same `isMain` gate, same fire-and-forget relay, #625 partial-success behavior preserved inside the runner); the container-side `audible_backup` MCP tool becomes a generic `run_sidecar(name, flags)` tool. **`github_backup` stays core** — it is a git add/commit/push of a host backup dir + DB dump (no docker image, no bind-mounts), so it does not fit the image+mounts sidecar registry and is deployment infra, not per-plugin domain knowledge. Pairs with the companion `nanoclaw-media` audible-backup skill migration (switches the skill to call `run_sidecar`). Audible backup output payload is unchanged (books[], partial-success, log tail).

## [1.2.90] - 2026-07-19

- Extracted the session-JSONL wipe helpers out of the `src/index.ts` god-file into a dedicated `src/session-wipe.ts` (#749 seam 1). Behavior-preserving move of `wipeSessionJsonl` and its `unlinkJsonlInSlug` / `removeToolResultsDirInSlug` helpers, doc comments intact; `index.ts` re-exports `wipeSessionJsonl` so `nuke-session.test.ts` and the `deps.wipeSessionJsonl` scheduler wiring are unchanged. `BEST_EFFORT_FS_CODES` moved next to `isFsErrorWithCode` in `src/fs-errors.ts` to avoid a circular import. `index.ts` drops ~620 lines (3599 → 2980) with no functional change; the remaining seams (gate helpers, message pipeline, thin `main`) stay tracked in #749.

## [1.2.89] - 2026-07-18

- Fixed cadence prechecks masking their failure cause and not honoring the 30s timeout (#812). `runScript` (agent-runner) collapsed every `execFile` failure — timeout-kill, signal, genuine non-zero exit, spawn failure — to the bare string `precheck script failed: execfile-error`, so `task_run_logs.error` and the operator's alert couldn't tell a hung precheck killed at the timeout from a script that exited non-zero (the disambiguating `code`/`signal`/`killed` fields were dropped). **Bug A** now threads those controlled fields into the persisted error via a pure `formatExecErrorDetail` helper — e.g. `execfile-error (timed out after 30s, signal=SIGKILL)` vs `execfile-error (exit=1)`. Raw child stderr is never logged or persisted (it may carry secrets — `coding-policy: no-secrets`); the child is spawned with stderr discarded. **Bug B**: the 30s `execFile` timeout sent a single SIGTERM with no escalation, so a precheck blocked in a network syscall (Google gateway, `gh`) ignored it and ran to its own socket timeout (110s observed) while holding the maintenance slot; `runScript` now spawns the child in its own process group (`detached`) and escalates SIGTERM → 5s grace → SIGKILL against the whole group, so a python grandchild forked by bash is bounded too, not just bash. `runScript` is extracted from `index.ts` into a testable `run-script.ts` module (chips at #749); new coverage asserts the timeout-vs-exit detail disambiguation and that a SIGTERM-ignoring child (and its grandchild) is actually SIGKILLed near the timeout. Distinct from the closed #747 (scripts missing from the published tree; this is the script present, running, then failing). The heartbeat/Haiku `</text>` formatting leak in the resulting alert is tracked separately (admin-tile).

## [1.2.88] - 2026-07-18

- Fixed inbound voice messages being silently dropped on the prod (OneCLI) path (#810). #770's credential migration set a custom `undici@8` `fetch` on the OpenAI client to tunnel transcription through the gateway; that fixed the JSON/dispatcher path but broke the multipart (audio upload) path with the inverse dual-undici mismatch. The OpenAI SDK builds its multipart body with the GLOBAL `FormData` (Node's bundled undici), and `undici@8`'s `fetch` only serializes a `FormData` instance of its OWN class as multipart — a foreign one stringifies to `[object FormData]`, so the SDK's `supportsFormData` guard threw `The provided fetch function does not support file uploads with the current global FormData class` on every voice note. Worse, the `message:voice` handler re-threw that non-operational `TypeError` *past* `deliverInbound`, so the note was never written to `messages.db` — no agent wake, no reaction, no user-visible trace, strictly worse than the intended `[Voice message - transcription failed]` fallback. Two fixes: (1) the OneCLI gateway path now issues the multipart POST to `api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions` directly via undici's own `fetch` + `FormData` (keeping the entire request on `undici@8`, tunneled through the proxy dispatcher with a placeholder Bearer the gateway swaps), bypassing the SDK whose global-FormData builder can't be serialized by the gateway fetch; the direct-`.env`-key path (local dev) keeps the SDK unchanged. An undici fetch transport failure on the gateway path (proxy/TLS/DNS/timeout/reset — which undici rejects as a non-`OpenAI.APIError` `TypeError('fetch failed')`) is classified operational and degrades to null, so a routine gateway outage doesn't surface as a grammy error. (2) the `message:voice` handler delivers a fallback row BEFORE re-raising a genuine defect, so a voice note is never lost even when transcription throws — operational/network failures still degrade quietly like the other media handlers. Only the OneCLI-configured prod path was affected (`isOneCliConfigured()` true). The dual-undici mismatch is invisible to the mocked-SDK unit tests that let #770 ship it, so new coverage asserts the gateway path builds an undici `FormData` and POSTs with the placeholder Bearer + proxy dispatcher, that a non-2xx/failed gateway response degrades without dropping the note, and that the handler always delivers a row before any re-raise.

## [1.2.87] - 2026-07-18

- Removed the host-side `sync_tripit` op, Phase 3 (final) of #748, now that the in-container TripIt → Reclaim sync is live-verified (gateway path-injection for TripIt confirmed 200 + real feed; Reclaim header and Google/OOO connection confirmed 200; a real `nightly-travel-sync` agent run persisted 4 trip segments via `persist_tz_segments` end to end). Deletes the `sync_tripit` IPC case (`src/ipc.ts`) and its MCP tool (`container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts`), and drops `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync` from `Dockerfile.orchestrator` — the orchestrator no longer runs the sync or holds TripIt/Reclaim/Google credentials. `sync-to-public.sh` swaps `sync_tripit` for `persist_tz_segments` in its private-handler scrub lists. The five host credentials (`TRIPIT_ICAL_URL`, `RECLAIM_API_TOKEN`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_REFRESH_TOKEN`) are stripped from the NAS `.env` (they now live only in the OneCLI vault / gateway connection; re-probed 200 after the strip, backup kept). `fetch_trakt_history` stays — Trakt remains blocked on `onecli/onecli#365`. The `tz_state` machinery (`applyTripitSegmentsToTzState`, `walkTzSegments`, the state-01x migrations) keeps its historical `sync_tripit` references intact: they document what the #542 host-op was named, and the mechanism they describe is unchanged — only its feeder moved from the host op to `persist_tz_segments`.

## [1.2.86] - 2026-07-18

- Enabled the in-container TripIt → Reclaim timezone sync, Phase 1 of #748 (additive — the host-side `sync_tripit` op stays until the Phase 3 cutover). The sync's only reason to run host-side was its host-only credentials; once the OneCLI gateway swaps them on the wire, the `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync` CLI can run in the agent container instead. What does NOT move is the owner-`tz_state` ingestion the op also performs on every success (`applyTripitSegmentsToTzState` + the #584 `onTzFlipped` local-schedule recompute), read by `scheduler-timezone`, the 30-min heartbeat advisory, and the #574 location cascade. This PR keeps that write host-side behind a new credential-free path: `container/Dockerfile` installs the CLI in the agent image (pinned `#v0.3.3`, Renovate-tracked via a github-tags custom manager); `applyOneCliToSpawn` puts `ONECLI_URL` + `ENABLE_OOO=1` on the agent spawn, gated specifically on `HTTPS_PROXY` landing (the package fail-fasts when `ONECLI_URL` is set without it) and never forwarding `ONECLI_API_KEY`; and a new `persist_tz_segments` IPC handler + isMain-gated MCP tool takes the container-supplied `segments[]` and persists exactly as the op's success path does. The handler re-checks the directory-verified `isMain` host-side (the MCP tool is isMain-gated, but the IPC task-file path is reachable by any container, so this is what blocks a non-main agent from poisoning owner tz), refuses a non-array payload rather than clearing `tz_state` (only an explicit `[]` clears — matching the op's skip-on-malformed behaviour), and caps the array at `MAX_TZ_SEGMENTS=200`. The deny/reject/persist decision and the spawn-env gating are extracted into pure `classifyTzPersist` / `oneCliSpawnEnvArgs` helpers with unit coverage. Pairs with `nanoclaw-travel#194` (rewrites `nightly-travel-sync` Step 1 to run the CLI in-container and call `persist_tz_segments`); the TripIt vault path-injection + `.env` strip land in Phase 2/3.

## [1.2.85] - 2026-07-17

- Fixed isolated cadence tasks resuming a pinned SDK session on every wake (#801). The scheduler resumed `scheduled_tasks.session_id` for every recurring (non-`once`) task regardless of `context_mode`, which was inert — so a task registered `isolated` (every cadence-registry row, and the maintenance heartbeat) resumed its own prior-wake output each fire (100% cache hit). For a precheck whose per-wake step is stateless by design, that injects cross-wake narrative the step never intended; a weak `agentModel` then treats its earlier output as fact and ratchets it. Observed in `nanoclaw-travel#187`: the `drive-engine` cadence wake posted an escalating series of *false* "the engine is broken" alarms over one day (telemetry showed `created:0, deleted:0` every run), each wake compounding the last resumed session's hallucination. Session resume is now opt-in via `context_mode: 'group'` (`resumesSession = isReusable && context_mode === 'group'` in `src/task-scheduler.ts`): `isolated` rows never resume, never persist, and never retain a session — they wake fresh every fire, matching the heartbeat's own long-documented intent (`src/index.ts`). `group` recurring tasks keep the #336 cross-fire reuse. This also removes the #114 disk-accumulation and #720 fabrication-precedent exposure at the root for isolated rows.

## [1.2.84] - 2026-07-17

- Added a cadence-registry near-miss guard (#803). A precheck whose `CADENCE` cap is an exact integer multiple of its cron period wedges, because the cursor stamps at run *completion*, not fire time: on a weekly cron a 168h cap leaves every same-time fire ~167.8h old and skips forever; on a daily cron an N-day-multiple cap slips the run by one whole period. This is the fleet-wide shape behind `nanoclaw-admin#353`/#354 and the eight tile-side cap fixes filed under #803. `rebuildCadenceRegistry` now reads each declared precheck's `CADENCE = timedelta(...)` (via `parseCadenceCapMs` — a bounded regex over the enumerable `weeks|days|hours|minutes|seconds` kwargs, returning null on any shape it can't attribute), computes the cron's nominal interval in UTC (`cronIntervalMs`, min-gap sampled so an unevenly-spaced cron is measured against its tightest period), and warns loudly when `detectCadenceCapRace` finds the cap divides the interval evenly. The warn is deliberately warn-**not**-skip: a runtime skip would stop the task firing entirely, strictly worse than the near-miss it surfaces — so the row still registers. The author-time gate stays each tile's per-skill `near_miss` regression test; this is the fleet-wide net for a skill shipped without one. The three detector functions are pure and exported, covered by unit tests — including mixed positional/keyword and expression `timedelta` inputs that must parse to null — plus two rebuild-level tests asserting a race-cap skill still lands its row. See `nanoclaw-host: rules/overlay-tile-authoring.md` for the authoring-side invariant.

## [1.2.83] - 2026-07-17

- Fixed `.npmrc`'s supply-chain delay, which has never been in effect. The file read `minReleaseAge=7d` — wrong on both halves. The key is `min-release-age` (kebab-case), so npm reported `Unknown project config "minReleaseAge"` on literally every npm command in the repo while `npm config get min-release-age` returned `null`; and the value is a bare number of DAYS (`hint: '<days>'`, `type: [null, Number]` in `@npmcli/config`'s `lib/definitions/definitions.js` — note `npm config ls -l` prints values and their origin, not the schema, so it will not show you the units), so even the renamed key would have been rejected — verified: `min-release-age=7d` produces `npm warn invalid config ... Must be one of: null, numeric value`, while `min-release-age=7` is accepted silently. Now `min-release-age=7`, confirmed live (`npm config get min-release-age` → `7`) and confirmed non-breaking (`npm ci` still resolves at both the root and `container/agent-runner` under the 7-day floor). The setting reads as a deliberate posture — only install versions public for 7+ days, so a compromised release has a window to be caught before it reaches a build — and it was silently doing nothing, which is worse than absent: everyone reading the file believed the protection existed. The file header now records that it does NOT reach `container/Dockerfile`'s `RUN npm install -g` either, since the docker build context is `container/` and this file is never copied in; those packages are pinned literally and watched by Renovate instead (#796). Surfaced while reviewing #796.

## [1.2.82] - 2026-07-17

- Pinned the globally-installed npm packages in the agent image and put a scanner on them (#796). `container/Dockerfile` ran `npm install -g agent-browser @anthropic-ai/claude-code` unpinned, so two rebuilds of the same commit could produce different images with no diff explaining it — including a `deploy.sh --no-cache` run whose stated purpose was something else. Now `agent-browser@0.32.1 @anthropic-ai/claude-code@2.1.212`, both of which are the versions already deployed AND the current `latest`, so the pin is a behavioural no-op today; it only removes the silent drift. #639 did not cause this — it took the line from 3 unpinned packages to 2 — and declined to pin there rather than bundle a dependency-policy change into a Composio-removal PR. Renewal is a committed scanner rather than memory (`dependency-management` Freshness): neither existing manager could see this line — Dependabot's `docker` ecosystem reads only `FROM` pins, Renovate's `npm` manager reads only `package.json` — so `.github/renovate.json` gains a `custom.regex` manager watching the two literals, in its own PR group (`agent image npm globals`) rather than batched into "npm minor and patch", because a claude-code bump changes the agent's own runtime inside every container and deserves a standalone review. Dependabot still owns the base-image `@sha256` pins in the same Dockerfile; the two tools match disjoint text. Both verification steps caught real bugs: `renovate-config-validator` rejected a first draft using `managerFilePatterns` (invalid on this Renovate — it would have committed a silently-dead manager watching nothing), and a `--dry-run=extract` against the real repo showed the first regex pair produced OVERLAPPING `replaceString`s — anchoring on `npm install -g` dragged the whole line into claude-code's replace target, so bumping agent-browser first would have left claude-code's match stale and silently dropped its update. Regexes tightened to the bare `pkg@version` token (each unique in the file); the dry-run now extracts the two as independent, non-overlapping targets. Image rebuilt and checked: both pins install exactly and `claude --version` reports 2.1.212.

## [1.2.81] - 2026-07-17

- Test files in `container/agent-runner` are now typechecked (#795). No tsc run has ever read them: the root `tsconfig.json` doesn't include `container/agent-runner` at all, and that package's own `tsconfig.json` carries `exclude: ["src/**/*.test.ts"]` because the same config drives the emit-to-dist build (compiling tests would ship them in the image). vitest doesn't typecheck. So `npm run typecheck`, `tsc -p container/agent-runner`, and 3651 passing tests were all green over genuinely type-invalid code — #639 shipped `Type '"gmail"' is not assignable to type 'AclPrefix'` past every one of them, and only a reviewer reading the diff caught it. New `container/agent-runner/tsconfig.test.json` (`extends` the build config, `noEmit`, includes tests), invoked by a new ROOT script `typecheck:agent-runner`; CI's agent-runner step runs that instead of a bare `tsc --noEmit`, which silently re-read the excluding config. The gate is owned by the root project rather than the package: the root `vitest.config.ts` is what includes `container/agent-runner/src/**/*.test.ts` and the root manifest is what declares `vitest`, so the root both runs and typechecks those files, and the package's manifest stays scoped to what the container ships plus what its build needs. No new dependency and no image change — declaring `vitest` in the package would ship ~259M of dev deps into every agent container, and `npm prune` cannot reclaim it (measured: pruning takes node_modules 568M→279M while the image stays 3.76GB either way, because the install layer keeps the bytes). Fixes the one real finding the widened gate surfaced: `system-prompt-assembly.test.ts`'s type-level pin, which asserts the frozen-prompt builder rejects volatile inputs (`currentMessage`, `gitStatus`, `cwd`, `memoryMd`, `timestamp`), had every `@ts-expect-error` parked above the *call* — but `@ts-expect-error` only suppresses the line immediately following it, and the excess-property error is reported at the *property*, so the directives suppressed nothing, pinned nothing, and reported as unused. The pin has never worked; its own comment conceded "CI does not enforce them in this repo". Directives moved onto the offending properties, and both regression shapes are verified to fail the gate: widening `FrozenSystemPromptInputs` trips the pin, and a retired `SourcePrefix` member in a test fixture reproduces the exact #639 error.

## [1.2.80] - 2026-07-16

- Retired Composio from the codebase (#639, under umbrella #564). #638 moved Gmail/Calendar/Tasks/Drive to native Google REST through the OneCLI gateway, leaving the Composio MCP server registered-but-forbidden — `nanoclaw-admin`'s `rules/google-access.md` could only ask the agent not to call it ("registered is not permitted"). This makes that structural: the `composio` entry is gone from `mcpServersConfig`, so `mcp__composio__*` is unreachable rather than merely discouraged, and `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` / `COMPOSIO_MCP_URL` / `COMPOSIO_USER_ID` are dropped from `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` + `CONTAINER_VARS` (`src/container-runner.ts`), `.env.example`, and `@composio/mcp` from the agent-runner image. **Renamed, not deleted:** `composio-fidelity` (#140) carries no Composio logic — it regex-sweeps tool results for fabricated-ID signatures and is registered on `matcher: 'mcp__.*'`, so it guards every MCP surface (tessl, nanoclaw, byair). Deleting it would have removed fabricated-ID detection everywhere; it becomes `mcp-fidelity` (`detectMcpFidelity`), which is what this issue's own Risks section called for. **Deleted:** `composio-arg-validator` (#326, rule table keyed entirely on `mcp__composio__*`) and the #319 body summariser — `GMAIL_SUMMARY`/`CALENDAR_SUMMARY` were its only `summariseBody` configs and `SUMMARISE_COMPOSIO_BODIES` was never set in production, so it was already inert; the shared lazy Anthropic client stays because #392's external-file summariser uses it, as does `structured-summary.ts`. **Narrowed:** the four Composio read rows in `untrusted-input-wrap.ts` were the only producers of the `gmail`/`calendar`/`slack`/`github` source prefixes, so those `SourcePrefix` members, their `SINK_ALLOWLISTS` rows, and the `gmail_send`/`slack_post` egress-allowlist paths (config, parser, `extractDestinations`, `splitRecipientList`) are removed rather than left as unreachable code that reads like live gating — `GatedSink` is now `send_message_to_chat` alone. Precisely what this does and does not cost, because "removes no live protection" would be doing too much work: (a) the deleted rows had **no producer** — nothing could emit those four prefixes once #638 moved Gmail/Calendar off MCP, so no marker is being dropped; (b) a **stale** `gmail:`/`calendar:` marker (in a live transcript, or a `/workspace/state/compact-provenance/` sidecar — `isWellFormedSource` validates shape, not membership) now classifies as `__unknown__`, which gates *more* strictly, not less, and is unreachable in practice anyway: `deploy.sh` kills every agent container (step 5) and clears every session (step 6), and a sidecar is only ever read back for its own `expectedSessionId`, so no in-flight session carries one across the deploy; (c) what is genuinely **not** covered is the native path itself — Google traffic arrives over Bash+REST, which these MCP-tool-name-keyed gates never matched. That is a gap #638 opened, not one this change creates, and it is tracked in #794 rather than papered over here. Separately, no `egress_allowlist.json` exists in production, so the egress gate returns `null` and is inert today regardless. **Left frozen:** `last_composio_check` in `src/db.ts` + `state-009` + `heartbeat-state-json-migration` — a one-time JSON→SQLite migration of already-written on-disk data, where deleting the read path would change what happens to an unmigrated host's records. Test fixtures that used Composio tool names as arbitrary stand-ins are repointed onto live tools (`mcp__tessl__*`) and the `tessl__composio-fetch` cadence-name fixtures onto `tessl__google-fetch`, matching the skill's #638 rename; `secret-env-file.test.ts`'s membership assertions become a guard that no `COMPOSIO_*` var can return to the set. Operational follow-up after deploy: strip the three `COMPOSIO_*` rows from the NAS `.env` and retire the vault's Composio REST + MCP secret entries.

## [1.2.79] - 2026-07-15

- Fix the #770 OpenAI transcription gateway path throwing `UND_ERR_INVALID_ARG: invalid onRequestStart method` at request time. The `ProxyAgent` dispatcher comes from the standalone `undici` package, but the OpenAI SDK calls Node's **bundled** global `fetch` — a different undici build whose internal dispatcher-handler interface rejects the foreign-version dispatcher, so every real transcription failed even though the gateway swap itself works (verified live: a placeholder-Bearer `GET /v1/models` through the same `getOneCliOutboundConfig('main')` + `ProxyAgent` returns 200 once fetch and dispatcher share a package). Fix: pass undici's own `fetch` as the OpenAI client's `fetch` option (`src/channels/telegram.ts`) so it and the `ProxyAgent` are the same undici build. The mocked unit tests couldn't catch this — they stub the `OpenAI` SDK, so the real fetch+dispatcher handshake never runs — which is why it only surfaced under a post-deploy end-to-end probe of the deployed code; the direct (OneCLI-unconfigured) path is unchanged and keeps Node's global fetch. Regression guard added: the configured-branch test now asserts a custom `fetch` is supplied and the direct-branch test asserts it is not.

## [1.2.78] - 2026-07-14

- Host-side OpenAI voice transcription now routes through the OneCLI MITM gateway, so the real `OPENAI_API_KEY` moves to the Agent Vault and is stripped from the NAS `.env` (#770, closes the last untracked `.env` credential under umbrella #564). `transcribeVoice` in `src/channels/telegram.ts` was the only host reader of the key — it built an `OpenAI` client straight from the `.env` value and hit `api.openai.com` directly, bypassing the gateway so the vaulted key could never inject and the plaintext key had to stay on disk. Now, when OneCLI is configured (`isOneCliConfigured()`), the client sends a placeholder Bearer (`ONECLI_MANAGED_PLACEHOLDER`) and tunnels through the gateway via an undici `ProxyAgent` built from `getOneCliOutboundConfig('main')` — the same orchestrator-outbound-through-gateway path #637 established for Anthropic. The gateway swaps in the vaulted OpenAI key on `api.openai.com` (typed `openai` secret, built-in `Authorization: Bearer` injection; live-verified: a placeholder-Bearer `GET /v1/models` through `onecli run` returns 200 + the real model list). The OneCLI CA is attached to the destination TLS via `ProxyAgent`'s `requestTls.ca`, NOT the proxy-TLS layer — the MITM cert is presented for `api.openai.com` after CONNECT, mirroring the `http.request` `ca`-on-options split the credential-proxy makes. A gateway that's configured-but-unreachable reports transcription unavailable rather than falling back (there's no real key on disk to fall back to); OneCLI **un**configured (local dev) still reads the real `.env` key on the direct path. `ONECLI_MANAGED_PLACEHOLDER` relocated from `src/container-runner.ts` to its canonical OneCLI home in `src/onecli-client.ts` (re-exported from container-runner so existing consumers keep resolving it), avoiding a `telegram.ts` → `container-runner.ts` import that would drag the spawn module's graph into the channel's tests. Adds `undici ^8.7.0` as a direct dependency (its `ProxyAgent` is the only proxy primitive undici fetch honors via `fetchOptions.dispatcher`; `https-proxy-agent` is a node:http agent that global fetch ignores). New `_oneCliProxyAuth` unit tests (`src/channels/telegram.test.ts`) cover the userinfo→`Basic`-token derivation undici's `ProxyAgent` does not do from a URI's userinfo (lift-and-strip, no-userinfo, percent-decoded userinfo). NAS `.env` `OPENAI_API_KEY` real-value strip is the post-deploy operational step. The CI Codex-reviewer's `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`CODEX_API_KEY` (GitHub Actions secrets) are a separate surface, unaffected. The new npm dependency's renewal mechanism (`dependency-management` Freshness) is a committed Renovate config (`.github/renovate.json`, `enabledManagers: npm`) covering both npm manifests — scoped so it never overlaps the docker ecosystems Dependabot already handles; requires the Renovate GitHub App enabled on the repo to run.

## [1.2.77] - 2026-07-14

- Made the OneCLI credential-proxy resilient to transient gateway blips during container spawns (#787). Every proxied spawn does a gateway `applyContainerConfig` round-trip, and a single blip inside the (previously 1500ms) timeout window failed the spawn closed — the "queue retries with backoff" is the *next* scheduled cadence fire, so the current maintenance run was wasted, not recovered in place (observed 5× on 2026-07-14: isolated `sync-tripit` hits, then a `composio-fetch`/`sync-tripit` cluster when the NAS gateway got rough). The two error variants — "MITM CA could not be delivered" (via `getOneCliOutboundConfig` → `mountOneCliAgentCa`) and "managed credentials were withheld" (via `applyOneCliToSpawn` returning false) — were the same root: one un-retried round-trip. New `applyContainerConfigWithRetry` in `src/onecli-client.ts` wraps **both** call sites with a bounded in-spawn retry (`APPLY_CONFIG_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 3`, `APPLY_CONFIG_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS = [300, 900]`). A transient failure surfaces two ways and both retry: a thrown transient error (`isRetryableApplyConfigError` — a status-less `OneCLIError` from a pre-gateway timeout/connreset, or a 429/5xx `OneCLIRequestError`) AND a falsy resolve (the SDK swallows gateway/config fetch failures into `false` — the documented main recoverable path). A deterministic 4xx (bad key, unknown agent) and any unknown error type are NOT retried — they rethrow to the caller's existing catch, so a misconfig fails fast instead of triggering a retry storm. `args` is truncated back to its pre-call length between attempts — and on ANY thrown failure before rethrowing — so a failed attempt's partial `-e`/`-v` append never double-lands, and a mid-append throw never leaks a half-applied proxy env/mount to the caller that catches it (a clean falsy resolve is left as-is on purpose, preserving the #640 NO_PROXY decoupling). The per-call `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` is raised 1500 → 3000 so a slow-but-alive gateway isn't turned into a hard "not active"; `ensureAgentForTier` (startup) stays un-retried. Container-runner's fail-closed logic is unchanged — it only ever sees the post-retry result. Coverage: `src/onecli-client.test.ts` moves the `applyOneCliToSpawn` block onto fake timers. Both call sites route through the one shared helper, so its full branch matrix is exercised via `applyOneCliToSpawn` — recover-on-transient-throw, recover-on-transient-false, no-retry-on-4xx, no-retry-on-unknown-error, retry-exhaustion, argv-no-double-append, and argv-truncated-on-throw (retryable and non-retryable); `getOneCliOutboundConfig` adds recover-on-transient-throw and retry-exhaustion (against both a thrown 5xx and a falsy resolve). Full suite green. The companion operator-alert gating (page only on non-recovery, not per self-healing blip) is tracked separately in `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin`.

## [1.2.76] - 2026-07-13

- The maintenance-container skill-dependency closure now sees cross-skill SUBPROCESS dependencies, not just `Skill()` invocations (#441). A skill can depend on another skill's *files* by shelling out to a script at its mount path — `morning-brief`'s `resolve-reminder-schedule.py` runs `.../skills/tessl__scheduler-timezone/scripts/compute-schedule-value.py` as a subprocess. Pre-#441 `computeEffectiveSkillContext` scanned only each skill's SKILL.md for `Skill(skill: "...")` calls, so it never saw that edge; `scheduler-timezone` (on `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST`) stayed filtered out of the maintenance container and the subprocess hit a missing mount path — the 2026-07-12 morning-brief Step-9 failure that dropped that day's flight reminders (`jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#439`). New `extractMountPathDeps(content)` in `src/skill-dep-closure.ts` captures the depended-on skill from any `tessl__<name>/` reference; the trailing `/` anchors it to an actual path INTO the skill's dir, so a bare `tessl__<name>` mention in a doc-comment (the #337 audit's `tessl__check-email` / `tessl__trusted-memory` prose) does not falsely rescue it. The closure's BFS now unions `Skill()` deps with mount-path deps, and `computeEffectiveSkillContextForSpawn` folds each skill's `scripts/` + `references/` text into the source blob (via new `readAuxSkillText`, best-effort ENOENT-skip / other-errno-propagate per `error-handling`, bounded to those two subdirs one level deep) so a mount-path reference in a script is visible to the extractor. Over-inclusion stays the safe failure mode (the module's existing invariant); `reachableSkills` — consumed by #552 rule `requires:` filtering — broadens accordingly, which only ever loads an extra prompt/rule, never suppresses one. Adds 7 cases to `src/skill-dep-closure.test.ts` (mount-path extraction: path-anchored match, multi/dedup, slashless-non-match, underscores/hyphens; closure rescue via mount path; slashless-no-rescue) and 1 end-to-end case in `src/container-runner.maintenance-blocklist.test.ts` (a blocklisted `scheduler-timezone` rescued because a loaded `morning-brief`'s script references its mount path, asserting the full skill mount incl. `scripts/` lands). Closes `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#441`.

## [1.2.75] - 2026-07-12

- Added a host-side pre-spawn eligibility gate for scheduled tasks (#754). Cadence skills that only do useful work inside a bounded window (`flight-assist`, fired every 2 minutes → ~30 container spawns/hour) previously spawned a container on every fire even off-window, paying a full spawn just for an in-container precheck that returns "nothing to do". The gate (`src/spawn-gates.ts`) evaluates a host-side predicate BEFORE any spawn: out of window, `runTask` records a distinct `task_run_logs.status = 'skipped_out_of_window'` and returns without spawning; in window, the existing spawn path is unchanged. The gate is keyed on the task's skill (`parseTaskSkill`), so it is source-agnostic (cadence-registry or imperative) and only the registered skill (`tessl__flight-assist`) is gated — every other task spawns unconditionally. The trip-window predicate reads the group's `travel-db.json` (`in_window ≔ ∃ trip: (start − 24h) ≤ now ≤ end`, v1 UTC date bounds, union of trips) with the same realpath symlink-containment as `checkTaskEvidence` (the group folder is container-writable). Fail behaviour is asymmetric because flight-assist is safety-relevant: an absent travel-db is out-of-window (no itinerary to act on), but a present-but-unreadable/corrupt travel-db fails OPEN with a WARN so an active trip is never blinded. Unblocks the companion plugin-side work in `jbaruch/nanoclaw-travel`. New status is non-error/non-alerting, following the `precheck_skipped` (#581) precedent, and makes the spawns-saved directly measurable in `task_run_logs`. The precheck-gating audit (`src/audit-precheck-gating.ts`) now counts `skipped_out_of_window` as a ground-truth gate-out alongside `precheck_skipped`, so a windowed cadence's saved spawns show as gated rather than inflating the ungated-fire count.

## [1.2.74] - 2026-07-12

- Stripped the retired `container_config.stage2Enabled` flag from every `registered_groups` row (#753). The field backed the Stage-2 Haiku classifier removed during the subscription-OAuth cutover; no production code reads it and the `ContainerConfig` type no longer declares it, but it lingered in stored blobs (main was `false`; several trusted groups `true`), confusing operators and agents reading DB dumps. New state-migration `state-016` scrubs it in place via `json_remove` (only rows whose config is valid JSON and actually carry the key are rewritten — NULL, corrupt, and already-clean rows stay byte-identical). The valid-JSON guard and the key-presence test are ordered by a `CASE` rather than chained `AND` terms (SQLite may reorder `WHERE` conjuncts, so a bare chain could evaluate the lookup first and abort startup on a corrupt blob), and presence is tested with `json_type` rather than `json_extract(...) IS NOT NULL` so a `"stage2Enabled": null` value is stripped too. Because `parseContainerConfig` round-trips unknown keys verbatim, a `state-016` alone could be re-dirtied by a read-modify-write of a stale IPC blob or a hand-edited row, so `setRegisteredGroup` — the single write chokepoint — now strips retired keys before persisting. No `schema_version` bump (dropping an ignored key is backward-compatible) and no runtime behavior change (the field was already unread).

## [1.2.73] - 2026-07-12

- Added the `sqlite3` command-line binary to the agent-runner container image (#765). The image already shipped Python's `sqlite3` module, so DB access was never blocked — but every ad-hoc query against `/workspace/store/messages.db` fell back to a ~15-line `import sqlite3` heredoc instead of a one-line `sqlite3 db "SELECT …"`. Installing the CLI (one apt package alongside `python3` in `container/Dockerfile`) makes ad-hoc DB dips a Bash one-liner, a permanent per-query token saving that eases rate-limit pressure on subscription billing. No capability change; purely DX / token economy.

## [1.2.72] - 2026-07-10

- Retired the three Sessionize host IPC handlers — `sessionize_get_event`, `sessionize_get_events`, `sessionize_open_cfps` — and the agent-facing MCP tools that shimmed to them (part of #564 cleanup). These were **ad-hoc/overlay concerns that leaked into core `ipc.ts`**: the deterministic conferences path (`check-cfps`) already runs the in-container `discover-open-cfps.py` / `verify-sessionize.py` drivers, and since #768 containers reach `sessionize.com` directly with the placeholder `X-API-KEY` that the gateway swaps (path-scoped per key), the host-IPC round-trip is redundant — an ad-hoc query is now just an in-container API call. Removed from `src/ipc.ts`: the three handler cases plus the now-dead helpers they alone used (`normalizeSessionizeEvent`, `fetchSessionizeEventsBatch`, the `SessionizeNormalizedEvent` type, and the `SESSIONIZE_BATCH_*` constants). Removed the three tool registrations from `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts`. `SESSIONIZE_SPEAKER_KEY` / `SESSIONIZE_EVENT_API_KEY` stay in `CONTAINER_VARS` / `ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` (containers still get the placeholder + swap); dropping the host readers unblocks stripping their real values from the NAS `.env` (follow-up NAS step). The container-runner `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` docstring is updated to drop the stale host-handler reference. Coverage: the `normalizeSessionizeEvent` and `fetchSessionizeEventsBatch` unit suites are removed with the code they covered; full suite green. The `check-cfps` SKILL.md "tool stays available for ad-hoc queries" notes are dropped in the conferences tile in lock-step.

## [1.2.71] - 2026-07-10

- Migrated the injection-gap container-outbound credentials to the OneCLI vault swap: added `SESSIONIZE_SPEAKER_KEY`, `SESSIONIZE_EVENT_API_KEY`, and `BYAIR_MCP_URL` to `ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` (#640, part of #564). 1.2.66 recorded these as permanently stuck in `.env` because "OneCLI injects headers/params only" — that was **stale, not true**: OneCLI shipped URL-path injection in v1.40.0 (`onecli/onecli#356`), and the NAS gateway was simply an old `:latest` pull (v1.38.0). Upgrading the gateway to v1.41.0 unblocked all three. `Sessionize` uses an `X-API-KEY` **header** (not a path key — the earlier note was wrong); its two keys share `sessionize.com`, disambiguated by the gateway's `pathPattern` matching (`/api/universal/open-cfps` for the speaker key, `/api/universal/event` for the event key). `BYAIR_MCP_URL` is query-param injection (`api_key` on `api.byairapp.com`). All three swap-verified host-side through the gateway (placeholder → real data): ByAir MCP `initialize` and both Sessionize endpoints (TripIt's iCal path injection is proven too, but TripIt is the host-side `sync_tripit` handler tracked separately in #748 — not migrated here). To carry the one URL-valued var (`BYAIR_MCP_URL`), `ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` changes from a `Set<string>` to a `Map<string, string>` mapping each var to the placeholder it forwards: scalar creds keep the bare `onecli-managed` sentinel; `BYAIR_MCP_URL` forwards a URL-shaped placeholder (`BYAIR_MANAGED_PLACEHOLDER` = `https://api.byairapp.com/mcp?api_key=onecli-managed`) so the consuming skill's URL parse still passes while the gateway swaps the embedded sentinel. Dev (OneCLI unconfigured) still falls back to real-value `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` forwarding, and the fail-closed contract is unchanged. Coverage: the `#640` placeholder suite now iterates the map and asserts each var forwards its own mapped placeholder (never the real value); the `#719` Sessionize env-file suite pins `isOneCliConfigured()` false to assert the dev-fallback path explicitly. The real `.env` values are stripped in a follow-up NAS step after live-verify (the vault entries already exist and swap correctly).

## [1.2.70] - 2026-07-10

- Removed the `CREDPROXY_AUTH_DEBUG` auth-path trace and its tests now that the #640 cutover is landed and verified (#640, part of #564). The gated per-request trace (added in 1.2.67) did its job — it pinned the cutover 401 to the gateway-injected `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` sentinel — and is investigation scaffolding with no further purpose, so `src/credential-proxy.ts` no longer carries the `process.env.CREDPROXY_AUTH_DEBUG` branch and `src/credential-proxy.test.ts` drops the three trace tests. The `DUMP_API_REQUESTS` request-body dump left enabled in the orchestrator override during the investigation was turned off, and the now-vaulted `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` / `TOMTOM_API_KEY` / `YOUTUBE_API_KEY` real values were stripped from the NAS `.env` (agents receive the `onecli-managed` placeholder and the gateway swaps the vaulted value; `GITHUB_TOKEN` keeps its real `.env` value for the host-side `github_backup`).

## [1.2.69] - 2026-07-10

- Landed the OneCLI agent-proxy cutover permanently and removed the transitional `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY` flag (#640, part of #564). With the Anthropic-under-proxy 401 fixed (1.2.68) and live-verified, the decoupling gate #757 introduced is no longer needed: the agent-spawn proxy now applies whenever OneCLI is configured (`isOneCliConfigured()`), the same single condition that gates managed-var placeholdering — so in any deployment with `ONECLI_URL`/`ONECLI_API_KEY` set, every agent's outbound HTTPS traverses the OneCLI gateway and `ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` swap in from the vault. Removed: the `oneCliAgentProxyEnabled()` helper (`src/onecli-client.ts`), both two-conjunct gates in `src/container-runner.ts` (the managed-vars forwarding gate and the spawn-time proxy-application gate, each now just `isOneCliConfigured()`), the `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY` env var from `.env.example`, and the flag references in `docs/OPERATIONS.md` + `docs/SECURITY.md`. Dev (OneCLI unconfigured) still falls back to real-value `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` forwarding, and the fail-closed behavior when the gateway is unreachable at spawn time is unchanged (managed placeholders withheld → spawn throws → queue retries with backoff). The `CREDPROXY_AUTH_DEBUG` trace (1.2.67) is kept as a permanent, default-off diagnostic. Coverage: the obsolete "configured-but-flag-off" tests are removed; the remaining spawn-path tests assert the proxy + managed-var placeholdering apply when OneCLI is configured and fall back to real values when it is unconfigured.

## [1.2.68] - 2026-07-10

- Fixed the root cause of the `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY=1` cutover breaking every real agent turn with a fast Anthropic 401 (`Invalid API key`) (#640, part of #564). The OneCLI gateway's `container-config` for each agent carries `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<sentinel>` (a MITM placeholder the gateway expects to swap on outbound `api.anthropic.com` requests), and the SDK's `applyContainerConfig` injects **every** `config.env` key onto the spawn — so that sentinel landed on the agent. But NanoClaw's agents reach Anthropic through the credential-proxy (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` → `host.docker.internal`, `NO_PROXY`-excluded), never through the gateway, so the swap never fired. Worse, the Claude SDK treats a set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as api-key mode: it dropped the OAuth flow and sent `x-api-key: <sentinel>` with no `Authorization` header, so the cred-proxy's OneCLI-injection gate (which requires an `Authorization` header) was skipped and the sentinel reached Anthropic raw → 401 on every real turn. The fix (`applyOneCliToSpawn` in `src/onecli-client.ts`) strips the gateway-injected `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from the spawn argv — scanning only the tail `applyContainerConfig` just appended, so an api-key-mode placeholder container-runner sets earlier is preserved — so the agent keeps `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` and its Anthropic traffic rides the cred-proxy → OneCLI vaulted Bearer, exactly as with the flag off. The Anthropic credential's location is unchanged: it lives only in the OneCLI vault, injected by the credential-proxy (the #637 path). `OPENAI_API_KEY` is deliberately left in place — OpenAI traffic DOES traverse the gateway, so its placeholder swap works as designed. Root cause was pinned live via the `CREDPROXY_AUTH_DEBUG` trace (added in 1.2.67): working turns showed `Authorization`/`oneCliUsed:true`/200, the broken proxied spawn showed `x-api-key`/`oneCliUsed:false`/401 with no OAuth exchange. Coverage: spawn-path tests assert the gateway `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is stripped (with no dangling `-e`), that `OPENAI_API_KEY` survives, and that a caller-set api-key-mode placeholder is untouched.

## [1.2.67] - 2026-07-10

- Added a gated per-request auth-path trace to the credential proxy to diagnose why real agent-runner turns 401 (`Invalid API key`) under `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY=1` while a bare `claude -p` under identical proxy + CA passes (#640, part of #564). When `CREDPROXY_AUTH_DEBUG=1` is set on the orchestrator, `src/credential-proxy.ts` logs one INFO line per upstream response — `{endpoint, method, authMode, hasAuthHeader, hasXApiKey, oneCliUsed, tier, upstreamStatus}` — placed in the upstream-response callback so the OneCLI-injection decision and the upstream status are captured together for the same request. `endpoint` is an allowlisted label (`v1/messages` / `oauth/create_api_key` / `other`), never raw path material, so a path-embedded credential can never reach the log; `oneCliUsed` reflects what was actually applied (OneCLI only tunnels when the upstream is HTTPS). Booleans, status codes, and the fixed endpoint label only; no token values ever logged (no-secrets). Default OFF, single-shot capture pattern (set, fire one request, unset). Inert when unset — a pure diagnostic with no behavioral change to the request path.

## [1.2.66] - 2026-07-10

- Migrated the remaining header/param-injectable container-outbound credentials to the OneCLI vault swap: added `TOMTOM_API_KEY`, `YOUTUBE_API_KEY`, and `GITHUB_TOKEN` to `ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` alongside `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` (#640, part of #564). With `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY=1` live, main/trusted agents now receive `onecli-managed` placeholders for all four, and OneCLI's MITM gateway injects the real vaulted value on the outbound request — so the real keys never enter the agent environ. All four swap-verified through the gateway (placeholder → real data): Maps Distance Matrix, TomTom geocode (`api.tomtom.com`, param `key`), YouTube Data API (`www.googleapis.com` path-scoped to `/youtube/*`, param `key`), and GitHub (`api.github.com`, header `Authorization: Bearer`). The `ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` docstring precondition relaxed: a host-side reader no longer disqualifies a var, since only the CONTAINER is placeholdered — `GITHUB_TOKEN` keeps its real `.env` value for the host-side `github_backup` `git push` while agents get the placeholder + swap. The injection-gap keys stay in `.env` (`SESSIONIZE_*` path-embedded, `BYAIR_MCP_URL` query-embedded) — OneCLI injects headers/params only. Coverage: a spawn-path test asserts every managed var is placeholdered (never the real value) when the proxy is applied.

## [1.2.65] - 2026-07-10

- Fixed OneCLI agent-proxy CA delivery under Docker-out-of-Docker, the blocker that made a live `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY=1` cutover break every agent's external HTTPS (#640). The OneCLI SDK's `applyContainerConfig` writes the MITM CA to a temp file **inside the orchestrator container** then bind-mounts it into each agent spawn — but the orchestrator runs under DooD (mounted `/var/run/docker.sock`), so the host daemon resolves that `-v` source against the **host** filesystem, where the path doesn't exist, and docker silently creates an **empty directory**. In-container the CA files were empty (0 lines), `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`/`REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE` unset, and every gateway-MITM TLS handshake failed (`curl (77) error setting certificate file`), so Maps/GitHub/Composio/web all died under the proxy. It stayed invisible pre-cutover because agents reach Anthropic over the plain-HTTP credential-proxy (NO_PROXY-excluded, no gateway CA) and the orchestrator's own outbound reads the CA in-process. The new `mountOneCliAgentCa` (`src/container-runner.ts`) writes the CA **content** (from `getOneCliOutboundConfig(tier).ca`) to a host-visible `data/onecli-ca/<tier>.pem` (under `DATA_DIR`, not the agent-RW `store/`) via `toHostPath()` — the same DooD translation every other agent mount already uses — bind-mounts it read-only at `/onecli/ca.pem`, and overrides every CA env var the SDK leaves broken or empty (`SSL_CERT_FILE`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`, `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`, `GIT_SSL_CAINFO`). Written `0644` because agents run as a non-root `--user` and onecli's own `ca-bundle.pem` is `0770`/uid-999 (unreadable by the agent). Atomic write (tmp + rename) so concurrent same-tier spawns never read a torn file. Fail-closed: when the proxy is applied but the CA can't be delivered (gateway unreachable), the spawn cleans up and throws rather than launching a container that would fail TLS on every external call — the queue retries with backoff. Validated end-to-end in a throwaway agent container before shipping: mounted-CA + `CURL_CA_BUNDLE` → `http=200` real Distance Matrix data through the gateway. Inert until `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY=1`. Coverage: spawn-path tests assert the CA mount + all five CA env vars land when the proxy is applied, and that a CA-delivery failure fails the spawn closed.

## [1.2.64] - 2026-07-09

- First credential migrated to the OneCLI vault swap: `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` (#640, part of #564). When the new `ONECLI_AGENT_PROXY=1` gate is on, main/trusted agents receive `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY=onecli-managed` (a non-empty sentinel so consumer skills that guard on "is the key set?" don't hard-fail) instead of the real value, and OneCLI's TLS-MITM gateway overwrites the `key` query param on outbound `maps.googleapis.com` requests with the vaulted secret — so the real Distance Matrix key never enters the agent environ. The forwarding gate (`ONECLI_MANAGED_VARS` in `src/container-runner.ts`) mirrors the exact condition that decides whether the gateway proxy is applied — `isOneCliConfigured() && oneCliAgentProxyEnabled()`.
