The fix points the agent runner's MCP registration at a Composio **custom MCP server** (`backend.composio.dev/v3/mcp/<id>/mcp`), which authenticates headlessly with the same `ak_*` key via `x-api-key` — real remote MCP, no OAuth, no local proxy/shim. The server is bound to the gmail/calendar/tasks auth configs plus the `composio` no-auth toolkit (for `COMPOSIO_REMOTE_WORKBENCH` + `COMPOSIO_SEARCH_TOOLS`) with `allowed_tools` scoped to the ~9 slugs the skills actually use, keeping the per-turn tool-list small (cost). `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` now registers `composio` as `{type:'http', url: COMPOSIO_MCP_URL + "?user_id="+COMPOSIO_USER_ID.trim(), headers:{'x-api-key': COMPOSIO_API_KEY}}`, gated on all three vars; the old `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY` migration-guard becomes a partial-config guard. The retired `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY` is removed from `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS`/`CONTAINER_VARS` in `src/container-runner.ts` and replaced by `COMPOSIO_MCP_URL` (account-identifying server id → same secret env-file treatment as `COMPOSIO_USER_ID`); `.env.example` and `src/secret-env-file.test.ts` updated in lockstep. The `.trim()` on `COMPOSIO_USER_ID` guards a stray trailing space found in the NAS `.env` (harmless to containers — docker-compose trims it — but it would 400 a direct `.env` reader). The Composio→OneCLI retirement epic (#638/#639) supersedes this remote-MCP path later; until then this is the headless-compatible fix. Refs #581.

- Fixed tile delivery breaking when the orchestrator's tessl CLI crosses 0.81. tessl 0.81 renamed the local registry install dir from `.tessl/tiles/` to `.tessl/plugins/`, and 0.81's `tessl update` migrates an existing workspace by writing `plugins/` and **deleting** `tiles/`. `getRegistryTilesDir()` hardcoded `.tessl/tiles/`, so the next cache-busting orchestrator image rebuild (the `npm install -g tessl` on `Dockerfile.orchestrator` is floating per `nanoclaw-host: tessl-version-floating`) would have left the resolver reading a deleted dir — every agent container would spawn with **no tiles** (not stale ones). `getRegistryTilesDir()` now prefers `.tessl/plugins/<owner>` when present and falls back to `.tessl/tiles/<owner>` for pre-0.81 installs and cold start (preserving the `getInstalledTiles()` → `null` "never installed" signal). This single chokepoint covers the spawn-time tile copy, the registry-availability guard, `getInstalledTiles()`, and `set_additional_tiles` validation. `scripts/reconcile-tiles.sh` learns the same preference for its container canonical-root probe, its local `tessl install` verify dir, and its legacy-path `find` predicate. The orchestrator's internal mirror into each agent container (`container-runner.ts` write ↔ agent-runner read, both `.claude/.tessl/tiles/`) is a matched pair not managed by the tessl CLI and stays as-is. tessl is intentionally left floating (no Dockerfile pin) per the version-floating rule. Caught 2026-05-29 while triaging why private tiles silently stopped updating — a 0.81 scratch-install probe surfaced the dir rename.

- byAir is now registered as a Claude MCP tool inside the agent container so the agent can answer ad-hoc flight questions ("has Amir landed?") on demand (#645). byAir was previously reachable only through the `jbaruch/nanoclaw-flight-assist` tile's Python scripts: the precheck polls `BYAIR_MCP_URL` via the `ByAirClient` HTTP wrapper and filters the raw ~13KB response down to a ~1KB operational slice before any state write, so the agent never saw byAir directly and had no pull-based path to look up an arbitrary or friend's flight. New `container/agent-runner/src/byair-mcp.ts` exposes a pure `byairMcpServer(byairUrl)` helper that returns `{ byair: { type: 'http', url } }` when the URL is set and `{}` otherwise; `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` spreads it into `mcpServersConfig` right after the Composio block. Design tension (raw vs. shim) resolved in favour of **raw**: the use is occasional and pull-based, so the per-call ~13KB context cost is acceptable — a filtering shim's ~1KB slice only pays off under a frequent loop, which this is not. The registration is deliberately **not** `alwaysLoad` (unlike the `nanoclaw` stdio server, matching Composio), so byAir's tool surface stays deferred behind a ToolSearch hop — out of the always-on context and, critically, off the proactive precheck/wake loop (the precheck keeps reaching byAir via the Python `ByAirClient`, untouched by this change). No auth header: the API key rides inline in the URL query string (`api.byairapp.com/mcp?api_key=…`), mirroring how the precheck consumes the same env var. `BYAIR_MCP_URL` already reaches **every** main/trusted container through `CONTAINER_VARS` + the secret env-file in `src/container-runner.ts` (no new credential plumbing), so the byAir tool registers in all main/trusted chats — not only those carrying the `nanoclaw-flight-assist` overlay. The spread is a no-op only in untrusted tiers, where no byAir credential is forwarded. Because the credential already lived in those containers (the flight-assist precheck reads it), this widens only the read-only tool surface, not the credential surface — same gating shape as Composio's `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY`. Broad main/trusted availability is intended: the agent should be able to answer an ad-hoc "has Amir landed?" in any operator-controlled chat, not just one with the flight overlay mounted. byAir reads are intentionally **not** added to the `untrusted-input` source taxonomy in this PR: byAir returns the operator's own travel data plus friends' flights the operator added, a low injection surface unlike the arbitrary third-party content from `web`/`gmail`/`slack` reads that taint provenance — defense-in-depth tainting can be a separate follow-up if warranted. 4 unit tests in `byair-mcp.test.ts` pin the registration shape (undefined/empty → `{}`, set → `http` transport with the URL verbatim, inline `api_key` query param preserved). Closes #645.

- Fixed `src/onecli-client.ts` reading `ONECLI_URL` + `ONECLI_API_KEY` from `process.env`, which was empty inside the orchestrator container — `docker-compose.yml` forwards an explicit env allowlist, not `env_file`, so OneCLI's two vars never reached `process.env` and `isOneCliConfigured()` returned `false` even with both populated in `~/nanoclaw/.env` on the NAS. Switched to `readEnvFile(['ONECLI_URL', 'ONECLI_API_KEY'])` — the canonical pattern the rest of the orchestrator uses for credential-shaped reads (see `src/env.ts`: "Does NOT load anything into process.env — this keeps secrets out of the process environment so they don't leak to child processes"). Tests now mock `./env.js` instead of mutating `process.env`. Caught live during #636 (operational install on NAS) when `docker exec nanoclaw printenv ONECLI_URL` returned empty even after `~/nanoclaw/.env` was populated and the container was recreated.

- OneCLI groundwork (#635 of umbrella #564) — added `@onecli-sh/sdk@^2.1.0` and `src/onecli-client.ts`, a thin SDK wrapper exposing `isOneCliConfigured()`, `ensureAgentForTier(tier)`, and `applyOneCliToSpawn(args, tier)`. The orchestrator's `main()` calls `ensureAgentForTier` once per trust tier (`main`/`trusted`/`untrusted`) after the credential-proxy starts; `container-runner.ts` calls `applyOneCliToSpawn(containerArgs, trustTier)` between `buildContainerArgs` and the docker spawn. Both code paths gate on `ONECLI_URL` + `ONECLI_API_KEY` being set — when either is missing (the default until the operational install sub-issue #636 lands on the NAS), every export is a graceful no-op and the spawn path is byte-identical to the prior behavior. Stage 1's error policy is permissive: a OneCLI gateway that's unreachable, returns 5xx, or rejects with `OneCLIError` logs a `warn` and falls through to the existing credential-proxy path. Unexpected error classes still propagate. `.env.example` documents both new env vars under a dedicated OneCLI section pointing at #564. The subsequent sub-issues — #636 (operational install on NAS), #637 (Anthropic/OpenAI credential swap from credential-proxy to OneCLI MITM), #638 (Gmail/Calendar/Drive via `gemini-cli-extensions/workspace` MCP), #639 (composio retirement), #640 (remaining `.env` key migration) — depend on this groundwork landing and the gateway being reachable from the orchestrator container. Tests in `src/onecli-client.test.ts` use a mocked SDK and cover: unconfigured no-op behavior, configured success path (tier-scoped identifier passed to `ensureAgent`, args mutated by `applyContainerConfig`), graceful degradation on `OneCLIRequestError` and `OneCLIError`, and propagation of unexpected error classes. Closes #635.

- `audible-backup` now honours an operator-maintained skip-list at `<library>/audible-backup-skiplist.txt` so the same Plus-only / unowned ASINs that hit the `"not downloadable"` branch every weekly run (`1663704015` Healing the Wounds, `B079LRSMNN` Galaxy's Edge, `1774242834` Tin Man at last verification) stop generating `status: "skipped"` records once the operator has ack'd them once. File format: one ASIN per line, `#` introduces an end-of-line comment for the operator's title hint, blank lines and pure-comment lines ignored. New `load_skiplist(path)` helper reads the file (missing file → empty set, bootstrap state not an error); `find_new_books` gains an optional `skiplist_asins` parameter that filters skiplist'd ASINs out before the download branch fires. The "not downloadable" branch in `download_and_decrypt` now also emits a one-shot stderr reminder naming the skiplist file path + ASIN, so the operator sees the explicit-ack invitation alongside the skipped record. Per-run JSON output gains a top-level `skipped_by_filter: ["<asin>", ...]` field (sorted) so the audit trail is preserved across runs — once a book is filtered upstream the per-book `books[]` no longer mentions it, and without this field the operator would have no way to confirm "yes, I told it to skip this on purpose" from the JSON alone. Explicit-ack design (operator preference per #632 closeout): no auto-promotion after N consecutive skips, the operator has to add the line themselves. Eight new unit tests in `test_backup.py` cover the parser (one ASIN per line, inline `#` comments stripped, blank lines / pure-comment lines skipped, missing file returns empty set) and the filter integration (filters skiplist ASINs, third-arg default preserves legacy callers, known + skiplist both apply). Closes #632.

- `audible-backup`'s `find_new_books` keyed off ASIN-presence in inventory only, so once a record existed no later run revisited the entry — even if the m4b was removed from disk (Google Drive sync hiccup, manual cleanup, partial restore, disk migration). Operator only noticed at playback time. New `find_missing_on_disk(inventory, books_dir)` helper surfaces inventory rows with a non-empty `filename` whose file is missing under `books_dir`; main wires it after `find_new_books` and emits per-book `status: "missing_on_disk"` records into the JSON output alongside the existing `ok` / `skipped` / `failed` buckets, plus a new `missing_on_disk` count field. Soft-alert design per the issue (operator preference): no automatic redownload, preserves enriched metadata, lets a slow Google Drive sync rehydrate the file on next access. The dry-run path includes the missing set in its `books` list and the empty-new-books branch surfaces them too — those were the cases where a missing-on-disk gap would otherwise stay invisible across an entire `--dry-run` audit cycle. Records with an empty `filename` are skipped (never-downloaded state, outside scope); a directory at `<books_dir>/<filename>` is correctly classified as missing (defends against operator state where they mkdir'd over a missing m4b for chapter splits). Six new unit tests in `test_backup.py` cover the happy path, empty-filename / missing-filename-key skip paths, empty-inventory passthrough, full-record return shape, and the directory-vs-file rejection. Closes #631.

- `audible-backup` sidecar lifted out of `/tmp/audible-backup/` on the NAS into `container/audible-backup/` in the repo, patched to discover audible-cli artifacts by mtime instead of by ASIN-prefix filename glob, and migrated from `audible decrypt` to direct ffmpeg decryption (#625). `backup.py` had used `download_dir.glob(f"{asin}*.aax")` to locate the downloaded encrypted audio file; audible-cli's filename convention has drifted across releases and currently emits title-prefixed names like `Red_Rising-LC_128_44100_stereo.aax`, so for every weekly cadence run since `books-library.csv` last advanced on 2026-03-29 the glob matched zero files and `backup.py` reported `"no audio file found for <ASIN>"` — even though audible-cli HAD successfully fetched the files into `/library/tmp_download/`. The end state on 2026-05-22 was 12-of-15 books failing with the same error, ~5 GB of stranded title-prefixed downloads in `tmp_download/`, and the cadence task `cadence-registry::telegram_swarm::tessl__entertainment-sync` reporting `status=success` on every weekly run because `backup.py` was returning exit 0 regardless of per-book outcomes. The shell-script equivalent `scripts/audible-backup.sh` had already been migrated to mtime-based discovery in `8d07215d` for the exact same reason (the comment trail in the shell script names Moriarty/Dave Smith/Red Rising as titles the ASIN-prefix glob had stranded), but `backup.py` — which is what the IPC handler actually runs via the `audible-backup:latest` Docker image — was never updated. The fix replaces both the discovery glob in `download_and_decrypt` and the per-book cleanup glob with a single `download_dir.iterdir()` walk filtered by `stat().st_mtime > before_download`, with a -1s margin for filesystem timestamp resolution so files that land in the same second as the recorded start aren't lost. A second discovery path parses `audible-cli`'s `File <path> already exists. Skip download.` stdout lines to find pre-existing files on a recovery / idempotent-retry run — audible-cli does NOT bump mtime when the target file already exists, so the mtime walk would otherwise mis-fail as "no audio file" on every retry. The audio classifier prefers `.aaxc` (encrypted with voucher) over `.aax` (activation bytes) when both appear. Per-book cleanup now iterates the `touched` list directly instead of re-walking by mtime so the recovery path cleans up stranded files even when their mtimes predate the run. A SECOND, independent bug surfaced during the live retry on 2026-05-22: `audible-cli` 0.3 (released March 2024) removed the `decrypt` subcommand the script was calling, so every backup since whichever build refreshed `audible-cli` past 0.3.0 would have hit `Error: No such command 'decrypt'.` — the title-prefix glob bug had been firing first and masking it. The decrypt path is now ffmpeg-based: AAX uses `ffmpeg -activation_bytes <hex>` with the per-account bytes fetched once via `audible activation-bytes` and cached for the run; AAXC uses `ffmpeg -audible_key <hex> -audible_iv <hex>` with the per-file key+IV extracted from the `.voucher` JSON (handles both the nested-object and JSON-string shapes audible-cli has emitted over its release history). The voucher is now archived alongside the AAXC source in `/library/aax/` — the prior code only moved the audio file, leaving every AAXC archive un-re-decryptable since the voucher would get deleted by the cleanup pass. `backup.py` now exits 1 when `failed > 0` (skipped books — Plus-only / not owned — are expected and stay at exit 0), so the IPC handler's `error` branch fires and the cadence wrapper records the run as failed instead of silent success. The IPC handler in `src/ipc.ts` now parses the script's stdout JSON even on non-zero exit so the per-book `books[]` breakdown isn't lost on partial-success runs (mixed ok/failed), with the script's `error.message` merged into the parsed payload as a sibling field; the bare `{error, raw, logs}` payload only fires when stdout isn't JSON-parseable. The sidecar source files (`backup.py`, `enrich.py`, `Dockerfile`, `README.md`) and a thin `build.sh` now live in `container/audible-backup/` — the previous `/tmp/audible-backup/` location was one NAS reboot away from disappearing, with no git history to recover from. Three pure helpers (`parse_skipped_paths`, `classify_artifacts`, `aaxc_key_iv`, plus `parse_activation_bytes`) are extracted from `download_and_decrypt` / `get_activation_bytes` and pinned by 25 stdlib-unittest cases (`test_backup.py` × 19, `test_enrich.py` × 6) covering voucher-shape variants, classifier priority, the recovery-path regex anchor, the empty-stdout RuntimeError path, and the `merge_enrichment` rules including the `series_title` → `series_name` schema fix. `audible-cli` is pinned to `==0.3.3` in the Dockerfile so a future rebuild doesn't silently pick up another breaking subcommand-removal the way the 0.3.0 `decrypt` regression went undetected for ~8 weeks. The base image (`python:3.12-slim`) and apt-installed `ffmpeg` are deliberately left floating — operator carve-out from `coding-policy: dependency-management`'s strict pin requirement: the sidecar is rebuilt only on demand, the operator has opted out of manual base-image maintenance, and floating these layers lets each rebuild pick up Debian / Python security patches without a manifest update. The tradeoff (rebuild-to-rebuild drift on the OS layer) is accepted for runtime infrastructure but not for the Python application dependency where a version bump can silently break the script contract. Live retry on 2026-05-23 with the rebuilt image successfully downloaded + decrypted 11 of 12 listed failures (Triggernometry/`B0G1Z1H6QL` still fails — audible-cli delivers it as `.mp3` which the classifier doesn't yet handle; tracked separately as the MP3-source sub-bug already noted in the shell-script's classifier comments). Multi-chapter Audible Originals (`B0B2DZBVRR` Moriarty downloads into a `<title>/` subdirectory) are a separate sub-bug — the run completed on the Audible-provided trailer rather than the full multi-chapter content. Closes #625.

- Fixed `writeFlightAssistLocation` leaving the per-group `flight-assist/` state subdir owned by root:root, which blocked the agent container's sync_tripit precheck from writing its own state files (`flight-*.json`, sync-tripit lock files) with PermissionError. The orchestrator runs as root inside its container, so `fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true })` created the subdir with root ownership and mode 755 — every other state subdir (`heartbeat/`, `composio-fetch/`, etc.) is created from inside the agent container as `HOST_UID:HOST_GID` (999:10 here) so flight-assist was the only outlier and the only host-written state-subdir owner. `container-runner.ts` chowns the per-group state dir at creation but never visits host-created per-skill subdirs, so the writer now matches that pattern itself: `lchownSync` the dir to `HOST_UID:HOST_GID` after the idempotent mkdir, gated by an `lstatSync` symlink-refusal guard (the dir lives under a mount the agent can write, so a non-traversing chown is mandatory — `chownSync` would let an attacker who `rmdir`'d the empty subdir and replaced it with `flight-assist -> /etc/shadow` redirect the root chown to an arbitrary target; mirrors the `chownRecursive` pattern in `container-runner.ts`). Self-healing on every write: the idempotent mkdir + unconditional lchown also fixes already-deployed root-owned dirs the next time the owner shares a location, so no manual chown is required on a fresh deploy. EPERM/EACCES on the lchown means the orchestrator is running unprivileged (e.g. unit tests) — surfaced via `logger.warn` rather than silently swallowed per `nanoclaw-host: no-error-suppression`. Existing root-owned `flight-assist/` dirs on the NAS at deploy time can be either left for the next owner location-share to self-heal, or manually unstuck via `sudo chown -R 999:10 /home/jbaruch/nanoclaw/data/state/*/flight-assist/`.

- Added `extra_hosts: host.docker.internal:host-gateway` to the orchestrator service in `docker-compose.yml` so the credential-proxy can reach the LiteLLM router's published port from inside its own bridge network. Live verification on 2026-05-23 — after the previous round of PRs documented `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://192.168.10.32:4000` as the cutover knob — found that the orchestrator container's bridge network (`nanoclaw_default`, 172.18.0.0/16) is isolated from the LiteLLM project's bridge network (`nanoclaw-litellm_default`), and docker's iptables rules don't forward inter-bridge traffic to host-published ports. Neither the NAS LAN IP nor the bridge gateway IP (`172.18.0.1:4000`) was reachable from the orchestrator container. `host.docker.internal:host-gateway` is the canonical Docker-on-Linux pattern: the docker daemon resolves `host-gateway` at container start to the host's bridge interface IP, which IS the bind target of the `0.0.0.0:4000` published port; reaching it via the in-container `host.docker.internal` hostname works regardless of which compose project the LiteLLM container belongs to. The orchestrator cutover knob is now `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://host.docker.internal:4000` in `~/nanoclaw/.env`; the LAN-IP form in the previously-merged docs is wrong and has been corrected in `container/litellm/docker-compose.yaml`, `.env.example`, and the orchestrator compose's removal-notice block. The change requires recreating the orchestrator container (not just `restart`) so the new `extra_hosts` entry takes effect — `./scripts/deploy.sh` handles that as part of its normal `docker compose up -d --build` step. Refs #610.

- Documented the proven UGOS-Pro-project-on-symlinked-repo-dir topology in `container/litellm/docker-compose.yaml`'s setup block, the orchestrator compose's removal-notice block, and `.env.example`. Live verification on 2026-05-23 found four facts the previous docs assumed but did not state explicitly: (1) UGOS Pro renders projects from a SQLite DB at `/volume1/@appstore/com.ugreen.docker/db/docker_info_log.db` (table `compose`, columns `name`, `path`, `content`) — inserting a row makes the project appear in the Projects UI, deleting the row removes it. (2) UGOS Pro's UI env-var entry pastes literal values into the compose file's `environment:` block (not into a separate env layer), so when the project dir is symlinked into a tracked repo path those values land in the tracked file — a close call this session, recovered before git contamination because the file was still untracked on the NAS. (3) The safe pattern is `${VAR}` placeholders in the compose plus a relative `.env` symlink at `<repo>/container/<project>/.env -> ../../.env`; Linux resolves the relative target against the symlink's own parent, so the chain `/volume1/docker/<project>/.env` → `<repo>/container/<project>/.env` → `~/nanoclaw/.env` is followed correctly through both symlink layers at docker compose read time. (4) UGOS UI Stop+Start on a project configured this way does NOT rewrite the compose file — verified by file-size check across multiple restart cycles (stayed at 4386 bytes), and by inspecting the post-restart container env (`LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` length 64, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` length 108, `LITELLM_GATEWAY_API_KEY` length 0) which proves docker compose interpolated `${VAR}` from the symlinked `.env` rather than from any UGOS-stored UI state. The canonical setup steps now live in `container/litellm/docker-compose.yaml` (header comment block) covering: orphan cleanup, dir symlink creation, `.env` symlink creation, sudo INSERT into the UGOS DB so the project surfaces in UI, click Start (NOT "Edit"). The orchestrator's removal-notice block in `docker-compose.yml` generalises the pattern for future UGOS Pro projects and adds the "never paste literals into UI" warning explicitly. `.env.example` documents that LITELLM_* keys live in `~/nanoclaw/.env` (the runtime counterpart of this example file, gitignored) and reach the UGOS project via the `.env` symlink — NOT via UGOS UI's env field. Refs #610.

- Removed the `nanoclaw-litellm` service from `docker-compose.yml`. The LiteLLM router now runs as a separate UGOS Pro project on the NASync NAS, while its compose file + config stay in this repo under `container/litellm/` and the NAS surfaces them via a symlink at `/volume1/docker/nanoclaw-litellm` → `~/nanoclaw/container/litellm/`. The orchestrator gets the same treatment (`/volume1/docker/nanoclaw` → `~/nanoclaw/`) so both projects show up in UGOS Pro's Projects UI while their source-of-truth stays under `deploy.sh`'s control. Driving reason: the second-deploy verification on 2026-05-22 caught a `nanoclaw-litellm` SIGKILL-on-recreate that couldn't be traced to my own `deploy.sh` step 5 (which excludes nanoclaw-litellm post-#624); a parallel agent's docker activity on the shared NAS was the most plausible culprit, and the project-level supervisor in UGOS Pro re-launches the container automatically after such kills whereas `restart: always` alone does not (Docker's restart policy intentionally skips after `docker kill` / `docker stop`). The paste-ready compose lives at `container/litellm/docker-compose.yaml` (UGOS Pro reads this name by convention) and `container/litellm/litellm.config.yaml` carries the canonical LiteLLM router config. Secrets stay out of the repo — `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`, `LITELLM_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` live in `~/nanoclaw/.env` (gitignored) and reach the UGOS Pro project via a `.env` symlink at `container/litellm/.env -> ../../.env`; docker compose interpolates `${VAR}` placeholders from that file at spawn time. **NEVER paste literal env values into UGOS Pro's project env UI** — UGOS dumps UI-entered values into the compose file's `environment:` block, which (through the dir symlink) would land secrets in the tracked repo file. See the entry above for the full topology. The credential-proxy's bypass logic, the per-target `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` vs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` split, the `ANTHROPIC_BYPASS_URL` env var, and the 19 cred-proxy tests from #610 all stay — none of that depended on the service living in this compose. The orchestrator reaches the LiteLLM router via `http://host.docker.internal:4000` (orchestrator's `extra_hosts` block maps `host.docker.internal` to the host gateway so inter-bridge docker traffic resolves to the LiteLLM project's published port; operator flips by adding `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://host.docker.internal:4000` to `~/nanoclaw/.env` once the UGOS project is up). `deploy.sh`'s `grep -Ev '^nanoclaw(-litellm)?$'` exclusion and `scripts/test_deploy_agent_grep.py`'s regression test stay as forward-compat guards against any future re-addition of a similarly-named sidecar; `deploy.sh` also gained `--remove-orphans` on its main `docker compose up -d --build` so the pre-removal nanoclaw-litellm container is cleaned up automatically when this PR's compose change reaches the NAS.

- `scripts/deploy.sh` step 5 ("Gracefully close agent containers") now excludes `nanoclaw-litellm` from the agent set (#610 follow-up). The pattern `docker ps … | grep '^nanoclaw-' | grep -v '^nanoclaw$'` was added in #221 to match every agent container while skipping the orchestrator itself; when #610 introduced `nanoclaw-litellm` as an infrastructure sidecar (no `/workspace/ipc/input` mount), the pattern matched it as an agent. The `_close` sentinel write failed (mount unresolvable), the container went into the `UNRESOLVED` list, and the post-grace force-kill loop SIGKILL'd it with exit 137 ~30s after spawn — observed on the 2026-05-22 deploy where the LiteLLM container died with empty logs. The exclude is now `grep -Ev '^nanoclaw(-litellm)?$'` and the inline comment documents the rule: any future infrastructure containers added to `docker-compose.yml` whose names match `^nanoclaw-` must be appended to this alternation.

- `nanoclaw-litellm` healthcheck now probes via `python3 -c "import urllib.request..."` instead of `wget --spider` (#610 follow-up). Post-deploy verification on 2026-05-22 found the LiteLLM upstream image ships neither `wget` nor `curl` — `/usr/bin/` carries `python3` + `python3.13` only — and every healthcheck probe failed with `OCI runtime exec failed: exec: "wget": executable file not found in $PATH`. The container kept serving traffic on port 4000 because `restart: always` does not restart on healthcheck-only failures, but the perpetual `unhealthy` flag polluted any monitoring consuming Docker's health state and would have blocked a future `depends_on: { nanoclaw-litellm: { condition: service_healthy } }` wiring from the orchestrator. The replacement probe lives inline in `docker-compose.yml`, uses the stdlib `urllib.request`, returns 0 only on a successful response from `/health/liveliness`, and times out at 4s to stay under the healthcheck's `timeout: 5s` envelope. Catches `(urllib.error.URLError, TimeoutError)` per `coding-policy: error-handling` — URLError covers connection refused, DNS, TLS, and HTTPError on non-2xx (HTTPError subclasses URLError); TimeoutError is the Python 3.10+ alias for socket.timeout. Anything else (e.g. ImportError from a broken stdlib) propagates so the operator sees the real problem rather than a probe falsely reporting `unhealthy`. Failure cases print a single-line `unhealthy` token and exit 1 so Docker's `.State.Health.Log[].Output` stays one-line per probe instead of a multi-line urllib traceback.

- New `nanoclaw-litellm` Docker service (pinned to `ghcr.io/berriai/litellm:v1.85.1`) stands up a NAS-local LiteLLM router between the orchestrator's credential-proxy and the `litellm.ai` cloud gateway (#610, first sub-issue of #609's cost-migration epic). Stage 0 config (`container/litellm/litellm.config.yaml`) carries Anthropic-shape passthrough for `claude-opus-4-7`, `claude-sonnet-4-6`, and `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` with router-level fallback to `api.anthropic.com` on gateway 5xx — workload-keyed `model_list` aliases (per-tier inbound, per-scheduled-task) land in #613. Two new env vars documented in `.env.example`: `LITELLM_GATEWAY_API_KEY` (cloud-gateway virtual key the container uses for outbound) and `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` (inbound master key — kept distinct from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` so a key rotation on the LiteLLM side doesn't couple to the Anthropic provider credential, and a future LiteLLM debug-log of the master_key value can't leak the provider credential). The credential-proxy picks the right `x-api-key` per target — `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` when forwarding to the primary, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` when bypassing to api.anthropic.com — and falls back to `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for the primary when `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` is unset so a partial-config deploy still flows traffic (graceful path for incident recovery, not steady state). Orchestrator-side bypass in `src/credential-proxy.ts` wraps the existing upstream-firing path in a `sendUpstreamRequest(targetUrl, fromBypass)` helper that retries against a configurable bypass URL (`ANTHROPIC_BYPASS_URL`, default `https://api.anthropic.com`) on ECONNREFUSED, ENOTFOUND, EHOSTUNREACH, ECONNRESET, idle-timeout >3s, or 5xx from the primary — covers the case where `nanoclaw-litellm` itself is unreachable, which the in-router fallback chain can't handle since it's running inside the container that's down. The idle-timeout install is gated on `bypassEnabled && !fromBypass` per `coding-policy: error-handling` Graceful Fallback so slow-Anthropic-edge requests in same-origin / OAuth / bypass-attempt paths aren't cut off when there's no fallback to drive to. The bypass attempt's own failure surfaces as the existing 502 path so a double-failure mode (LiteLLM down + Anthropic unreachable) doesn't infinite-loop. Bypass is automatically disabled when (a) the primary and bypass URLs share origin (no second target to try) or (b) auth mode is OAuth (the OAuth-exchange endpoint doesn't exist on LiteLLM, so a bypass would route mid-handshake to the wrong target). 19 tests in `src/credential-proxy.test.ts` cover the same-origin 502 path, the bypass behaviours (primary refuses → bypass succeeds, primary 5xx → bypass succeeds, primary 4xx → no bypass passthrough, both unreachable → 502, primary idle-hang → bypass succeeds via configurable `idleTimeoutMs` option), the OAuth-mode no-bypass guard, the `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` / `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` per-target key split, and the slow-but-valid upstream regression guard (proves a 150ms response with bypass disabled still completes when `idleTimeoutMs` is 50ms). Tee-and-capture for `usage.jsonl` continues to fire on whichever attempt produces the response (primary or bypass), so observability stays continuous through the bypass path until #615 migrates the wiretap to a LiteLLM callback. Refs #609 #610.

- Agent-runner now suppresses the SDK contradiction-shape false-positive at the result-classification seam (`#619`). On some normal completions the Claude Agent SDK reports `is_error: true` despite the other three classification fields signaling clean termination (`subtype: 'success'`, `terminal_reason: 'completed'`, `stop_reason: 'stop_sequence'`). The legacy heuristic in `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` (`isError = errMsg.is_error === true || (subtype !== 'success' && subtype !== 'unknown')`) trusted the single contradictory signal over the three concurring ones and emitted `status: 'error'` rows into `task_run_logs`. Downstream effect: `skills/heartbeat/scripts/heartbeat-checks.py::check_task_failures` read those rows for 24 h and fired the `system_health_issues` wake-reason every cycle, dropping heartbeat gating from 77.1 % (2026-05-17) to 13.9 % (2026-05-22 partial) — exactly the bleed-through that masked the `#598` email-loop fix landed in `nanoclaw-admin#279`+`#288`. The classification decision is extracted to a pure `classifyResultIsError(errMsg)` in `container/agent-runner/src/classify-result-error.ts` (single-owner for the suppression, follows the `format-error-result.ts` / `silent-stop-synthesis.ts` extraction precedent) and applied at the existing `if (isError) { ... } else { ... }` seam in the result loop. The suppression is narrow: `subtype === 'success' && is_error === true && stop_reason === 'stop_sequence' && (terminal_reason === 'completed' || !terminal_reason)`. Every other `is_error: true` shape — `tool_use_limit_reached`, `permission_denied`, `max_tokens`, refusal, `error_during_execution` — stays a genuine failure; the `#582` test for `subtype: 'success' + is_error: true + stop_reason: 'tool_use' + terminal_reason: 'tool_use_limit_reached'` continues to classify as error (pinned in the new test suite as well so a future refactor can't silently widen the suppression). Companion admin-side filter in `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#295` covers existing `task_run_logs` rows during the 24 h `TASK_FAILURE_HOURS` overlap with this deploy. 10 unit tests in `container/agent-runner/src/classify-result-error.test.ts` pin the contract: clean success, classic error_during_execution, legacy `subtype !== 'success'` heuristic, the four positive cases of the suppression (with and without `terminal_reason`), and four negative cases (tool_use_limit, subtype=error, wrong terminal_reason, unknown subtype). Closes #619.

- Agent-runner now splits its scheduled-task precheck exit into two distinct shapes so silent-success watchdogs can act on the row in `task_run_logs` (#581 follow-up to #591). Pre-fix `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts:4421-4435` emitted `writeOutput({status: 'success', result: null})` whenever the precheck script returned `wake_agent: false` OR crashed / produced non-JSON / omitted `wake_agent`. Verification after #591 merged found this is the actual shape AyeAye kept flagging for `nightly-external-sync`: every fire 2026-05-21 → 2026-05-22 ran in 4–6 s (just the precheck script's runtime) with `status='success', result=null` — far below the 78–553 s actual-sync window. The agent never woke. The cursor stamp from the one nightly-external-sync run that DID reach Step 10 (2026-05-20T04:06:58Z, durable in `/workspace/group/state/nightly-external-sync-cursor.json`) put every subsequent fire well inside the 3-day cadence cap; the precheck was correctly saying "no work needed" but the row in `task_run_logs` looked identical to the original #581 bug shape (an agent that woke and produced an empty result). Fix splits the two outcomes: a new `'precheck_skipped'` status value (added to `TaskRunLog.status` in `src/types.ts` and `ContainerOutput.status` in `src/container-runner.ts` and `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts`) carries a non-null diagnostic result `<internal>precheck-skipped: ${JSON.stringify(scriptResult.data)}</internal>` so the precheck's gating-decision payload (e.g. `reason: within_cadence`, `last_run`, `age_hours`, `cadence_hours` for cadence-cap prechecks) reaches `task_run_logs.result` for operator audit; the `<internal>` envelope strips to empty in the orchestrator's chat-echo path so the precheck-skip doesn't spam chat. The genuine-failure case (precheck script crashed, emitted non-JSON, or omitted `wake_agent`) now lands as `status: 'error'` with `<internal>precheck-error: ...</internal>` + a populated `error` column — flows through the existing error pipeline instead of masking as silent success. Pure helpers `buildPrecheckSkippedOutput` and `buildPrecheckErrorOutput` extracted to `container/agent-runner/src/precheck-emission.ts` for unit-testability following the precedent of `result-suppression.ts` (#591), `silent-stop-synthesis.ts`, and `format-error-result.ts`. Task-scheduler's `runStatus` mapping (`src/task-scheduler.ts`) now propagates `'precheck_skipped'` verbatim through `logTaskRun`, and `scheduleClose()` fires for the new status the same as for `'success'` so the container winds down (the agent never woke, there's no script-result poll to wait for). The `AgentResult` union in `src/session-commands.ts` widens to include `'precheck_skipped'` for type-assignability with `runAgent`'s callback signature, even though slash commands never emit it (the agent-runner only writes that status from the scheduled-task `runScript` branch). 8 new unit tests in `precheck-emission.test.ts` exercise the data-payload-passthrough, empty-data, nested-shape, and never-emits-success contracts of both builders; 1 new task-scheduler integration test asserts `status=precheck_skipped` lands in `task_run_logs` with the diagnostic result and chat-echo stays suppressed (the `<internal>` envelope strips to empty in the orchestrator's tag-strip path, so the operator does not see a "precheck skipped" spam in chat every fire). Refs #581.

- `fetch_markdown` host handler now uses a tolerant JSON parser (`parseSnitchmdStdout`) that recovers the payload when CloakBrowser leaks log lines onto stdout BEFORE snitchmd's JSON. Post-merge smoke test on 2026-05-21 caught this on the cold-pull path: CloakBrowser writes `[cloakbrowser] Newer Chromium available: ... Downloading in background... Download progress: 9% ...` to stdout while waiting for the chromium update to land, and snitchmd's JSON output is appended after. The strict `JSON.parse(stdout)` choked on the prefix even though the actual fetch succeeded (visible in the cached payload that was written before the error path fired). Cached calls were never affected — the chatter only fires on the cold-pull tick. New parser tries the strict parse first (fast path), then falls back to slicing from the first `{` to the last `}` and retrying. Returns `ok=false` only when both attempts fail so the existing "non-JSON output" diagnostic branch (with its URL-secret scrub) still catches genuinely malformed output. 7 new unit tests in `src/fetch-markdown-args.test.ts` cover the clean fast-path, the pre-JSON-noise repro from the smoke test, post-JSON noise (defensive), empty / no-braces stdout, the slice-still-malformed case, and propagation of non-SyntaxError exceptions through the fast-path catch. Follow-up to #603.

- New `mcp__nanoclaw__fetch_markdown` tool wraps the `syabro/snitchmd` Docker CLI (CloakBrowser + rs-trafilatura) as a host-side MCP handler, giving the agent a clean-markdown fetch path that bypasses Cloudflare / anti-bot gates and renders JS-only SPAs (#169). Driving motivation: the existing fetch story was bimodal — built-in `WebFetch` returns empty shells on React/Vue/Angular pages and silently 403s on Cloudflare; the `agent-browser` skill handles everything but spins up a full Playwright session (slow, token-heavy from screenshots) just to extract text. snitchmd fills the gap: anti-bot bypass via CloakBrowser, content extraction via rs-trafilatura, disk cache by URL+flags so repeat fetches are free. Architecture: container-side MCP tool in `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` routes through `runHostOperation('fetch_markdown', …, 260_000)` to the host handler in `src/ipc.ts` `case 'fetch_markdown'`, which `execFile('docker', ['run', '--rm', '-v', '<HOST_PROJECT_ROOT>/store/snitchmd-cache:/cache', <image>, url, ...flags])` — sibling container via the orchestrator's mounted `/var/run/docker.sock`, same Docker-out-of-Docker pattern as the agent-container spawner. snitchmd's `--json` mode is always passed so the handler can write back a 3-line header (title, final_url, chars + optional quality) plus the markdown body. Default image is `syabro/snitchmd:latest` — snitchmd is an app-level renderer + content extractor, not an API contract, and the floating tag lets us ride upstream CloakBrowser fingerprint updates as anti-bot detection evolves (operators who need a reproducible build can pin to a specific tag or `sha256:…` digest via `SNITCHMD_IMAGE` env without a code change). Cold-pull on first call is bounded by a 240 s `execFile` timeout, the IPC envelope adds 20 s headroom so the docker process fails before runHostOperation times out. URL validation rejects non-http(s) protocols and unparseable strings upfront with an actionable error message; the 16 MiB `maxBuffer` cap catches runaway pages before exhausting orchestrator memory. Exposed flags mirror the snitchmd CLI: `wait`, `wait_until` (commit | domcontentloaded | load | networkidle), `wait_for_selector`, `favor_precision` / `favor_recall`, `include_links`, `include_images`, `max_chars`, `no_cache`, `timeout`. Snake_case at the agent boundary, camelCase inside the IPC envelope (`waitUntil`, `waitForSelector`, etc.) per the project's existing convention. The tool is registered for ALL trust tiers (not gated by `isMain`) — fetch is read-only and useful from untrusted containers too; the host handler doesn't reach any privileged credentials, so the gate would just hide a legitimate capability. Untrusted-input envelope: a new `READ_TOOL_PATTERNS` row in `container/agent-runner/src/untrusted-input-wrap.ts` maps `mcp__nanoclaw__fetch_markdown` to the existing `web` source prefix, so #322's walk-back applies cross-source-cell rate limits on chains that surface fetched content into sensitive sinks. Wiki ingest (`container/skills/wiki/SKILL.md`) flipped to `fetch_markdown` as the default URL→text path; `WebFetch` and the `agent-browser` skill remain documented escalations (static-only / interactive flows). New `docs/fetch-tools.md` carries the three-tool decision matrix (WebFetch vs fetch_markdown vs agent-browser), tiebreakers (try-cheapest-and-escalate, cache-wins-for-repeats), and operational notes on cache path / image pinning / cold-pull behaviour. The same-issue admin-tile skill migrations (`check-cfps` Step 3 JS-rendered fallback, `no-unverified-claims` Verification Checker fetch step — currently routed through Composio's `CLOUDFLARE_BROWSER_RENDERING_*` tools) are tracked as a follow-up PR against `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin` since they live in a separate repo. Closes #169.

- Host-side writer for the per-group `current-location.json` stateful artifact consumed by `jbaruch/nanoclaw-flight-assist` (`nanoclaw-flight-assist#18`). The orchestrator's `onLocation` callback now wraps `storeLocation` so every Telegram `message:location` / `message:venue` / `edited_message:location` event from the owner ALSO atomically writes `<DATA_DIR>/state/<group.folder>/flight-assist/current-location.json`. **`src/flight-assist-location.ts` is the artifact's authoritative schema, writer contract, and migration-policy declaration per `jbaruch/coding-policy: stateful-artifacts`** — this module is the artifact's owner; flight-assist's `state.read_current_location` is the documented non-owner reader (must not migrate; rejects schema-version mismatches by returning `None`). Schema v1: `{schema_version: 1, latitude (float in [-90, 90]), longitude (float in [-180, 180]), captured_at (RFC-3339 UTC instant)}`. The flight-assist tile (registry 0.1.9) already shipped the reader-side validator in PR #19; without the host writer, the precheck's origin-resolution ladder degraded silently to `home_address` even when the owner was sharing live location. Atomic tmp-and-rename per the existing `db-dump.ts` pattern. Filters: skips when `record.sender !== ASSISTANT_OWNER_TG_USER_ID`, when the owner-id env is unset, or when `chat_jid` is not in `registeredGroups` (a group-member's pin doesn't mean "the owner is here", and a non-registered chat has no state dir). Filesystem errors (`NodeJS.ErrnoException` — `.code` present) are logged-and-swallowed so message ingestion never fails on the sidecar write; programming bugs propagate so they surface in dev rather than hiding behind a `warn`. 8 unit tests in `src/flight-assist-location.test.ts` cover the documented-schema write, `recorded_at` passthrough (including live-update tick times), the three skip paths, group-folder resolution, atomic overwrite (no `.tmp` leftover after a successful write), and the fs-error swallow path.

- Retired the temporary `usage-log: parser returned null (structural diagnostic)` INFO log in `src/credential-proxy.ts` introduced by PR #491 (gzip-decompression fix). AyeAye's hourly watchdog logged 7 consecutive clean days on 2026-05-11 and self-deleted; the safety-net diagnostic has earned its retirement. The record-write tracker (`noteCaptureWrite`) and the actual `appendUsageRecord` path are untouched. Closes #492.

- MCP wiring for the three `AGENT_MODEL` IPC handlers (#595). Adds `set_agent_model`, `set_maintenance_agent_model`, and `set_task_agent_model` as `mcp__nanoclaw__*` tools in `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts`, fire-and-forget via `writeIpcFile(TASKS_DIR, …)` with "requested" response phrasing since the host applies asynchronously. The three host-side handlers (`src/ipc.ts:1791`, `:1891`, `:1978`) already shipped in #396 / #511 / #587 but were reachable only via direct IPC file writes; the MCP layer now exposes them to the agent. Unlike the `set_trusted` / `set_trigger` siblings, these three tools are registered for ALL tiers (not wrapped in `if (isMain) { ... }`) per the existing tier-gating policy at the top of `ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` — the host-side handlers have an owner-of-bill self-target carve-out, so a non-main tier can legitimately demote its own group's model to Haiku; cross-folder writes are rejected by the host's auth check. `set_task_agent_model`'s tool description carries the host-side rejection contract verbatim — cadence-registry rows (`source = 'cadence-registry'`) reject because their `agent_model` is reasserted from SKILL.md `agentModel:` frontmatter on every rebuild, so an imperative write would silently revert; only operator-scheduled rows (`source = 'schedule-task'`) accept. All three tools are added to `DestructiveScope` in `confirmation-tokens.ts` and to `classifyDestructiveOp`'s direct-map so the existing PreToolUse confirmation-token gate covers them — chains carrying untrusted-provenance markers must present a valid operator-issued token before a model/cost change is honoured, matching the protection on adjacent `set_trusted` / `set_trigger`. Input-normalisation + IPC-payload construction extracted to `agent-model-payload.ts` so the empty/whitespace → null behaviour is testable in isolation (mirrors the host-side trim-then-clear semantics so the "requested" response text never claims the bridge pinned a whitespace value while the host quietly cleared it); MCP-facing param for the task variant is `task_id` (snake_case, matches `pause_task` / `resume_task` / `cancel_task` / `update_task` siblings) and gets remapped to `taskId` (camelCase) once inside the payload builder so the host-side handler shape is unchanged. 16 new tests in `agent-model-payload.test.ts` cover the normaliser and three payload builders; 1 new test in `confirmation-tokens.test.ts` covers the destructive-classification and untrusted-chain deny / trusted-chain allow paths for the three new scopes. Closes #595.

- New `write_trusted_memory` MCP tool gives the agent an explicit operator-approved-write path into `/workspace/trusted/` that bypasses #325's memory-quarantine hook (which only intercepts `Write` / `Edit` tool names) when the operator dictates content directly in chat (#585). The tool requires an `operator_justification` parameter (≥8 chars) citing the chat turn where the operator provided the value; every successful call emits a structured `memory_quarantine.operator_approved_write` log line to stderr for forensic review. Pure validation + atomic-write logic lives in `container/agent-runner/src/write-trusted-memory.ts` (`performOperatorApprovedWrite`, exported); the MCP handler in `ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` is a thin adapter. Tier enforcement is filesystem-mount-based — `/workspace/trusted/` is mounted RW only for main + trusted containers per `src/container-runner.ts`, and the new tool is NOT listed as an allowed sink under any `capability-acl.ts` external-content prefix so chains carrying untrusted-provenance markers (web / email / cross-group / file Read) deny at the ACL layer before reaching the filesystem. The existing quarantine hook for `Write` / `Edit` is unchanged; the new tool is a sibling, not a replacement. 20 new unit tests in `write-trusted-memory.test.ts` cover the happy path, every file_path rejection (empty / relative / absolute outside trusted / traversal escape / quarantine subtree), every justification rejection (empty / whitespace-only / sub-8-char), atomic write semantics (no `.tmp` leftover after successful rename, exercised against a real-fs tmpdir round-trip), recoverable fs-error handling (`EACCES`, `ENOSPC`), unexpected-error propagation, structured log payload shape + 200-char truncation + no-log-on-failure, and the agent-facing description's load-bearing phrases. Refs #325 #318.

- Wrapper scheduled-task skills (`nightly-external-sync`, `entertainment-sync`, `soul-searching-wrapper`) now land in `task_run_logs` with the SDK's text result instead of null. Agent-runner's success-result branch carries a new `chat_displayed` boolean alongside the unchanged `result` field so the orchestrator + task-scheduler can skip their chat-echo path without collapsing the observability signal. Closes #581.

- Narrowed catch-all handlers introduced by the #584 recompute path so programming bugs surface instead of getting swallowed as a warn (`coding-policy: error-handling`). The two `tz_state` writer callbacks (`applyTripitSegmentsToTzState`, `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory`) now catch only `SqliteError` with `SQLITE_BUSY` / `SQLITE_LOCKED` (mirroring `src/index.ts`'s pattern around `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory`); every other error propagates so persistent DB faults / schema corruption / programming bugs aren't hidden behind the "tz_state write already landed" log line. The `setTaskNextRun` writer catch inside `recomputeLocalSchedules` is narrowed the same way. The compute path no longer catches at all — `computeNextRunDetailed` is documented as resilient (cron parse failures → `pause-broken-cron` remediation, invalid per-task tz → `clear-bad-timezone`, throwing resolver callbacks → TIMEZONE fallback), so any throw signals a programming bug and must propagate per the error-handling rule; the prior `tryComputeNextRunForRow` wrapper (a structural swallow that returned errors as values) and the inner `decideCatchUp` catch are both removed. The catch-up branch is gated on a non-null `nextRun` so `decideCatchUp` is not invoked on the documented broken-cron path. Unit tests in `db.test.ts` + `task-scheduler.test.ts` cover both directions: transient SQLite contention is swallowed with a warn, programming bugs (generic `TypeError`, non-transient `SqliteError` like `SQLITE_CORRUPT` / `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY`, and unexpected throws from `computeNextRunDetailed`) propagate. Addresses the `error-handling` concern from the PR #592 policy review.

- `tz_state.current_tz` writers now invalidate cached `next_run` values on `schedule_timezone='local'` rows so a zone flip mid-slot retargets the next fire against the new zone instead of the prior one (#584). The recompute path asks `cron-parser` for the PREVIOUS occurrence in the new zone via `.prev()` and, when that prev is missed-and-fireable (`last_run < prev <= now <= prev + 24h`), overrides `next_run` to `now` so the next scheduler tick fires the row immediately rather than waiting until the next future cron slot — this addresses the driving symptom from the issue (owner lands in Berlin where 7am already passed → brief fires now, not 17h later). `cron-parser.next()` always returns a strictly-future occurrence, so the missed-today slot is invisible to `.next()` alone; the previous-occurrence lookup is the only path that surfaces it. The catch-up decision lives in a new pure helper `decideCatchUp({scheduleValue, scheduleTimezone, lastRun, prevCronTz, now}) → {shouldCatchUp, prevOccurrence}` for unit-testability. `startOfTodayInTz` now resolves the UTC offset at the INTENDED midnight via a two-pass `formatToParts` lookup rather than at `now`, so a DST transition on the calendar day (spring-forward 2026-03-08 or fall-back 2026-11-01 in `America/New_York`) doesn't shift the computed midnight-UTC instant by ±1h. NaN from `startOfTodayInTz` (or a null `tz_state.current_tz`) now skips the catch-up branch entirely per the function contract, rather than treating the unusable gate as `firedTodayInNewZone=false`. The test-injection `computeNextRun` dep seam was removed — the new prev-based logic exercises the real production catch-up branch directly. Refs #456.

- Hard-exit watchdog no longer kills heavy maintenance composes mid-turn (#589). Three reinforcements on top of the #545 activity-aware budget: (1) the default `HARD_EXIT_IDLE_BUDGET_MS` rises from 30s to 90s, since Claude's thinking-then-`send_message` turn after a heavy data fetch (e.g. morning-brief's 33KB context load) routinely emits no intervening SDK event for 30+ seconds and was tripping the watchdog mid-compose; the SIGKILL deploy-kill cascade (#249/#250) still bounds genuinely-stuck cases at the host hard timeout, so 60s extra grace is cheap. (2) Per-skill override via SKILL.md `drain_timeout_ms` frontmatter: heavy skills (morning-brief, soul-searching, entertainment-sync) can declare a longer budget without bumping the global default. Resolved once at runQuery start from the prompt's `Skill(skill: "...")` invocation against `/home/node/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`; missing/malformed values fall back, values above `DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` (10 min) are rejected to stay well below Node's `setTimeout` Int32 cliff, and the skill name is regex-gated (`^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*$`) plus path-prefix-checked against `skillsDir` so a prompt-smuggled `Skill(skill: "../...")` can't drive the resolver out of the mount. (3) In-flight `tool_use` re-arms unconditionally: between a `tool_use` SDK event and its matching `tool_result` the agent is provably alive (the SDK is blocking on a tool), so the idle gap is not real silence — a Bash that legitimately runs 60s+ no longer trips the watchdog. New `parseSkillNameFromPrompt`, `parseDrainTimeoutMsFromFrontmatter`, `resolveDrainTimeoutMs`, `DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` exports in `container/agent-runner/src/hard-exit-watchdog.ts`; `decideHardExitWatchdog` grows a `pendingToolCallCount` parameter. The frontmatter parser strips unquoted inline `# ...` comments to match the host-side cadence-registry's semantics (so `drain_timeout_ms: 180000 # 3 minutes` parses correctly); `resolveDrainTimeoutMs` narrows its filesystem catch to `ENOENT`/`ENOTDIR` per `coding-policy: error-handling` and lets other errors (permission failures, malformed paths) propagate. 31 new unit tests in `hard-exit-watchdog.test.ts` (37 total, 6 pre-existing) cover the three new behaviours plus the fall-back paths (no skill call, skill not installed, missing frontmatter, malformed override, missing skills dir, inline-comment-annotated value, quoted-with-hash sentinel, value above the cap, path-traversal in skill name, slashed / dot-prefixed / hyphen-prefixed skill names, canonical `tessl__<name>` shape, EACCES propagation). Closes #589.

- Per-task `AGENT_MODEL` override for scheduled tasks (#509 Phase 3). Adds `scheduled_tasks.agent_model` plus the `set_task_agent_model` IPC handler; resolution ladder, writer-scope reasoning, audit-doc cross-link, and test inventory live in PR #587's body and `docs/maintenance-cost-audit-2026-05-14.md`. Refs #509.

- The agent-runner's SDK-result-error path now writes a structured `subtype=… | is_error=… | terminal_reason=… | stop_reason=… | summary=…` string into `task_run_logs.error` instead of the legacy `${subtype}: ${summary}` form. The legacy form collapsed to useless `"success: completed"` strings when the SDK returned the contradictory `subtype: 'success' + is_error: true` shape that fires on maintenance-container inactivity timeouts (observed on the 2026-05-15 heartbeat false-success runs that motivated #582 — the only diagnostic surface that survives a maintenance-container exit is `task_run_logs.error`, and the legacy form had stripped the actionable fields out of it). New 9-case unit suite in `format-error-result.test.ts` covers the contradictory-shape preservation, the 500-char cap that holds the IPC marker JSON within the safe-size budget from the #149 review, the summary-source fallback order (`terminal_reason` → `errors[0]` → `textResult` → `subtype`), and the defensive `String()` coercion on `terminal_reason` so a non-string SDK payload can't throw before `writeOutput(...)` and lose the DB `error` diagnostic entirely. Closes #582.

- The orchestrator-injected `<context>` tag at the top of every agent prompt now carries `utc_datetime`, `local_datetime`, `local_date`, `weekday`, `timezone`, `timezone_source`, and (when the cascade resolves via a fresh-location pin) `location_lat` / `location_lng` / `location_age_minutes` — replacing the pre-#576 single-attribute `<context timezone="..." />` shape that the agent had to mentally re-derive a local frame from on every turn (#576). Driving symptom on 2026-05-16: the agent called a Friday `hack-a-team` event "сегодняшний" (today's) for a user who had crossed into Saturday in their local frame — the calendar payload was fresh in context but the date framing was loose because the agent had no resolved local-date anchor to ground "today" on. The enriched tag now names that anchor explicitly. `timezone_source` is a four-value union (`location` for a fresh shared-location pin <4h old, `segment` when the walker hit a covering TripIt segment, `home_fallback` when the walker returned `home_tz`, `container_default` when no resolver run was possible because neither location nor segments were available) so the agent — and the paired `local-context-anchoring` rule in `jbaruch/nanoclaw-trusted#42` — can express uncertainty on the `container_default` path rather than confidently misanchoring. The walker's segment-vs-home distinction is reconstructed by re-running `walkTzSegments` and comparing to `home_tz` (the resolver itself doesn't expose its branch); the corner case of "user genuinely in home tz with a covering segment" labels as `home_fallback` but the operational outcome is identical and `local-context-anchoring` doesn't branch on that distinction anyway. `location_*` attrs are populated ONLY when `timezone_source === 'location'` so the agent never reads stale coords for "here" framing — stale-pin paths surface `timezone_source: segment` / `home_fallback` and the agent should say it doesn't know `here`. New module `src/agent-context.ts` owns the pure builder + formatter: `buildAgentContext(input)` takes `{now, homeTimezone, containerTimezone, latestLocation, segments}` and returns the `AgentContext` shape; `formatAgentContextTag(ctx)` renders the self-closing `<context ... />` tag; the convenience `buildAgentContextFromDb({ownerSenderId, containerTimezone})` wraps both with a `readTzStateForContext()` + `getLatestLocationForSender(ownerSenderId)` pair so callers don't reach into SQLite directly. The new `readTzStateForContext` in `src/db.ts` is a pure read (no writes, no warning emissions — those side effects belong to `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory`, not the per-prompt context builder); it mirrors `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory`'s schema-version gate so an unfamiliar `tz_state.schema_version` returns null and the builder falls through to `container_default`. `local_datetime` is built via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `timeZoneName: 'longOffset'` so the emitted ISO string parses cleanly with `new Date(...)`; UTC's "GMT" output normalizes to `Z` per ISO-8601 rather than `+00:00`. `formatMessages` in `src/router.ts` now accepts `string | MessageFormatContext` for backward compatibility: the existing 2-arg `formatMessages(messages, timezone)` form still works (emits the legacy single-attribute tag, used by the `session-commands.ts` /compact pre-prompt path and by every existing test); the new `formatMessages(messages, {timezone, contextTag})` form carries the pre-built enriched tag and is what `src/index.ts`'s two main agent-invocation paths use (the pre-spawn formatter at the end of `processGroupMessages` and the pipe-into-running-container formatter inside `startMessageLoop`). The session-commands /compact pre-prompt path continues on the legacy 2-arg shape — its `formatMessages` dep call site bypasses the enriched tag — because the pre-compact context is a separate slice that doesn't drive the user-facing "today / here" framing the rule cares about; promoting it is a small follow-up. Tests: 15 new in `src/agent-context.test.ts` covering the source mapping (container_default fallback, fresh-location → `location` with coord exposure, fresh pin in a different tz produces that tz, stale + covering segment → `segment`, stale + no covering segment → `home_fallback`, no-location + covering segment → `segment`), the datetime fields (`utc_datetime` is ISO-8601-Z with no millis, `local_datetime` carries the offset, `local_date` differs from UTC date across midnight, weekday matches local frame, UTC normalizes to `Z`), and `formatAgentContextTag` (renders all required attrs, includes `location_*` only when `source=location`, escapes XML special chars in tz / weekday values); all 83 existing `formatMessages` tests in `src/formatting.test.ts` pass unchanged (the 2-arg form is preserved bit-for-bit). 3178 total tests still green. Adds zero new runtime dependencies. Paired with `jbaruch/nanoclaw-trusted#42` which adds the always-apply `local-context-anchoring` rule directing the agent to actually use these enriched attrs for relative-time / relative-place phrasing — the rule and this enrichment must ship together for the symptom to be addressed end-to-end.

- `walkTzSegments` no longer ships the next foreign trip's tz when the user has landed home between trips. Production incident on 2026-05-16 11:53Z: Baruch flew back to Nashville (Geecon return segment ended 2026-05-16T05:00Z) and was sitting at home until the next departure (Stockholm, 2026-05-17T22:10Z, ~41h later). The walker returned `Europe/Berlin` per the #571 arrival-tz rule and the morning brief rendered events with a +7h offset — the Saturday 07:30 CT Ensworth meet showed as 14:30. The #571 rule was correct for the mid-flight case (gap between two FOREIGN stays = mid-transit toward the next stay) but over-corrected for the home-bounded case (gap between an inbound home segment and the next departure = sitting at home). The walker now classifies the gap by the MOST RECENT prev-ended segment's tz: if it equals `home_tz`, the user is home → return `home_tz`; otherwise the user is in transit → return the arrival (first-future) segment's tz (#571 behaviour preserved). Detection relies on the same lodging-primary chronological-order invariant the existing arrival branch uses — `nextSegTz` is first-wins, `prevEndedTz` is last-wins (= most recent under chronological iteration). The symmetric case (foreign trip ending into a future home segment that hasn't started yet) needs no new branch: `nextSegTz` IS `home_tz` there, so the existing arrival-tz fallback returns the right answer naturally; a new pinning test covers that path against future refactors. The #574 Phase 2 location-first resolver (just merged in #578) still calls `walkTzSegments` as the stale-location / no-location / coord-lookup-failed fallback, so this fix lands the right answer for every cascade branch that depends on the walker — including the common case where the user hasn't shared location recently because they're at home and the morning brief is reading TripIt-only signal. 3 new walker tests in `src/db.test.ts` cover the home-bounded gap: the live 2026-05-16 mixed-shape repro (Krakow date-only past, Nashville datetime past = home, Stockholm datetime future), a date-only-only equivalent, and the symmetric foreign-→-future-home pin. The existing #571 tests (gap between two foreign zones, live 2026-05-13 mid-flight Atlanta→Krakow) still pass unchanged. Closes #573.

- Host-side TZ resolver consumes the locations table — `tz_state.current_tz` now reflects the owner's actual coords when fresh, falling through to the TripIt-segment walker otherwise (#574 Phase 2). Phase 3 (#577) wired location capture into a `locations` table but nothing read from it yet; the 30-min heartbeat advisory still walked TripIt segments only, exactly the inference-from-itinerary stack that produced the #571 / #572 / #573 mid-flight bugs. Phase 2 plugs the locations data into the advisory so the resolver answers "where is the owner" via the freshest measurement first, with the walker as a tested fallback for stale / missing location data. **No upstream change to `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync` was needed** — my earlier "needs IATA airport codes" framing was a mistake. The walker (`walkTzSegments`) already returns the correct IANA tz string for every case, including mid-flight (the #572 fix returns the arrival segment's `timezone` directly). For TZ resolution alone, the walker's tz string IS the answer; the IATA→coords lookup I had proposed only mattered if some other feature wanted destination coords for non-TZ purposes (e.g. "what restaurants near my arrival airport"), which is not Phase 2's job. Cascade in `src/tz-resolver.ts::resolveCurrentTz` (pure function): `age < 4h` → `tz_from_coords(location)` via the new `geo-tz`-backed `src/tz-from-coords.ts` wrapper (drops to walker on out-of-range / data-gap coords with source `walker_coord_lookup_failed` so the operator can investigate without reading warn logs); `4h ≤ age < 12h` → walker (mid-flight returns arrival tz per #572, covering segment returns segment tz, else `home_tz`) with source `walker_stale_location`; `age ≥ 12h` → same walker fallback PLUS `warning: 'stale_no_share'` so the orchestrator can nag the owner to re-share. `STALE_FOR_TZ_FALLBACK_HOURS = 4` (TripIt cascade trigger) and `STALE_WARNING_HOURS = 12` (operator-visible nag) are named constants per the discussion on #574. Picked `geo-tz` (~900 kB, actively maintained, 100% accuracy via timezone-boundary-builder shapefiles) over `tz-lookup` (~72 kB, ~10% miss rate on inhabited points per the maintainer's own README): a 10% miss rate on the owner's own city would be a visible regression vs the TripIt-walker baseline, and server-side install size is irrelevant against correctness here. `geo-tz` may return multiple zones at literal tz-boundary coords (rare in practice for phone-shared locations with sub-meter accuracy); we pick the first entry (`geo-tz` orders multi-results deterministically). State migration `state-015` adds a `last_stale_warning_at TEXT NULL` column to `tz_state` and bumps `schema_version` 3 → 4 (lock-step with `SUPPORTED_TZ_STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `src/db.ts` per `coding-policy: stateful-artifacts`); the cooldown is enforced inside `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory` so a 30-min cadence advisor can't double-fire the nag inside the 12 h window — the cooldown stamp is updated atomically with the DB fire decision so a concurrent advisory can't see a half-applied state. The stamp is reset to NULL the next time location is fresh again so the NEXT stale window gets one notification at its start, not silently never. `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory` now returns `{ flip, warningToFire }` instead of `null | { prev, next }` — callers in `src/index.ts`'s 30-min `setInterval` updated to consume both fields; the chat-send path forks into two independent best-effort sends (tz flip → `📍 Timezone changed: X → Y`, stale nag → `⚠️ I haven't seen your location in 12 h+…`). New `ASSISTANT_OWNER_TG_USER_ID` env var (sibling to the existing `ASSISTANT_OWNER_HANDLE` username field — numeric IDs are forever-stable, usernames change any time the owner edits their Telegram profile) tells the advisory who counts as "the owner". Unset means the location-first cascade is skipped entirely and the advisory falls back to walker-only behaviour, preserving the pre-Phase-2 contract bit-for-bit for zero-config installs. The malformed-segments JSON path was tightened in the same change: pre-Phase-2 a `JSON.parse` failure returned `null` and the whole advisory tick was a no-op; now the resolver still gets a fresh-location-first answer when one exists, and the walker fallback degrades gracefully to `home_tz` when the segments cache is broken (recovers on next `sync_tripit`). Tests: 7 new in `src/tz-resolver.test.ts` (fresh-pin happy path including just-under-4h boundary; exactly-at-boundary kicks into walker; walker_no_location; walker_stale_location with covering segment; walker_coord_lookup_failed on out-of-range coords; stale-warning surface at 12h boundary; no-location-ever doesn't fire warning), 11 new in `src/tz-from-coords.test.ts` (five global cities round-trip correctly; non-finite + out-of-range coords reject with null; WGS-84 edge coords are accepted), 6 new in `src/db.test.ts`'s new `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory — location-first cascade (#574 Phase 2)` describe block (fresh location flips tz to geo-tz answer, stale location falls through to walker, ≥12 h fires nag with cooldown stamp, cooldown active suppresses, fresh location clears cooldown stamp, no-owner-id path preserves walker-only behaviour); plus updates to 11 existing `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory` tests for the new return shape, and 1 update to `task-tz-state-json-migration.test.ts`'s schema_version assertion (3 → 4 in lock-step with the gate bump). The `_seedTzStateForTests` helper gained an optional `lastStaleWarningAt` arg so cooldown-state tests can construct arbitrary prior-fire timestamps. Adds one new runtime dependency: `geo-tz`. Closes Phase 2 of #574.

- Telegram bridge captures live-location updates (`edited_message:location`) into a new `locations` table; Phase 1's bare static-pin capture is no longer the whole picture (#574 Phase 3). Phase 1 shipped the initial-share path (one row in `messages` per `message:location` / `message:venue`) but the dominant use case is **continuous** live-location during travel — the user starts a single Telegram live share, and Telegram fires `edited_message:location` updates against the same `message_id` for the entire `live_period` (up to 8h). The Phase 1 PR didn't subscribe to that filter at all, so every position tick after the initial share was silently dropped, leaving the host-side TZ resolver stuck on the share-start coords even as the user crossed continents. Phase 3 adds the `edited_message:location` handler plus a separate `locations` table so the live-update stream doesn't pollute `messages` (live-location ticks aren't conversational signal — flooding chat history with `[Location 36.0,-86.9]` lines per minute is exactly what the dedicated table exists to avoid). The `locations` table carries `(chat_jid, sender, message_id, latitude, longitude, accuracy_m, source, recorded_at, live_period)`; `source` discriminates the four shapes Telegram delivers (`static`, `venue`, `live_initial`, `live_update`); the `(sender, recorded_at DESC)` composite index satisfies the Phase 2 resolver's "most-recent owner coords" hot query without a table scan. No FK to `chats` — locations are auxiliary telemetry that should survive a since-unregistered group's row deletion. Static pins and venues continue to land in BOTH tables (one `messages` row for the chat-history placeholder, one `locations` row for the resolver); live-location updates land in `locations` only. The handler reads `edit_date` (the tick time) rather than `date` (the original share time the Bot API echoes on every edit) as `recorded_at` — using `date` would freeze recorded_at and break the freshness gate in the Phase 2 resolver. New module-level helpers in `src/db.ts`: `storeLocation(record)` for INSERT and `getLatestLocationForSender(sender)` for the canonical resolver query — returns `null` when the sender has no rows so the Phase 2 cascade can fall through to TripIt deterministically. New types in `src/types.ts`: `LocationSource` (union of the four `source` literals) and `LocationRecord` (the storage / callback shape); new `OnLocation` callback added to `ChannelOpts` as optional (channels without location surfaces — Slack today — omit it). `src/index.ts` wires `onLocation: storeLocation` into the shared `channelOpts` block alongside `onMessage` / `onChatMetadata`. Defensive: unregistered-chat guard mirrors the existing `messages` write path — location telemetry from random chats the bot was added to without registration doesn't leak into the DB. Tests: 5 new outcome tests in `src/channels/telegram.test.ts` (static pin emits `source: 'static'` with `live_period: null`; `live_period > 0` reclassifies the initial share as `source: 'live_initial'`; unregistered chat doesn't trigger `onLocation`; `edited_message:location` appends `live_update` without rewriting `messages`, with `recorded_at` derived from `edit_date`; venue emits `source: 'venue'` with the title + coords) plus 4 storage-layer tests in `src/db.test.ts` (round-trip, missing-sender returns null, most-recent-across-chats wins, sender filter rejects other group members' shares). Test harness adds `createEditedLocationCtx` + `triggerEditedLocation` helpers; `createTestOpts` now mocks `onLocation`. Phase 2 (resolver) consumes this data per the cascade documented on the issue: `age<4h → tz_from_coords(latest_loc)`; `4h≤age<12h → tripit.activeFlightSegment(now) ? tz_from_coords(arrival_airport) : walker.resolve(now)`; `age≥12h → emit STALE_WARNING via main-group Telegram message + same fallback`. The walker's existing arrival-segment-tz behaviour (#572) is correct as the mid-flight fallback when stale coords + active TripIt segment exist; the upstream `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync` will need to add `from_airport` / `to_airport` IATA codes to its emitted segment shape so the Phase 2 resolver can do the IATA→coords lookup against an embedded airport dataset rather than parsing the segment's `label` field. Phase 3 makes Phase 1 actually useful for the TZ use case; Phase 1 alone captured a single moment in time and was useless for a traveling user. Closes Phase 3 of #574.

- Telegram bridge captures `latitude` / `longitude` from `message:location` and `message:venue` events inline in the placeholder text (#574 Phase 1). Pre-#574, `src/channels/telegram.ts:1609` stored a bare `[Location]` placeholder via `storeNonText` and discarded the lat/lng payload Telegram delivered; `message:venue` had no handler wired at all, so venues fell through every existing filter and stored nothing. The discarded payload is the input the host-side TZ stack needs to know where the user actually is — the entire `tz_state.segments` / `walkTzSegments` machinery (`src/db.ts`) exists to *guess* the user's location from TripIt itinerary because the bridge wasn't surfacing the ground-truth location-pin payload. That inference has been wrong enough times to drive #571 (gap fallback to `home_tz` mid-flight), #572 (fix returns arrival-segment tz in any gap), and the newly-filed #573 (over-corrected for home-bounded gaps). Capturing the location payload is Phase 1 of the replacement; Phase 2 will add a `tz_from_coords` resolver that reads the most-recent coords row and reverse-geocodes to an IANA tz, falling through to the walker only when no recent location is on file. Format is regex-parseable so the resolver can extract coords without a schema migration: `[Location <lat>,<lng>]` and `[Venue "<title>" <lat>,<lng>]`. Lat/lng are emitted with grammY's full Telegram precision (up to 6 decimal places, ~10 cm); the resolver downsamples as needed. Venue title is escaped — embedded `"` becomes `\"` and embedded newlines collapse to a single space — so the regex contract stays unambiguous when titles carry quotes or line breaks. Defensive: when `ctx.message.location` is missing on a `message:location` update (shouldn't happen per grammY's typing, but a malformed update mustn't crash the bridge), the handler emits the legacy bare `[Location]` placeholder and continues; the venue handler has a parallel fallback to bare `[Venue]`. Privacy mode caveat per the owner's verification on #574: this works only when the bot is either a group admin (Telegram auto-disables privacy for admins) OR globally privacy-off via BotFather `/setprivacy → Disable`; positive control on 2026-05-16T12:10:42Z in `tg:-1003859124897` confirmed the main supergroup satisfies one of those conditions. Per-chat privacy/admin verification for the long tail of registered groups is left for the Phase 2 PR. Tests: `src/channels/telegram.test.ts` adds 4 cases (`stores location with inline lat/lng coordinates`, `falls back to bare [Location] when location payload is missing`, `stores venue with title + coordinates`, `escapes quotes and strips newlines in venue title`) and grows the connect-time filter-registration assertion to include `message:venue`. Phase 2 (resolver + walker replacement) and Phase 3 (live-location `edited_message:location` support via UPDATE-by-message_id flow) are tracked as separate PRs against the same issue. Closes Phase 1 of #574.

- `walkTzSegments` no longer falls back to `home_tz` when the heartbeat fires in the gap between two booked segments. The new behaviour returns the arrival (next) segment's tz, keeping the morning brief / scheduler in the destination zone the user is heading into. Production incident on 2026-05-13 11:24Z (Geecon trip): Atlanta segment ended 02:40Z, Krakow segment starts 13:00Z. At 11:24Z the user was mid-flight (over the Atlantic), but the walker returned `home_tz = America/Chicago` and fired a "Timezone changed" notification on a TZ the user wasn't actually in — physically impossible and never actionable. The walker now classifies each non-covering segment as "ended before now" or "starts after now"; when BOTH classes appear in the walk, the agent is mid-transit and the first future segment's tz is returned. `home_tz` only fires before the first segment or after the last. The chronological-order invariant (lodging-primary first) lets the classifier track presence + first-future-tz without cross-shape datetime-vs-date comparisons.
