Degenerate segments (`from === to` / `from_dt === to_dt`) skip BEFORE classification so a malformed row can't seed the gap fallback with a nonsensical zone. 4 walker tests cover the new behaviour: the existing inter-segment-gap case flipped from `home_tz` to the arrival tz, the live 2026-05-13 mid-flight repro, an after-last-segment guard (single segment past, no future → still `home_tz`), and two degenerate-segment guards (datetime + date-only paths). Closes #571.

- Stage 1 trigger gate's keyword matcher now uses a Unicode-aware trailing boundary (`(?![\p{L}\p{N}_])` + `u` flag) instead of `\b`, so non-ASCII keywords actually match. JavaScript `\b` only treats `[A-Za-z0-9_]` as word characters — Cyrillic letters are non-word by that definition, so `ботики` followed by a space had no `\b` transition and the regex silently failed. Live 2026-05-12 observation in `Old.wtf - чат с ботами-занудами` (`tg:-1003869886477`): the chat's `registered_groups.trigger_pattern` JSON has `ботики`/`боты`/`агентики` as owner-set `keyword` entries, but Stage 1 returned `deny: no trigger pattern matched` on every one — Stage 2 Haiku caught it as a collective vocative and rescued the message, but every miss paid the Haiku tax instead of short-circuiting at Stage 1. Same defect exists for any Hebrew / Arabic / CJK keyword in any group's `trigger_pattern` JSON. Fix mirrors PR #406 (which applied the same `\p{L}` + `u` flag swap to `src/channels/telegram-sanitize.ts`) into `buildKeywordRegex` in `src/gates/trigger.ts:55-57` and the lock-step mirror `buildTriggerPattern` in `src/config.ts:368-370` — the lock-step comment at `src/gates/trigger.ts:49-54` calls out that any divergence between the two surfaces as a behaviour change for the legacy `TRIGGER_PATTERN` path in `src/channels/telegram.ts:1321`. 3 new tests in `src/gates/trigger.test.ts` cover the incident shape (Cyrillic keyword at start of message, Cyrillic keyword after whitespace, Cyrillic keyword does NOT match inside a longer Cyrillic word — substring-rejection semantics that the existing `@andy` vs `@andybot` test guards for ASCII); 40 existing keyword + boundary tests still pass unchanged. Closes #566.

- Forward `GITHUB_TOKEN` into main/trusted-tier containers via the existing 0600-mode `--env-file` plumbing so the `gh` CLI inside the container authenticates automatically. Driven by the cost-monitor dashboard skill family (`precheck-gating-monitor`, `session-cap-monitor`, `daily-spend-rollup`) shipped in `nanoclaw-admin@0.1.332` — those skills' SKILL.md bodies issue `gh issue list/edit/comment` against the `Precheck-gating dashboard` / `Session-cap dashboard` / `Daily spend dashboard` issues in `jbaruch/nanoclaw` on each fire; routing through Composio's MCP GitHub catalog instead would pay ~5-15K cache_create tokens per cold maintenance spawn × ~1800 fires/year, pure overhead for work `gh` does without any tool-schema cost. `GITHUB_TOKEN` joins `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` / `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY` / `COMPOSIO_USER_ID` in `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` (`src/container-runner.ts:465-499`) and `CONTAINER_VARS` (`src/container-runner.ts:2640-2668`), gated on the existing `isMain || isTrusted` tier filter so untrusted-tier containers don't receive it. `container/Dockerfile` installs `gh` from the official `cli.github.com/packages` apt repo (not in `debian:slim` default sources) — keyring under `/usr/share/keyrings/githubcli-archive-keyring.gpg`, repo source list, second `apt-get update` + install, all in the same RUN layer as the existing system deps to avoid an extra image layer. The CLI reads `GITHUB_TOKEN` automatically; no in-container `gh auth login` needed. Tests grow one case in `src/secret-env-file.test.ts` (`SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS lists GITHUB_TOKEN`) covering the addition without touching the existing 17. **Operator action required at deploy:** the existing `GITHUB_TOKEN` PAT in `~/nanoclaw/.env` is scoped `Contents: write` on `jbaruch/nanoclaw` only (for the host-side `github_backup` IPC handler's git push). The new container-side use requires expanding scope to ALSO include `Issues: write` and `Pull requests: write` on the same repo — the cost-monitor dashboards won't update otherwise. **Security note:** this PR keeps the secret in the container's `environ` for the spawn lifetime (same pattern as the Composio keys); the OneCLI proxy migration to move all credential injection orchestrator-side is tracked separately in #564. `.env.example` documents the dual-use-site contract and the OneCLI migration target.

- `tz_state` walker now resolves at per-segment ISO-datetime granularity instead of UTC-midnight string compare on date-only fields. Production incident on 2026-05-11 19:26 CT (00:26 UTC 2026-05-12): `tz_state.current_tz` flipped `America/Chicago` → `America/New_York` the moment UTC rolled over to the calendar day of the next morning's BNA→ATL flight; traveler was still physically in TN. Root cause was bilayer — the upstream `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync/lib/tripit.mjs::formatDate` flattened every Date through `toISOString().slice(0, 10)` before the segment left the parser, AND the host's `walkTzSegments` in `src/db.ts` compared with `now.toISOString().slice(0, 10)` against those date-only strings. Both layers had to lift to sub-day resolution. Upstream fix in https://github.com/jbaruch/reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync/pull/13 adds `from_dt` / `to_dt` (ISO 8601 UTC) to every emitted segment alongside the unchanged `from` / `to`. Host fix here: new state-013 migration bumps `tz_state.schema_version` 2 → 3 (no DDL — the new fields live inside the existing TEXT JSON column); `walkTzSegments` prefers `from_dt` / `to_dt` per segment when both are present, falls back to date-only `from` / `to` otherwise; `SUPPORTED_TZ_STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` advances in lock-step. Per-segment (not array-wide) fallback handles the mixed-shape transient between a host deploy and the next `sync_tripit` run — an older row cached on disk keeps working until the new tile version rewrites it. New `TripitSegment` interface fields are typed optional. 6 walker tests cover the incident's exact shape (00:30 UTC same-day no-match, +14:00 UTC same-day match, strict-equal-end edge, legacy date-only fallback, partial-shape defense, mixed-array per-segment resolution); 5 migration tests cover idempotency, conservative `WHERE schema_version = 2` guard, and chained v1 → v2 → v3 via state-012's `WHERE id = 1` path. 14 existing `_seedTzStateForTests({schemaVersion: 2})` call sites updated to 3 in lock-step with the gate; two unfamiliar-version tests advanced 3 → 4 so they keep exercising the rejection branch. Closes jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#229.

- `github_backup` pipeline now mirrors every group dir (and `groups/global/`) into the backup-repo rather than just two single files from `sourceGroup`. Pre-#557 `src/backup-sync.ts`'s `SINGLE_FILE_NAMES` was `['MEMORY.md', 'daily_discoveries.md']` plus a `memory/` mirror plus per-table SQL dumps — everything else under `groups/` was excluded. Result: `groups/global/SOUL.md` in the backup-repo on the deployed NAS was from 2026-04-04 (over a month stale) even though the nightly fire ran cleanly every day; `groups/<group>/blog-notes.md`, `cfp-state.json`, and the rest of agent-mutable per-group state were never copied at all. The new sync walks `groups/global/` → `backup-repo/global/` and every non-hidden `groups/<name>/` → `backup-repo/groups/<name>/` with delete-on-missing semantics, applies a denylist for content reproducible from deploy or `tessl install` (`backup-repo`, `.tessl`, `.claude`, `node_modules`, `dist`, `logs`, `tmp`, `conversations`, `.checkpoints`, `.archive*` directories; `*.lock`, `.tmp-*`, `scripts.new.*`, `scripts.version.*`, `*.bak[-.]20YY*`, `*.migrated-20YY*` files) at any depth, and sweeps orphans under the managed `global/` and `groups/` prefixes so deleted source files leave the backup tree too. The SQLite state-table dumps to `backup-repo/state/` carry over unchanged. `conversations/` (agent pre-compaction transcript archive, ~810 MB and growing on the swarm group) is in the denylist by design: large, append-only, agent can regenerate reasoning on demand; keeping it out caps backup-repo growth. First-run legacy-path cleanup removes the pre-#557 layout's root-level `MEMORY.md`, `daily_discoveries.md`, `blog-notes.md`, `memory/`, `skills/`, `trusted/`, `trusted-staging/` so the new namespaced layout (`groups/<group>/...`) doesn't leave stale duplicates at the root; idempotent on subsequent runs. IPC handler in `src/ipc.ts:3474-3530` now passes `groupsRoot: GROUPS_DIR` instead of a single `groupDir`, and the `isMain` authorization gate is unchanged. Rewritten `src/backup-sync.test.ts` covers 16 cases: global mirror, multi-group mirror, stale overwrite, orphan sweep under managed prefixes, recursion guard (backup-repo not mirrored into itself), denylist enforcement at any depth for each dir/file pattern, hidden top-level group skip, denied-content-in-dest sweep (denylist gates copies but dest scan disables denylist so previously-mirrored offenders get removed), legacy-path first-run cleanup + idempotency, nested subdirectory preservation, dump-plan integration, and actionable error messages on missing groupsRoot/backupDir. RAID 5 on the NAS protects against single-drive failure but not against accidental deletion, filesystem corruption, ransomware, or multi-drive failure during rebuild — the git-backup is what catches those cases, and the policy gap above meant SOUL.md and most per-group state weren't actually being caught. Closes #557.

- Conditional rule loading via `requires:` frontmatter on tile rule files (#552). Pre-#552 every rule in every installed tile was unconditionally concatenated into the per-group `/workspace/group/.tessl/RULES.md` — the audit on `telegram_old-wtf` measured 932 lines / 28 rules / ~12-15K tokens per turn on the trusted-tier baseline, of which roughly half is task-specific guidance that only matters when a specific skill is loaded. The new mechanism adds an optional `requires: [skill-a, skill-b]` frontmatter field on rule .md files — when present, the rule loads iff at least one listed skill is in the spawn's effective skill set (any-of semantics); when absent the rule loads unconditionally (current default, every existing rule's behavior preserved). New pure module `src/rule-requires-filter.ts` provides `parseRequiresFrontmatter(content)` (supports inline lists `[a, b]`, single bare values, and block-list `  - a\n  - b\n` form; quote-tolerant; malformed frontmatter returns `null` = "no constraint, load unconditionally" so a broken rule never silently disappears from a spawn) and `shouldIncludeRule(content, presentSkills)` (returns a tagged result with `reason: 'no-requires' | 'gating-skill-present' | 'no-gating-skill' | 'empty-requires'` so the install loop's structured log surfaces WHY a rule was filtered). Distinguishes `requires:` absent (load unconditionally) from `requires: []` present-but-empty (author explicitly parked the rule — never loads) — distinct semantics, distinct filter reasons. `src/skill-dep-closure.ts` grows a new `computeEffectiveSkillContext(blocklist, sources)` that returns BOTH `effectiveBlocklist` and `reachableSkills` (the positive presence set — every skill whose prompt actually loads into the agent's context for this spawn, including #544b's transitively-exempted ones); existing `computeEffectiveBlocklist` becomes a thin back-compat wrapper. The container-runner's pre-scan helper (`computeEffectiveSkillBlocklistForSpawn` → renamed `computeEffectiveSkillContextForSpawn`) now walks the same three skill source dirs as before (tile skills, built-in skills, AyeAye-staged skills) and returns the context shape. The rule-assembly loop at `src/container-runner.ts:1862-1888` reads each rule's frontmatter, applies `shouldIncludeRule` against the reachable set, and pushes filtered names into the existing `filteredRules` array — the single `install_blocklist_filtered` log line at the end of the install loop now aggregates both #337-maintenance-blocklist filters and #552-requires-filter filters with a `(requires: <list>)` suffix on the latter so operators can debug the gating decision. The pre-scan runs unconditionally now (previously skipped on default-session spawns since `null` blocklist meant nothing to filter) so the `requires:` filter has a reachable set to consult on every spawn; the cost is one BFS over the skill graph per `buildVolumeMounts` call, in practice <5ms even with 40+ skills installed. Cross-tile gating works (a rule in tile A can require a skill in tile B), built-in gating works (a rule can require `wiki` / `agent-browser` / `status`), and the #544b closure-rescued case works (a rule requiring a maintenance-blocklisted skill rescued via `Skill()` reference from a loaded root still loads, because reachable includes both roots and transitive exemptions). Adds 21 cases in `src/rule-requires-filter.test.ts` covering the parser (no-frontmatter / no-requires / inline / bare / block / empty / quoted / kebab+snake / unterminated / CRLF / nested-mapping non-match / block-list termination) and the decision function (any-of, all-absent, empty-of-purpose, absent skill set), 5 cases in `src/skill-dep-closure.test.ts` covering the new context-returning form (reachable = non-blocklisted roots when no references; closure rescues fill reachable; blocked-only chains stay out of reachable; default-class fast path; empty sources), and 10 cases in `src/container-runner.requires-filter.test.ts` exercising the install loop end-to-end (regression guard for non-#552 rules, present/absent gating, any-of, same-tile and cross-tile gating skill resolution, built-in skill resolution, maintenance-class skill blocklisted filters the requiring rule, #544 closure rescues the requiring rule too, `requires: []` parking). Foundational PR — no rule .md files in this tile carry `requires:` yet; subsequent PRs against `nanoclaw-core` / `nanoclaw-trusted` / `nanoclaw-admin` (steps 2 and 3 of #552) introduce per-rule declarations. Closes part 1 of #552.

- `groups/global/SOUL.md` repaths the persona-file references on lines 54-56 from `/workspace/extra/blogs/persona/` to `/workspace/trusted/persona/`. The old path resolved to nothing usable: `container/Dockerfile:58` creates an empty `/workspace/extra` (mkdir only, no host bind ever attaches), and no mount populates any deeper subpath, so the deeper `/workspace/extra/blogs/persona/` referenced by SOUL.md was an unrooted directory regardless of tier. The actual persona files live in host `trusted/persona/`, bind-mounted at `/workspace/trusted` (RW for both main and trusted tiers, gated on `isMain || trusted` in `src/container-runner.ts:1539-1558`). The broken refs only affected the trusted/main bots — those are the only tiers that load `groups/global/SOUL.md` at all; untrusted containers consume `SOUL-untrusted.md` via the file-overlay bind at `src/container-runner.ts:1482-1488` (the bind binds the host file `SOUL-untrusted.md` AS the container path `/workspace/global/SOUL.md`, so `@-include` resolution in `CLAUDE-untrusted.md` reads the sanitized content) and `SOUL-untrusted.md` never carried the broken paths. So pre-fix: trusted/main agents fell back to whatever persona detail SOUL.md inlined, and the detailed voice profile / bio schema / canonical-blog tone reference were dead refs. Same change also surfaces `product.md` (Tessl product positioning index) which already existed on disk but wasn't referenced anywhere in SOUL.md — trusted/main bot couldn't pull Tessl-specific context when discussing AI agents, spec-driven dev, or skill registries. Untrusted-side Tessl context is a separate concern living in `SOUL-untrusted.md` (runtime-patched on 2026-05-11). Reference incident: 2026-05-11 — the `telegram_old-wtf-pod` untrusted bot replied without Tessl context, which traced through #554 (the file-overlay mount design that misled a parallel diagnosis, closed as misdiagnosis) to this orphaned-paths bug in the trusted SOUL. Closes #555.

- Maintenance skill blocklist now exempts skills reachable via `Skill(skill: "...")` references from a loaded skill. Pre-#544b a non-blocklisted skill A whose SKILL.md said `Skill(skill: "tessl__B")` couldn't actually invoke B if B was blocklisted — the agent loaded A's prompt, tried to invoke B, and the SDK failed with `Unknown skill: B`. Reference incident: 2026-05-10 `wiki-lint` biweekly fire failed with `Unknown skill: wiki` because `wiki` (built-in) was in `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST` and `wiki-lint` (not blocklisted) invokes it via `Skill(skill: "wiki")`. New `src/skill-dep-closure.ts` adds two pure helpers: `extractSkillDeps(skillMdContent)` runs a fixed regex (`/Skill\(\s*skill:\s*["'](?:tessl__)?([a-zA-Z0-9_-]+)["']\s*\)/g`) over a SKILL.md to pull every `Skill()` invocation target — the skill-authoring rule mandates typed calls (no prose references), which makes this statically extractable; and `computeEffectiveBlocklist(originalBlocklist, skillSources)` BFS-walks the reference graph from every non-blocklisted skill (the "roots") and returns a fresh blocklist with every reachable skill removed. The transitive closure handles A → B → C chains: if A is a root and references B, and B references C, both B and C are exempted. New `computeEffectiveSkillBlocklistForSpawn` helper in `src/container-runner.ts` pre-scans the same three skill source dirs the install loop walks (tile skills under `<registryTiles>/<tileName>/skills/`, built-in skills under `<cwd>/container/skills/`, AyeAye-staged skills under `<groupDir>/skills/`) into a `Map<skillName, SKILL.md content>`, then runs the closure once before the install loop fires; the loop's three filter sites (tile / builtin / staging) all consult the EFFECTIVE blocklist instead of `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST` directly. Pre-scan failure modes follow `coding-policy: error-handling`: ENOENT on a missing source dir or a skill subdir without a SKILL.md is best-effort skip (tiles legitimately ship without skills, groups without staging); every other errno (EACCES, EIO, ENOTDIR, fs corruption) PROPAGATES so a perms / IO failure on the skill source dirs fails the spawn loudly rather than silently shipping a degraded blocklist that could reintroduce the runtime "Unknown skill" failure the closure exists to prevent. Default-session spawns skip the closure entirely (no blocklist to adjust). Adds 17 cases in `src/skill-dep-closure.test.ts` covering the regex (single/double quotes, prefix vs. bare, deduplication, whitespace tolerance, hyphen/underscore in names, empty / no-match) and the closure (direct rescue, transitive A→B→C rescue, cycle termination, mixed graph with both reachable and isolated blocklisted skills, blocked-only chain stays blocked when no live root reaches it, unknown-name reference tolerance, empty-sources fast path, no-mutation guarantee), plus 2 cases in `src/container-runner.maintenance-blocklist.test.ts` exercising the install loop end-to-end against a fixtured `wiki-lint` → `wiki` reference (rescued) and a separate `orphan-blocked` skill (stays blocked when nothing references it). Together with #544a (unconditional script copy) this closes both failure modes documented in #544. Closes #544.

- Maintenance skill blocklist no longer accidentally suppresses host-side scripts. Pre-#544 the filter loop in `src/container-runner.ts:1771-1789` skipped THREE filesystem operations on a blocklisted skill: (1) the agent-context tile copy at `dstTileDir/skills/<skillDir>` (intended — keeps the SKILL.md prompt out of the agent's loaded context to save tokens); (2) the agent's `Skill()` lookup copy at `skillsDst/tessl__<skillDir>` (intended for the same reason); (3) `copyTileScriptsToFlatDir` to `tmpScriptsDir`, which is the per-group `scripts/` flat dir host MCP handlers consume (UNINTENDED — scripts don't load into the SDK prompt surface, so excluding them costs zero context but breaks any host operation that looks them up by filename). The third side-effect surfaced today (2026-05-10) when `entertainment-sync`'s Sunday 15:00 UTC fire (first ever after `last_run = NULL`) failed at Step 1 with `trakt-watch-history.py not found` — `entertainment-sync` calls `mcp__nanoclaw__fetch_trakt_history()`, whose handler in `src/ipc.ts:3316-3331` reads `groups/<sourceGroup>/scripts/trakt-watch-history.py` and got ENOENT because `trakt-watch-history` is in `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST`. The fix moves the `copyTileScriptsToFlatDir` call ABOVE the blocklist check so scripts publish unconditionally; only the two prompt-context copies remain gated on the blocklist. The blocklist's load-bearing purpose (token savings on maintenance container context) is preserved exactly. Adds 1 case in `src/container-runner.maintenance-blocklist.test.ts` that fixtures a blocklisted `skill-block` with `scripts/blocked-helper.py`, verifies the script lands at `groups/<folder>/scripts/blocked-helper.py` post-publish, AND round-trips the file content so a partial-write regression still trips. Closes the entertainment-sync class of #544; the wiki-lint class (a non-blocklisted skill invokes a blocklisted skill via `Skill()`) is a separate concern tracked in a follow-up PR.

- Activity-aware hard-exit watchdog in `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts`. Pre-#545 the watchdog (added in #461) fired a flat 30s `setTimeout` from `_close` detection — intended to recover from a stuck SDK iterator (open keepalive, lingering Promise, pending tool_result that'll never arrive). It also killed legitimately-working scheduled tasks: for one-shot scheduled spawns there's no follow-up IPC message expected, so `shouldClose()` flips true very early in the run, and the 30s post-close budget became the entire compose-to-send window. Result on 2026-05-10: morning-brief composed for ~93s after close, was hard-killed before the final `send_message`, and was finally delivered at 15:30 UTC via heartbeat-driven Phase C recovery — 3.5h late. `grep -l 'Hard-exit watchdog' /workspace/host-logs/containers/telegram_swarm/maintenance/2026-05-*.log` showed 10+ matches in early May; the empty-result morning-brief shape (`task_run_logs.duration_ms` 60–300s, `result` empty) had been recurring for the full week. Post-#545 the budget measures IDLENESS (time since last SDK event) rather than wall-clock from close. Every event in the `for await (const message of query(...))` loop stamps `lastSdkActivityAt = Date.now()` (assistant turns, tool_use, tool_result, system/init, result events — anything proves the iterator is alive); the watchdog timer fires at the budget boundary and consults a pure decision helper (`decideHardExitWatchdog` in `container/agent-runner/src/hard-exit-watchdog.ts`) that returns either `{ action: 'exit' }` when `idleMs >= budget` or `{ action: 'rearm', rearmInMs: budget - idleMs }` otherwise. Re-arm uses the remaining budget rather than scheduling another full 30s, so the wall-clock-to-kill bound stays at "30s of continuous silence within the budget window", not "never on a chatty agent". Boundary semantics (`>=` for exit, never re-arm at 0ms) prevent the tight CPU loop a strict-`>` check would create when the timer fires exactly on the budget edge. The natural exit path still runs first when healthy — `process.exit(0)` synchronously terminates, so the timer's callback only fires when nothing has come out of the SDK for the full budget. Adds 6 cases in `hard-exit-watchdog.test.ts` (stuck SDK exits at budget, idleMs > budget still exits, working-agent re-arm computes correct `rearmInMs`, zero-idle re-arm uses full budget, explicit budget override for testability, boundary `idleMs == budget` exits). Closes #545.

- Persist `sync_tripit` segments onto the singleton `tz_state` row and wire a 30-min heartbeat advisory walker. Today `tz_state.current_tz` is written by the LLM-side `task-tz-sync` skill, which derives TZ from a parallel pipeline (Flighty Google Calendar via `composio-fetch` + `travel-schedule.json` fallback). That pipeline disagreed with the host-side `sync_tripit` (the actual canonical source) on 2026-05-09 and produced jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#224. The fix routes everything through one source. New `state-012-tz-state-segments.ts` migration adds `segments TEXT` to `tz_state` (JSON-stringified `[{timezone, from, to, label}]` from `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync`'s `sync.mjs --output=json`; `from`/`to` are `YYYY-MM-DD` strings produced upstream by `lib/tripit.mjs::formatDate`, so lex compare === date compare for the segment walk) and bumps the existing row's `schema_version` 1 → 2. New `applyTripitSegmentsToTzState` helper in `src/db.ts` walks the segments timeline (match rule `from <= today < to`, fallback to `home_tz` for gaps, first-match-wins on overlaps, skip empty-timezone rows), UPSERTs `current_tz` + `segments` + `schema_version=2`, and audit-logs the prev→next flip on the structured logger. Wired into `src/ipc.ts`'s `sync_tripit` success path: after the script-result file is parsed, the helper runs against the freshly-fetched timeline so the host's `tz_state` row is coherent on the same wall-clock tick as the user's response. New 30-min `setInterval` in `src/index.ts` calls `runTzHeartbeatAdvisory` to re-walk the cached segments without re-fetching iCal — a `from`/`to` boundary crossing flips `current_tz` mid-day and the orchestrator notifies the main group via its channel (`📍 Timezone changed: prev → new`, matches the circuit-breaker notify pattern). Reader gate `SUPPORTED_TZ_STATE_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `src/db.ts:1707` advances 1 → 2 in lock-step with the migration. The two existing UPSERT call sites against `tz_state` (`_seedTzStateForTests` and `migrateTaskTzStateJsonFiles`) are extended to write the new gate value explicitly so a fresh-DB JSON-import lands at the writer's known shape rather than the schema's `DEFAULT 1` (which the reader gate would reject). Adds 5 cases in `src/state-migrations/state-012-tz-state-segments.test.ts` (column shape, singleton CHECK preserved, mid-migration row bump, no-op on fresh DB, JSON round-trip) and 23 cases in `src/db.test.ts` covering the pure walker, the writer helper (gap days, exact-`to` boundary, missing-row early exit, scheduler_tz preservation), and the heartbeat advisory walker (silent skips, malformed-JSON tolerance, flip semantics). Sequencing: this host PR merges first; after deploy, `tessl_update` pulls the new orchestrator and `sync_tripit` starts populating `tz_state.segments`. Then the tile-side cleanup (jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#223) lands, removing `task-tz-sync`'s Flighty primary path and the `composio-fetch` Flighty merge — inverse order would freeze `tz_state.current_tz` because the old writer would be gone before the new one ships. During the transient (host shipped, tile not yet), both writers update `current_tz`; the heartbeat advisory walker corrects within ≤30 min so any wrong value from the legacy derivation converges fast. Closes #542.

- Pre-resume artifact pruning sweep on persisted SDK sessions — both default-session (user-facing DM resumes) and maintenance-session (recurring scheduled-task resumes). Implementation in `src/session-artifact-retention.ts` walks the session's `<sessionId>.jsonl` transcript and prunes two classes of heavy artifacts that otherwise re-inflate context on cold resumes: (1) inline base64 image blocks — keep most-recent N (default `SESSION_INLINE_IMAGE_KEEP_RECENT=2`), replace older `{type: 'image', source: {type: 'base64', data: ...}}` blocks with text stubs; bottom-up walk so "recent" is genuinely the last N. (2) Large old `tool-results/*.txt` side-file references — files larger than `SESSION_TOOL_RESULT_MAX_BYTES` (default 64 KiB / 65536) AND older than `SESSION_TOOL_RESULT_MIN_AGE_MS` (default 6h / 21600000) get their transcript references rewritten to text stubs, then the side-file is unlinked AFTER the transcript rewrite succeeds. Atomic via tempfile-then-rename: `<transcript>.tmp.<pid>` → `renameSync`; failure to unlink the side-file is best-effort (transcript already stubbed; failed unlink is a disk-space concern, not a correctness issue) and never fails container spawn. Security guards verbatim from source: `resolveToolResultRef` constrains resolved paths under `<projectDir>/tool-results/`; `lstat` (not `stat`) + `isSymbolicLink()` rejects symlinks even pointing into the allowed directory. Wired into both spawn paths: `src/index.ts` runs the sweep AFTER the existing `spawnStart`/`wasNukedDuringSpawn` capture so nuke detection covers the retention sweep too (sweep is local file I/O <100ms but a nuke landing in that window must still be detected); `src/task-scheduler.ts` runs it before `runContainerAgent` for maintenance task fires that resume a persisted `task.session_id`. Operator knobs in `.env.example`: `SESSION_ARTIFACT_RETENTION` to disable globally with any of `0`/`false`/`no`/`off`/empty, plus the three threshold knobs above. Logs at INFO with structured retention counts (`imageBlocksReplaced` / `toolResultRefsReplaced` / `toolResultFilesDeleted`) only when something was actually pruned. Port of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#193` (squash `7511cf43`, merged 2026-05-09 20:23Z) with two adjustments: issue references `#187/#189` (source-fork tracking) → `#538` (this fork's tracking issue) in `.env.example`; and `src/task-scheduler.ts` resolution kept this fork's direct `const output = await runContainerAgent(...)` form rather than picking up the source fork's pre-existing watchdog refactor (`containerPromise` + `Promise.race` against a watchdog timeout) which is NOT part of PR #193's diff (verified +38/-1 on that file in the source PR). The retention block + `DATA_DIR` import + `pruneSessionArtifacts`/`resolveSessionArtifactRetentionConfig` imports were taken verbatim. Closes #538.

- Credential-proxy now upgrades SDK-emitted ephemeral prompt-cache breakpoints to the 1h tier on outbound `/v1/messages` for a tightly-scoped set of requests: `tier === 'main'` AND `session === 'default'` AND no `task_id` (user-facing turns only) AND group ∈ `NANOCLAW_PROMPT_CACHE_1H_GROUPS` (default `['telegram_swarm']`). Implementation in `src/prompt-cache-ttl.ts` mutates `cache_control: {type: 'ephemeral'}` blocks in `system`, `tools`, and `messages[].content` to add `ttl: '1h'`; deliberately does NOT invent new breakpoints, only extends what the SDK already emitted, so prefix boundaries stay byte-for-byte aligned with the SDK. Wired into `src/credential-proxy.ts` immediately after the wire-tool filter so the forwarded body, `DUMP_API_REQUESTS` captures, and usage accounting all agree on what was sent. JSON parse failures forward the body unchanged and log a single WARN per affected request. Operator knobs in `.env.example`: `NANOCLAW_PROMPT_CACHE_1H` to disable globally with any of `0`/`false`/`no`/`off`/empty, and `NANOCLAW_PROMPT_CACHE_1H_GROUPS` (comma-separated, or `*` for all main default-session groups) to override the default allowlist. Cost projection from #537's gap analysis on the `telegram_swarm` group (446 user-message gaps over 13 days): 59% of inter-message gaps are <5m (already cached at 5m TTL), 31% land in the 5m–1h "value zone" the 1h TTL captures, 11% are ≥1h (no help). Order of magnitude ~$0.40/day per main DM at a ~10k-token cached prefix on Sonnet 4.6 — modest but real, and complements the pending #416 cache-control breakpoint split (this extends the TTL of breakpoints the SDK already emits, #416 splits the prefix that gets cached). Port of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#191` (squash `e7ffa49`, merged 2026-05-09) with two adjustments: default-group constant `['telegram_main']` → `['telegram_swarm']` to match this fork's main group folder, and issue references `#110` (source-fork tracking) → `#537` (this fork's tracking issue). Closes #537.

- Main-group CLAUDE.md mount source resolves to `groups/main/CLAUDE.md` instead of `groups/<registered-folder>/CLAUDE.md`. The previous code in `src/container-runner.ts:1321` hardcoded the per-group folder path on the assumption that main's CLAUDE.md was "always on disk, git-managed" — but that's only true for the literal `groups/main/` folder, which is the git-tracked template. Any registered main group whose folder name is something else (e.g. `groups/telegram_swarm/`) is gitignored runtime state with no automatic bootstrap, so the file was missing and `container-runner.ts:1360-1368` fired its "trust-tier source missing" WARN on every spawn until an operator hand-copied the template. The resolution change makes main symmetric with trusted/untrusted — all three trust tiers now point at a git-tracked template that is always on disk regardless of the registered folder name. Trusted resolves to `groups/global/CLAUDE.md`, untrusted to `groups/global/CLAUDE-untrusted.md`, and main now to `groups/main/CLAUDE.md`. The placeholder-creation step at `container-runner.ts:1342-1347` (which touches an empty CLAUDE.md on the host so runc can overlay the bind mount onto a read-only parent) stays gated to `!isMain && !trusted` because both main and trusted have a RW parent mount and runc creates the target itself; no change there. The companion edit in `scripts/migrate-thin-claude-md.ts` adds the SHA-256 of the current `groups/main/CLAUDE.md` (276 bytes, hash `27ad547724b9efbd1df4d9056ba2fea63f78ee4593d056b035ca9d332b1bb4b1`) to `KNOWN_VANILLA_TEMPLATE_HASHES` so the migration deletes any hand-copied bootstrap files (e.g. the one placed at `~/nanoclaw/groups/telegram_swarm/CLAUDE.md` as a quick fix on 2026-05-09 to silence the WARN before this PR landed) — those copies are now redundant with the bind mount and would otherwise drift. Reference incident: 2026-05-09 — AyeAye surfaced the recurring "CLAUDE.md trust-tier source missing" WARN on the swarm group; investigation found the missing per-group file, the wrong fix in the WARN itself ("Run `git pull` and `scripts/migrate-thin-claude-md.ts --apply`" doesn't create missing files, only cleans existing ones), and this resolver gap as the root cause. Adds 3 cases in `src/container-runner.security.test.ts` covering: source resolves to `groups/main/CLAUDE.md` for a main group with a non-`main` folder name, mount is present (no WARN) when the registered folder lacks its own CLAUDE.md, and the CLAUDE.md mount is positioned after the group folder mount so the file shadow takes effect (parity with trusted).

- `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST` adds the four round-2 candidates after the same deeper audit method PR #533 used: `check-calendar`, `check-email`, `check-system-health`, `trusted-memory`. The audit covered `SKILL.md` + `references/` + `scripts/` across the full maintenance skill tree, plus the swarm group's `CLAUDE.md` / `SOUL.md` / `MEMORY.md`, plus `scheduled_tasks.prompt` against the live `store/messages.db` — zero `Skill()` invocations from any cadence-registered maintenance skill, zero references in group instructions, zero operator-scheduled task prompts mentioning the names. The only matches across all surfaces are two doc-comment lines in `tessl__heartbeat/scripts/heartbeat-precheck.py` listing `check-email` and `trusted-memory` as other writers to a shared state file (race-safety follow-up note, not active invocations). The "use as part of heartbeat or standalone" wording in the standalone `check-email` / `check-calendar` SKILL.md descriptions is aspirational — heartbeat does the work inline today via `references/email-triage.md` and the SKILL.md's own Calendar section. If a future refactor converges heartbeat onto the standalone skills, this blocklist line is the visible artifact that has to be touched in the same PR. Closes the round-2 work that #531 attempted prematurely and #532 walked back. List grows from 20 to 24 skills; rule list (9) unchanged. Documentation-only; operator sync on next NAS deploy activates the additions.

- `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST` shrinks from 28 to 20 skills — eight broken entries removed after a deeper audit (`SKILL.md` + `references/` + `scripts/` across the full maintenance skill tree, the surfaces #337's pre-deploy validation missed) found cadence-registered maintenance skills actively invoking blocklisted skills via `Skill()` and silently no-op-ing because the skill files were absent from the maintenance container per `container-runner.ts:1763`. The eight removed: `audible-backup`, `check-cfps`, `check-orders`, `check-travel-bookings`, `check-watchlist`, `recommend-books`, `recommend-shows`, `soul-searching`. The calling fires: `entertainment-sync` invokes `audible-backup` / `check-watchlist` / `recommend-books` / `recommend-shows` (cadence Sundays — 0 runs since round-1 deploy on 2026-04-30, bug latent); `nightly-external-sync` invokes `check-cfps` / `check-orders` / `check-travel-bookings` (cadence daily 08:45 UTC — 4 runs since deploy, durations 158s/4s/60s/56s, the 4s run on 2026-05-06 a silent no-op); `check-watchlist`'s own daily cadence at 09:30 UTC (4 runs, 63s/27s/4s/4s — the two ~4s runs silent no-ops, three of four with empty `result` columns); `soul-searching-wrapper` invokes `soul-searching` (cadence Sundays — 0 runs, latent). All marked `success` in `task_run_logs` because no exception was thrown — the agent receives the prompt `Skill(skill: "tessl__<blocklisted>")`, the SDK can't resolve the missing skill, the agent ends the turn empty, the orchestrator records the run as `success`. The `HEARTBEAT-CRASH:` marker #337's deploy-and-watch pattern relies on is heartbeat-specific; failures in `entertainment-sync` / `nightly-external-sync` / `check-watchlist` / `soul-searching-wrapper` don't surface there, which is why #349's "Win — tighten" verdict on 2026-05-07 missed the bug entirely (heartbeat's own metric was healthy because heartbeat doesn't invoke any of the eight). Round-1 final shape: 9 rules + 20 skills. Documentation-only on the codebase side (`.env.example` is operator-read, not runtime-consumed); production behavior depends on operator-side `.env` sync on the next NAS deploy. Per #532's deeper-audit method (now the policy for any future round-N tighten).

- `.env.example` now documents `MAINTENANCE_RULE_BLOCKLIST` and `MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST` (#337's per-task-class slim-prompt vars) — closing a documentation gap where the deployed `.env` carried 9 rules + 28 skills' worth of blocklist content but nothing in the repo told a fresh maintainer the vars existed, what their format was, or how to validate after a change. Round-2 tightening was attempted in PR #531 (added `check-calendar`, `check-email`, `check-system-health`, `trusted-memory` to the skill list per #349's "Win — tighten" verdict) and pulled back out same-day in this fix — the audit method underpinning #531's selection covered SKILL.md files and `scheduled_tasks` prompts but missed two surfaces that turned out to matter: skill `references/` subdirectories (heartbeat does email triage via `references/email-triage.md` inline and calendar work in its own SKILL.md Calendar section, neither delegating to the standalone skills via `Skill()`) and skill `scripts/` directories (heartbeat-precheck.py mentions both `tessl__check-email` and `tessl__trusted-memory` in a doc-comment about cross-writer race-safety on session-state.json — not active invocations, but a signal the grep should have surfaced). `check-email` and `check-calendar` additionally have a documented design tension with `sanitizer-helper.md` calling out the inline-vs-delegate duplication as a "reasonable follow-up to converge"; adding them to the blocklist would commit to the duplication permanently and would break heartbeat the moment the convergence refactor lands. Round-1's 9 rules + 28 skills stay as the deployed blocklist (already validated by 7+ days of `HEARTBEAT-CRASH:`-free running per #349). Re-attempting round-2 requires (a) a deeper audit covering SKILL.md + `references/` + `scripts/` + group `CLAUDE.md`, (b) a decision on heartbeat's email/calendar inline-vs-delegate design, (c) the deploy-and-watch validation per #337 over a few days. Documentation-only on the codebase side: `.env.example` is read by operators, not by `readEnvFile` at runtime, so neither #531 nor this fix has changed production behavior — the round-1 deployed state stays unchanged.

- `task_run_logs.task_id → scheduled_tasks(id)` FK switches to `ON DELETE CASCADE` so deleting a parent task atomically clears its run-log children. Without the cascade, `rebuildCadenceRegistry`'s orphan DELETE (`src/cadence-registry.ts`) FK-aborts the entire per-spawn rebuild transaction whenever a previously-declared cadence-registry skill is uninstalled but its `task_run_logs` rows survive — the spawn never happens, the message cursor rolls back, retry loops until the per-group circuit breaker trips at 5 consecutive failures and pauses the group for 30 minutes. The same latent bug applies to `unscheduleTask` via the schedule-task IPC path; the `deleteTask` writer at `src/db.ts:1475` already worked around it by deleting children first, but the cadence-registry orphan path didn't (and shouldn't have to). Reference incident: 2026-05-08 — three retired verify-fix watchdog skills (`tessl__verify-fix-{451,493,497}`, the operator follow-up the PR #527 removal entry deferred) had been cancelled as cadence-declaring skills but their `cadence-registry::telegram_swarm::tessl__verify-fix-*` `scheduled_tasks` rows still held 115 surviving `task_run_logs` children (1 + 16 + 98). Every Telegram Swarm message after that point hit `SqliteError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` from inside `rebuildCadenceRegistry`'s orphan DELETE; the swarm's circuit breaker tripped and AyeAye stopped replying for the duration of the cooldown. SQLite cannot ALTER an existing FK in place, so the migration uses the standard 12-step table rebuild: detect via `pragma_foreign_key_list('task_run_logs')` whether `on_delete = 'CASCADE'`; if not, flip `foreign_keys = OFF`, `BEGIN`, `CREATE TABLE task_run_logs_new` with the cascade-bearing FK, `INSERT INTO task_run_logs_new SELECT … FROM task_run_logs` to preserve every row verbatim, `DROP TABLE task_run_logs`, `ALTER TABLE task_run_logs_new RENAME TO task_run_logs`, recreate `idx_task_run_logs`, `COMMIT`, then flip `foreign_keys = ON`. The migration is idempotent — a second pass detects CASCADE and no-ops. Adds 3 cases in `src/db.test.ts`: a fresh-init DB declares the cascade FK, parent-DELETE on a fresh DB cascades to two child run-log rows without the manual `deleteTask` workaround, and a hand-built legacy-shape DB with the pre-fix `NO ACTION` FK upgrades in place when `createSchema` runs — preserving the legacy row's `task_id` / `duration_ms` / `status` literally and confirming a second pass remains a no-op. Adds two `@internal` test helpers (`_runCreateSchemaForTests`, `_execRawForTests`) so the legacy-DB construction path and write-side raw SQL can be exercised without round-tripping through `_initTestDatabase`. Operator no longer needs to remember to clear `task_run_logs` children before retiring a cadence-registered skill; the FK cleans up after itself.

- Remove `scripts/verify-fixes/` entirely (`verify-493-precheck.py`, `verify-451-precheck.py`, `verify-497-precheck.py`, `README.md`) — added then removed before release; never shipped to a tagged version. The watchdog framing introduced in PR #521 and reshaped in PR #523 was the wrong shape from day one and the [Unreleased] entries below for those PRs should be read as superseded by this removal (the `usage.jsonl` mount work in #523 / #524 lives on and is correct as documented). Both shipped prechecks carried correctness bugs that drove pointless production wakes: `verify-493` counted inbound on every non-main group instead of mirroring `resolveGatesForGroup`'s eligibility filter (`stage2Enabled === true AND requiresTrigger !== true` — the TypeScript field names; the SQL column is `requires_trigger`), so a single classifier-ineligible group with traffic (`telegram_old-wtf-pod` with `requiresTrigger=true, stage2Enabled=false`) made the precheck wake every cycle; `verify-451` built `IPC_ROOT / main_folder / "tasks"` (`/workspace/ipc/telegram_swarm/tasks/`) but the trusted/main mount in `src/container-runner.ts:2319-2323` already binds the per-group dir directly at `/workspace/ipc`, so requests landed at the double-nested host path `~/nanoclaw/data/ipc/telegram_swarm/telegram_swarm/tasks/` where the orchestrator's IPC watcher never looks — every run timed out with `no_result_file_within_10s`. Together those false positives kept `system_health_issues` permanently dirty, which made the heartbeat-precheck always see a wake reason and burn ~$9.80/day waking Sonnet to read `{wake_agent: true, reason: ...}` and forward it to chat — paging dressed as agency. Even with the bugs fixed, the structural problem remained: scheduled-task LLM-wakers cost a Sonnet invocation per check for monitoring concerns that want CI tests; synthetic single-shot pings test the happy path under perfect conditions, not real failure modes. The intent each watchdog claimed to cover is already enforced at CI time by integration tests written alongside the original fixes: `src/gates/haiku-classifier.test.ts:169-219` ("emits a usage.jsonl record for every API call (issue #493)") asserts the `appendUsageRecord` call fires with `tier:'classifier'` shape; `src/ipc-auth.test.ts:2982-3158` covers `list_learned_triggers` with five cases (main no-filter, main with filter, non-main implicit-self scope, cross-folder denial, empty-learned); `src/shutdown-checkpoints.test.ts` pins the SIGTERM checkpoint write path. The watchdogs were redundant with these from the moment they were written. Operator follow-up (separate, not in this PR): cancel the three live `scheduled_tasks` rows (`cadence-registry::telegram_swarm::tessl__verify-fix-{451,493,497}`) via `mcp__nanoclaw__cancel_task` and retire the `tessl__verify-fix-{451,493,497}` tiles through their own tile-repo PRs.

- New regression-guard test in `src/container-runner.test.ts` would have caught PR #523's docker bind-mount conflict at PR-time instead of post-deploy. The check is structural: walk all `-v` arg entries from a real `runContainerAgent` spawn (admin / trusted / untrusted), parse them into `{hostPath, containerPath, readonly}` triples, and assert no `containerPath` is nested inside an RO-mounted parent's `containerPath` — that's the docker failure mode (the kernel refuses to create a bind target on a read-only filesystem; OCI runtime returns code 125; container fails to spawn). RW-parent + nested mount is fine — kernel creates the bind target on the writable filesystem (that's how `/workspace/group/CLAUDE.md` mounts inside `/workspace/group/`), and the detector specifically does not flag those. Tests cover (1) admin spawn with `usage.jsonl` present (the exact PR #523 scenario, now passing against the fixed `proxy-logs` path), (2) admin spawn without `usage.jsonl` (mount skipped), (3) trusted-non-main spawn, (4) untrusted spawn, (5) self-test that the parser + detector correctly catch the pre-#524 broken arg shape (silent regression of the protection itself), and (6) self-test that nesting inside an RW parent is accepted. Verified the test fails with the exact pre-fix bug — temporarily reintroducing `'/workspace/host-logs/usage.jsonl'` produces failure message `"/workspace/host-logs/usage.jsonl is nested inside RO mount /workspace/host-logs"` pointing directly at the conflict pair. All 2965 tests pass (2959 prior + 6 new).
- The `usage.jsonl` mount added in PR #523 moves from `/workspace/host-logs/usage.jsonl` to `/workspace/proxy-logs/usage.jsonl` (sibling, not child) to fix a docker bind-mount conflict that broke every main-group spawn. PR #523 mounted the single file INSIDE the existing `/workspace/host-logs/` directory mount, which is itself RO from `data/host-logs/`. Docker can't bind-mount a file inside a parent that's already RO-mounted — the kernel refuses to create the bind target on a read-only filesystem and the OCI runtime returns code 125. Reference incident: 2026-05-07 PR #523 deployed the file mount and broke every `telegram_swarm` spawn for ~5 hours until the per-group circuit breaker tripped at 5 consecutive failures (`Container exited with code 125: ... read-only file system: unknown`). Morning-brief, heartbeat, and inbound replies all stopped. Non-main groups (telegram_old-wtf, telegram_dedy-bukhtyat, etc.) were unaffected because the mount block is gated on `isMain`. The new sibling path keeps both mounts as independent top-level binds. Updates 2 test assertions in `src/container-runner.test.ts` to assert the new path. Updates `scripts/verify-fixes/verify-493-precheck.py`'s `USAGE_LOG` constant + the README path table to reference the new mount point. Admin-tile copy of the same precheck script needs the same path update in a follow-up `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin` PR. All 2959 tests pass.
- The regression watchdogs from PR #521 are reshaped from host-side bash + crontab to AyeAye-native precheck Python scripts wired through `mcp__nanoclaw__schedule_task` (so they live in `scheduled_tasks` alongside every other recurring job and respect the same precheck-gating contract). Three things changed: (1) `src/container-runner.ts` now mounts `~/nanoclaw/logs/usage.jsonl` read-only at `/workspace/host-logs/usage.jsonl` for admin-tile (main) containers when the file exists — the proxy-side spend log was unreachable from inside agent containers, which is why PR #521 fell back to host cron in the first place. The mount is conditional and the precheck tolerates a missing file. (2) `scripts/verify-fixes/verify-{493,497,451}-precheck.py` replace the `.sh` cron scripts: each emits the standard `{"wake_agent": true|false, "data": {...}}` JSON payload as the last line of stdout, always exit 0 (the outer-boundary contract per `error-handling`'s narrow exception so a parser failure never silently disables the watchdog), and report regression details directly to the LLM via the `WAKE_DATA` env var instead of a separate Telegram bot. 493 reads `usage.jsonl` over the last 60 min and `messages.db` for non-main inbound traffic; 497 reads `orchestrator.log` for the `Pre-shutdown checkpoint pass complete` line within 24 h of the most recent `deploy-kills.log` entry; 451 fires the `list_learned_triggers` IPC end-to-end against the main group's tasks/ inbox and validates response shape with a 10 s deadline, cleaning up its own request/result files. (3) `scripts/verify-fixes/README.md` rewritten with the new install (three `schedule_task` payloads to fire from the main group post-deploy) and explains why host cron didn't work — Synology cron requires root for `crontab -` install and the binary on this NAS isn't SUID, so PR #521's crontab entry was unreachable to the orchestrator owner. Adds 2 cases in `src/container-runner.test.ts` pinning the new mount: appears when `usage.jsonl` exists, skipped when it doesn't. The existing host-bash watchdog files are removed entirely — operator owns one place (`mcp__nanoclaw__list_tasks`) for all scheduled work, no two-place footgun. All 2954 tests pass (2944 prior + 2 new mount tests + 8 from earlier learner tests rebased onto this branch).
- Three regression watchdogs in `scripts/verify-fixes/` cover the fixes shipped in PRs #518 (#493 host-side Haiku classifier emit), #519 (#497 SIGTERM checkpoint), and #520 (#451 promote/reenable/delete/list IPC). Each runs as a host cron, reads NAS paths directly, and posts a Telegram alert via the same bot/chat the existing `heartbeat-external.sh` uses (so all regression alerts land in one channel). `verify-493-classifier-emit.sh` (hourly) compares the count of `tier:'classifier'` records in `logs/usage.jsonl` over the last 60 minutes against the count of non-main inbound messages from `store/messages.db`; alerts when the message count is non-zero but the classifier count is zero — the regression mode where either the haiku-classifier gate stops firing or the #493 emit hook silently regresses. `verify-497-shutdown-checkpoint.sh` (every 10 min) reads the most recent entry from `data/host-logs/deploy-kills.log` and verifies that at least one `groups/*/.checkpoints/default.md` has both an mtime within ±120 s of the kill (10 s drain + buffer) AND the load-bearing `**Trigger:** shutdown` marker the new render path emits — alerts when a kill happened in the last 24 h with no matching checkpoint, since that means either the SIGTERM path skipped `writeShutdownCheckpoints` or the trigger marker regressed. `verify-451-list-learned-triggers.sh` (daily) fires a `list_learned_triggers` IPC against the main group via the orchestrator's tasks/ inbox, polls for the result file with a 10 s deadline, and validates the response shape — alerts on missing result, error response, or missing `groups` array. Test cleanup is automatic so disk usage stays bounded. The `README.md` documents the install crontab plus the rationale for using host cron rather than an in-container `mcp__nanoclaw__schedule_task` payload (the simpler shape) — `logs/usage.jsonl` isn't bind-mounted into agent containers and adding the mount is its own scope. Retirement plan mirrors #492's structural-diag pattern: after a few clean weeks, file a follow-up issue and remove. Smoke-tested live on the NAS post-deploy 2026-05-07: 493 reported `classifier=14 inbound_non_main=4` (healthy), 497 reported `latest kill was 190363s ago, outside 24h window` (no false alarm), 451 reported `list_learned_triggers responded with valid shape` after a one-line shape-check fix that handles JSON-in-JSON escaping (`\"groups\"` and `"groups"` both match).
- Three new IPC handlers close the missing UX surface for #415's trigger-pattern learner — `promote_learned_trigger`, `reenable_learned_trigger`, `delete_learned_trigger`, and the read-side `list_learned_triggers` (covers #451 items 1, 2, 3; item 4 — producer-side enrichment of `haiku classifier verdict` with `messageId` + `inboundText` — touches `src/gates/haiku-classifier.ts` plus the host-log-parser contract and is its own scope per the issue body, deferred to a follow-up). The trigger-pattern learner (`src/gates/trigger-learner.ts`) writes proposals as `source: 'learned'` + `enabled: false`; the trigger gate (`src/gates/trigger.ts`) skips `enabled: false` rows so proposals are inert until promoted. Auto-rollback flips `disabled: true` when precision drops. Until now the only way to manage these states was hand-editing `registered_groups.trigger_pattern` JSON — error-prone, undocumented, no audit trail. **Promotion** flips `enabled: false → true` after the operator reviews a proposal; rejects already-disabled rows (operator must re-enable separately) and is idempotent on already-enabled rows. **Re-enable** flips `disabled: true → false` on auto-rolled-back proposals after the operator fixes the root cause (e.g. removed a noisy synthetic-identity nickname); idempotent on currently-active rows. **Delete** removes a learned proposal entirely from the array — for proposals whose precision is irrecoverable; the learner can re-propose the same body later as a fresh row with `pattern_version: 1`. **List** is read-side observability: returns the learned-source patterns with their full optional-field set (precision, sample_count, last_matched_at, last_updated_at, pattern_version, proposed_at, enabled, disabled) so a dashboard or status command can render them without parsing JSON columns directly. All four use the same `{groupFolder, kind, pattern}` identity the learner schema uses to supersede prior versions (see `src/gates/trigger-learner-schema.md`). Authorization mirrors `set_agent_model` (#395) — owner-of-bill semantics: main can target any group, non-main can target only its own folder. List honours the same scope: non-main with no `groupFolder` filter is implicitly scoped to the source folder, and an explicit cross-folder request is denied with a typed error. Mutating handlers refresh `available_groups.json` via the existing snapshot machinery; list writes its result to `_script_result_<requestId>.json` per the existing `scriptResultPath` convention (same as `list_installed_tiles`, `inspect_gate_decisions`). Adds 22 cases across `src/ipc-auth.test.ts`: 9 for promote (main + non-main auth, cross-folder rejection, unknown-pattern rejection, owner-set-not-promoted, disabled-pattern rejection, idempotent already-enabled no-op, missing-groupFolder rejection, no-learned-patterns case), 4 for reenable (flip success, non-main auth split, idempotent already-active no-op, unknown-pattern rejection), 4 for delete (success preserves siblings, refuses owner-set entries with same body, unknown-pattern rejection, cross-folder rejection), 5 for list (main no-filter returns all groups, main with filter returns one, non-main implicit-self scope, non-main cross-folder denied, empty learned list for owner-set-only groups). All 2944 tests pass (2922 prior + 22 new).
- Composio's REST and MCP gateways maintain independent key namespaces — verified empirically 2026-05-06 against `backend.composio.dev` (REST, header `x-api-key`, accepts project-scoped `ak_*` keys) and `connect.composio.dev/mcp` (MCP, header `x-consumer-api-key`, accepts consumer-scoped `ck_*` keys). The legacy single-`COMPOSIO_API_KEY` shape (introduced in #107) used the same env var for both surfaces; once Composio's REST gateway started rejecting `ck_*` keys with `Invalid API key` 401s, the inline-fetch path in `tessl__composio-fetch`'s precheck broke while MCP-mediated tools kept working — exactly the operator surface a single env var can't represent. Splits the wiring so each surface gets its own correctly-scoped secret: `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` stays on the REST surface (now expected to hold the `ak_*` project key the backend wants for `x-api-key`), and a new `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY` carries the consumer-scoped `ck_*` key the agent runner sends as `x-consumer-api-key` to the MCP gateway. Both forwarded into agent containers via `CONTAINER_VARS` in `src/container-runner.ts` (gated to main + trusted tiers, untrusted groups still receive nothing) and routed through the secret env-file (mode 0600) per `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` so neither value ever appears on the docker process command line. Agent runner's MCP server registration in `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` now reads `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY` instead of `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` for the `x-consumer-api-key` header. Lockstep `.env` migration required: copy the existing `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` value (a `ck_*` consumer key in current deployments) into a new `COMPOSIO_MCP_KEY` line, then either replace `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` with the new `ak_*` project key from the Composio dashboard (recommended — restores REST) or leave it on `ck_*` until the project key is provisioned (REST stays 401 but MCP keeps working). `.env.example` documents the surface split, header conventions, and prefix expectations so the operator-side contract is auditable. Tests in `src/secret-env-file.test.ts` lock down both keys in `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` and the env-file emitting both values together. No fallback shim — the environment variables decide which surfaces work; missing either disables that surface explicitly.
- `mcp__nanoclaw__list_tasks` no longer throws `t.prompt.slice is not a function` when any row in `scheduled_tasks` has a BLOB-typed `prompt` column (#512). Reader-side: the per-row formatter inside `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` is extracted into a pure `formatTaskRow(t)` helper in `format-task-row.ts` that coerces the prompt regardless of shape — `string` passes through, `null`/`undefined` becomes empty, `{type:'Buffer',data:[...]}` (the post-`JSON.stringify(Buffer)` shape better-sqlite3 produces for BLOB columns) decodes via `Buffer.from(...).toString('utf8')`, and any other shape falls through to `String(...)` so the line still appears rather than poisoning the entire listing through the outer try/catch. Writer-side: a new `src/coerce-task-prompt.ts` helper (`coerceTaskTextField`) is wired into `src/ipc.ts`'s `schedule_task` and `update_task` handlers — string passthrough, JSON-Buffer shape decoded to UTF-8, every other shape rejected with a WARN log so the IPC drops rather than persisting `"[object Object]"` for a malformed payload (a row that would later fire with garbage as its prompt). The persisted contract is now "prompt is always real TEXT in the DB", with the reader's coercion as defense-in-depth for any historical BLOB row already on disk. Adds `format-task-row.test.ts` (12 cases pinning the reader's shape coercion + truncation + missing-field tolerance), `coerce-task-prompt.test.ts` (11 cases pinning the writer's decode + reject contract including multi-byte UTF-8 round-trip), and four new cases in `ipc-auth.test.ts` covering schedule_task / update_task decode-and-reject end-to-end. Also drops a stray `<<<<<<< HEAD` merge marker that had landed on `main` in a prior edit. Operator one-shot for any historical BLOB row: `UPDATE scheduled_tasks SET prompt = CAST(prompt AS TEXT) WHERE typeof(prompt) = 'blob';`. Closes #512.

- `COMPOSIO_USER_ID` is now forwarded into main- and trusted-tier agent containers alongside the existing `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` injection (#509). The companion admin-tile PR (`jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#204`) enhances `composio-fetch-precheck.py` to do the 7-slot Composio fetch INLINE via `POST backend.composio.dev/api/v3/tools/execute/<action>` (with `x-api-key` header) when both env vars are set — eliminating the ~$5/cycle LLM token cost on the 30-min cadence (~$240/day baseline from #509's `logs/usage.jsonl` analysis). Without `COMPOSIO_USER_ID`, the admin-tile precheck falls back to the LLM SKILL.md path unchanged, so the admin PR is harmless on its own; this host PR is what flips the inline-fetch path on. Implementation: `'COMPOSIO_USER_ID'` joins `'COMPOSIO_API_KEY'` in both `SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS` (env-file 0600 path so the value doesn't appear on `docker ps` / `ps`) and the local `CONTAINER_VARS` array inside `buildContainerArgs` (the trust-gated forward set). Untrusted containers continue to receive nothing — same tier policy. The user_id is account-identifying (binds to which user's connections to act against) rather than a credential per se, but gets the same env-file treatment as the API key on the same conservative-handling principle. After deploy, operator must add `COMPOSIO_USER_ID=<value>` to `.env` on the NAS — value comes from the Composio dashboard's connected-accounts list (the auto-generated `pg-test-*` user_id when connections were OAuth'd via the dashboard "Connect" flow, or whichever stable id the operator chose). On first deploy without the env value set, the admin-tile precheck still works — it simply emits `wake_agent: true` + `inline_fetch_disabled:no_credentials` and the LLM SKILL.md path runs the fetch via MCP unchanged. Adds one `secret-env-file.test.ts` case pinning the new SECRET_CONTAINER_VARS membership; existing tests cover the env-file format. Refs #509, `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#204`.

- Per-session-slot AGENT_MODEL routing — the maintenance container slot can now be pinned to a different (cheaper) model than the user-facing default slot (#509 Phase 2). New `containerConfig.maintenanceAgentModel` field on `RegisteredGroup`. Resolution ladder for maintenance spawns (`sessionName === MAINTENANCE_SESSION_NAME`): `maintenanceAgentModel` → `agentModel` → global `AGENT_MODEL` → `DEFAULT_AGENT_MODEL`. Non-maintenance spawns are byte-identical to the pre-#509 ladder — the user-facing chat never accidentally routes through the maintenance override even when both fields are set on the same group. Same validation as the existing per-group override (`resolvePerGroupAgentModel` against the user-facing-resolved value as the maintenance fallback — so a fat-fingered maintenance value falls back to the user-facing per-group choice rather than silently jumping to the global default and bypassing the operator's intentional per-group routing). Why this matters: per #509's `logs/usage.jsonl` analysis, the swarm group's maintenance container burned ~$280/day on `claude-opus-4-7` despite the maintenance work profile (heartbeat triage, nightly housekeeping, sub-skill orchestration) being the kind of work Sonnet handles equivalently to Opus. Setting `swarm.maintenanceAgentModel = 'sonnet'` (or `'sonnet[1m]'`) drops the maintenance per-cycle cost without touching user-facing chat headroom. The composio-fetch path lands separately as `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#204` + `jbaruch/nanoclaw#510` (script-only fetch, the dominant per-cycle cost item); this knob captures the *remaining* heartbeat + nightly cost. Implementation: new `resolveSessionAgentModel(containerConfig, isMaintenance, globalDefault) → {effective, source}` exported from `src/container-runner.ts` with three source-tag values (`global_default`, `group_override`, `maintenance_override`) so the per-spawn audit log (#418) attributes the value to the right config layer; `buildContainerArgs` accepts a new `sessionName` parameter (passed through from `runContainerAgent`'s `input.sessionName ?? DEFAULT_SESSION_NAME` resolution at the existing call site); the per-spawn info log gains a `sessionName` field. New `set_maintenance_agent_model` IPC handler mirrors `set_agent_model`'s semantics (string sets, null clears, empty/whitespace-as-clear, owner-of-bill authorization for non-main callers, `available_groups.json` snapshot refresh) so an agent or operator can flip the maintenance value at runtime without an orchestrator restart. Adds 9 unit tests for `resolveSessionAgentModel` (covering all 8 ladder branches plus the unknown-prefix maintenance fallback) and 4 spawn-arg integration tests pinning the docker `-e AGENT_MODEL=...` arg + the `source` field in the audit log for the four canonical configurations (maintenance + override; default-session + both fields set; maintenance-no-override; no-overrides-at-all). Operator step (post-merge): roll out to the swarm group first by sending a `set_maintenance_agent_model` IPC payload (`{type: 'set_maintenance_agent_model', groupFolder: 'telegram_swarm', maintenanceAgentModel: 'sonnet'}`) — same invocation surface the existing `set_agent_model` (#395) uses, which is host-side IPC rather than an MCP tool. If you want this reachable as `mcp__nanoclaw__*` from inside the main container the way `set_trigger` / `set_additional_tiles` / `set_trusted` are, that's a follow-up — neither this handler nor `set_agent_model` is currently registered in `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts`. If no quality regression observed in the next ~7 days of maintenance-session usage logs after rollout, extend to `telegram_main` and other heartbeat-running groups. Refs #509.

- The user-facing idle-close timer no longer closes a fresh container's stdin mid-conversation, and no longer ignores user input when computing "idle" (#506). Two coupled fixes: (A) re-anchor the timer when a follow-up user message is piped into an active container — previously the countdown was anchored only to user-visible agent output, so a tool-heavy turn that ran past `IDLE_TIMEOUT` (default 30 min) without emitting a `result.result` would close stdin mid-turn even though the user had just spoken. (D) a generation-guarded map keyed by `chatJid` ensures a stale `setTimeout` from a prior container generation cannot close a fresh replacement container that took over the same chat — observed 2026-05-04 in `telegram_main`, where a timer scheduled at 20:15:53 fired at 20:45:53 and closed a fresh container's stdin 7 seconds after spawn, forcing a cold-start cache rebuild ($0.15 on Sonnet, ~$0.30 on Opus 4.7) for the next user message. Implementation: new `src/idle-timer.ts` exports `installIdleTimerControl(chatJid, group, onTimeout)` (clears any stale entry on install), `getActiveIdleTimer(chatJid)` (lookup for the pipe path), `releaseIdleTimerControl(chatJid, control)` (drops the entry only if identity matches — a late-finishing prior cycle can't clobber a fresh control), and a `_resetIdleTimerStateForTesting` helper. The timer's `setTimeout` callback re-checks `activeIdleTimers.get(chatJid) === control` before invoking `onTimeout` — second line of defense against any future refactor that bypasses the install-time clear. Tier semantics preserved: main/trusted use `IDLE_TIMEOUT`, untrusted hard-coded to 5 min. `src/index.ts` replaces the inline closure-bound `idleTimer` / `resetIdleTimer` block in `processGroupMessages` with `installIdleTimerControl(chatJid, group)`, calls `idleTimerControl.reset('agent-output')` on any non-null agent result, calls `releaseIdleTimerControl` at end-of-cycle, and adds `getActiveIdleTimer(chatJid)?.reset('user-input')` to the pipe path right after a successful `queue.sendMessage`. Adds 8 unit tests covering deadline-firing, untrusted 5-min override, mid-window re-anchor, install-time stale-control clear, in-callback identity-guard short-circuit (captured via setTimeout spy), late-release-doesn't-clobber-fresh, manual `clear()`, and per-chat isolation. Refs ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#185, #186. Closes #506.
- `container/Dockerfile` defaults `agent-browser` screenshots to JPEG quality 75 via `AGENT_BROWSER_SCREENSHOT_FORMAT=jpeg` and `AGENT_BROWSER_SCREENSHOT_QUALITY=75` (#505). Without these knobs `agent-browser` falls back to PNG at full quality, which is the heaviest possible encoding — the 2026-05-04 `telegram_main` haircut-booking session captured 10+ screenshots and drove `cache_create_5m` to ~1.15M tokens with `cache_read` reaching 20.8M tokens across 80 minutes ($10.49 spent). JPEG at quality 75 cuts per-screenshot tokens by ~60–75% with no perceptible loss for the agent's pattern-matching purposes. The `agent-browser` SKILL.md examples now show `path.jpg` rather than `path.png` so generated commands match the new container default. This is Lever 1 from the issue analysis; Lever 2 (post-turn screenshot eviction via PostToolUse hook) is deferred until this change has been observed against a comparable browser-heavy flow. Refs ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#183, #184. Closes #505.
- `cadence-registry` now recognizes an optional `script:` frontmatter field (relative path under the skill's directory, e.g. `scripts/precheck-foo.py`) and populates `scheduled_tasks.script` from it on INSERT and shape-change UPDATE (#503). Before this, the cadence-registry path hardcoded `script=NULL` (line 441) — which made the agent-runner's existing precheck-gating contract (`{"wake_agent": false}` short-circuits the LLM, `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` `runScript`) unreachable for every cadence-registered sub-skill. The ten housekeeping sub-skills' SKILL.md prose claimed "the agent is never invoked" past their fire-time precheck, but in practice the LLM woke on every cron tick, ran each skill's Step-1 cursor read, and exited silently when the cadence cap blocked — ~10–20 wasted wake-ups/day per group. Implementation: `validateCadenceDeclaration` rejects absolute paths, `..` segments, and extensions other than `.py` / `.sh`; `composePrecheckScript(skillName, relScript)` exports the dispatch (`python3 <abs>` / `bash <abs>` against `/home/node/.claude/skills/<skillName>/<relScript>`) so the agent-runner doesn't need an executable bit on read-only-mounted skill scripts; `rebuildCadenceRegistry` resolves the host-side path under `<skillsDir>/<skillName>/`, errors-and-skips when the file is missing (operators see the gap at spawn rather than at fire time), composes the bash content, and treats a `script:` change as a shape change so swapping or removing a precheck refreshes the column on next spawn while preserving `session_id` / `last_run` / `last_result`. Companion: `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#199` adds `script:` declarations to the ten cadence-gated housekeeping sub-skills (memory-rotation, nightly-{backup,external-sync,undated-task-sweep}, entertainment-sync, memory-hygiene, wiki-lint, youtube-comment-check, soul-searching-wrapper, system-audit). Adds 13 new test cases (5 validator, 3 composer, 5 rebuild — script populated, NULL fallback, missing-file error, swap-as-shape-change, removal-as-shape-change). Closes #503.
- `scripts/deploy.sh` step 4b extends to retire the six post-#404 housekeeping sub-skills' legacy `task-subskill-*` `schedule-task` rows (#305 Phase 2b host follow-up). The companion admin-tile PR `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#198` (registry 0.1.305 → 0.1.306) added declarative `cadence:` frontmatter to `memory-rotation`, `nightly-backup`, `nightly-external-sync`, `nightly-undated-task-sweep`, `check-watchlist`, and `state-purge`; without this DELETE the cadence-registry's new `cadence-registry::*::tessl__<skill>` rows would coexist with the operator-bootstrapped legacy rows from #432 — both at the same cron, double-firing each housekeeping skill. The skill-internal Phase A lock (`follow_me_tasks` keyed by skill name) provides single-fire dedup at the skill level, but the second container still spawns + runs a full precheck before exiting on the held lock — pure waste. Step 4b's `MIGRATED` array gains six `[skill, "cron"]` tuples; per-skill `schedule_type` pinning keeps ad-hoc owner-scheduled rows of the OTHER shape (interval / once) safe from false-positive deletion. The narrow prompt predicate (`MANDATORY FIRST ACTION: Call Skill(skill: "...")`) is unchanged. Idempotent + safe-to-ship-before-admin-tile-publishes per the existing pattern (which it already has — admin#198 published first per the post-merge-publish-watch rule). Refs #305 #432 — companion `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#198`.
- Periodic `tessl_update` no longer races with mid-flight scheduled tasks (#496). Three coordinated mitigations close the window where `tessl_update`'s `closeAllActiveContainers` writes `_close` to a maintenance container running a follow-me task, the agent-runner watchdog fires `process.exit(0)` after 30s, and the host records `task_run_logs.status='success'` on a kill while the skill's `follow_me_tasks.pending_run_at` lock is left dangling — blocking the next scheduled fire on its Phase A gate. (1) `src/group-queue.ts` adds a per-`(group, session)` `forcedCloseAt` timestamp set ONLY by `closeAllActiveContainers` (the graceful end-of-task `closeStdin` path is intentionally unmarked); `src/task-scheduler.ts` consumes the stamp via the new `consumeForcedCloseAt` getter and reclassifies the run as `task_run_logs.status='killed'` when the stamp falls within the run's window — distinguishing bookkeeping-success (exit code 0 from the watchdog) from semantic-success. The `TaskRunLog.status` union widens to `'success' | 'error' | 'killed'`; existing readers (`audit-precheck-gating.ts`, `db-dump.ts`) tolerate the new value. (2) `src/db.ts` adds two non-owner reader helpers — `clearStalePendingRunAt(maxAgeMs)` and `getActivePendingRunAtNames(maxAgeMs)` — for `follow_me_tasks.pending_run_at` lock recovery / freshness inspection. The host is a non-owner reader per `coding-policy: stateful-artifacts`; clearing a value field whose owner is demonstrably dead is lock-recovery, not migration. (3) `src/index.ts` runs `clearStalePendingRunAt` once at startup (recovers from prior crashes) and gates the periodic 15-min `tessl update` on `getActivePendingRunAtNames` so it skips the tick when a fresh lock is held; the on-demand `tessl_update` IPC handler in `src/ipc.ts` defers with a structured `{deferred: true, activeLocks: [...]}` response on the same condition. Freshness threshold is 60 minutes — sized for the longest plausible follow-me task (morning-brief, ~10–15 min) with 4× margin while still recovering well before the next-day fire. Adds `_seedFollowMeTaskForTests` and `_rawQueryForTests` test-only helpers in `db.ts`. Three new test files / suites: `src/pending-run-at-cleanup.test.ts` (11 cases on the cleanup helpers), `src/group-queue.test.ts` adds 4 cases on the `forcedCloseAt` stamp lifecycle (set by `closeAllActiveContainers`, NOT set by graceful `closeStdin`, consume-and-clear semantics), `src/task-scheduler.test.ts` adds 3 cases on kill / happy-path / stale-stamp classification. Closes #496. Refs #497.
- Per-turn token classifier (`emitSessionTokens` in `src/usage-telemetry.ts`) now compares the full turn context size — `input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens` — against the warn / nuke thresholds, not the bare `input_tokens` delta (#498 Step 1). Under Anthropic prompt caching, `usage.input_tokens` is only the new tokens added this turn; the actual context the model saw is the sum. Pre-fix, six days of orchestrator logs (3,517 events) showed every session classified `below_warn` with peak `input_tokens=3,506` — while one session sat persistently at `cache_read=761,317` (76% of the 1M window). Threshold formulas in `src/threshold.ts` were already correct (`warn=700K`, `nuke=800K` against 1M); the bug was the input feeding them. The same fix applies to `lastUsedTokens` in `src/index.ts:1873` so the post-cross checkpoint diagnostic ("Tokens used at trigger: …") and `threshold_nuke_fired` / `threshold_nuke_inert` log lines no longer report the delta in place of the context size. Adds a `turn_context_size` field to the `session_tokens*` log shape for direct querying, and updates the caller invariant on `classifyUsage` + the field doc on `CheckpointInputs.usedTokens` to document the post-fix contract. Two new unit tests pin the cache-heavy and cache-creation-heavy production shapes against `warn` / `nuke`. Unblocks #498 Steps 2–6 (re-baseline observe-only telemetry, reschedule the `kill-auto-compaction-review-2026-05-05` task, settle #126 axes A/B/C, and eventually flip `ENABLE_THRESHOLD_NUKE=1`). Refs #104, #498.
- Per-group JSON-state migrations (epic #293) now emit a startup-summary log line per migration at the end of `migrateJsonState()` so operators have one grep target — `JSON state migration summary` — to answer "did the data plane come up clean" (#433). Each line carries `migrated=N skipped-already-done=M left-in-place=K` and, when K > 0, the list of group folders whose source files are still on disk; `leftInPlace > 0` lifts the line from INFO to WARN. The previous signal — absence of a `.migrated-<date>` rename — was an inverted check that kept the state-010 / `task-tz-state.json` failure in #431 hidden for ~3 days. Implementation threads an optional `MigrationSummary` counter through the shared helpers in `src/json-state-import.ts` (`parseJsonObjectOrWarn`, `handleConstraintViolationOrRethrow`, `renameMigratedSource`); each per-group migration constructs one summary at entry, passes it through, and returns it. New `hasMigratedSibling(filePath)` helper covers the skipped-already-done count by detecting `<file>.migrated-*` siblings (catches only `ENOENT`/`ENOTDIR`/`EACCES`/`EPERM` as recoverable per `error-handling.Specific Exceptions`; everything else propagates). Backward-compat preserved: the summary parameter is optional on every helper, callers without one still work. Includes 13 new unit tests in `json-state-import.test.ts` covering the helper-counter contract end-to-end. Closes #433.
- `schedule_task` and `update_task` IPC handlers accept the literal token `'local'` as a `schedule_timezone` value alongside null and pinned IANA names (#456 follow-up). The cadence-registry rebuild path was already writing `'local'` to `scheduled_tasks` (resolved at fire time by `task-scheduler.ts:computeNextRunDetailed` per #456 Wave 1), but the IPC paths fed the value through `isValidTimezone()` first — `Intl.DateTimeFormat(..., { timeZone: 'local' })` throws, so the handler logged "Invalid IANA timezone for schedule_task" / "Invalid IANA timezone in task update" and aborted. Agent-driven calls following the schedule-task SKILL classifier (which #456 Wave 2 ships in `nanoclaw-admin`, teaching `'local'` for tasks anchored to the user — "fill CFPs in the evening", "take my pills at 8am") therefore failed silently at the boundary; only the cadence-registry path could produce travel-anchored rows.
