Validation is extracted into `normalizeScheduleTimezone(input, scheduleType)` in `timezone.ts` — pure function, three terminal outcomes (`accept` with null / IANA / `'local'`, `ignore-non-cron` for `interval` / `once` schedule types where the column has no effect, `reject-invalid` for unrecognized strings). Both IPC sites consume it; both initial-`next_run` cron-parser calls now resolve `'local'` via `getCurrentTz()` with `TIMEZONE` fallback so the row stored matches what the next scheduler tick would produce on `computeNextRunDetailed`. Unblocks the smoke-test acceptance criterion in #456 ("a new owner-scheduled task created with `schedule_timezone='local'` via `schedule_task` IPC fires at the right wall-clock time when `current_tz` is changed mid-cycle"). Tests pin: `'local'` accepted on cron, ignored on `interval`/`once`, IANA names still accepted, unknown strings still rejected, ignore-on-non-cron takes precedence over reject-invalid (a typo'd tz on a once-task drops silently rather than failing the whole call — the field has no effect anyway). Refs #456.
- `tessl.json` (project root) flips from `mode: "vendored"` with pinned `nanoclaw-host@0.1.17` + `coding-policy@0.2.3` to `mode: "managed"` with both deps floated to `"latest"`, and `scripts/deploy.sh` step 3b broadens its verification to walk every `tessl.json` in the repo (excluding `.tessl/`, `node_modules/`, `groups/`, `data/`, `dist/` generated subtrees) and reject both pinned `version` AND any non-`managed` `mode`. The root manifest is consumed by `tessl install` to populate gitignored `.tessl/tiles/` for `@.tessl/RULES.md` resolution at agent runtime; `mode: vendored` was a leftover from an earlier `tessl init` shape and was being silently rewritten in-place by every `tessl update / install` invocation against pin drift the operator never committed (HEAD `0.1.17`/`0.2.3` against working-tree `0.1.27`/`0.3.12` on the deployed NAS — same drift class as `tessl-workspace/tessl.json` already covered, but on a manifest the pre-fix step 3b never inspected). Verification rejects three classes of violation explicitly: unreadable JSON (`OSError`/`JSONDecodeError`), `mode != "managed"`, and any `dependencies.<tile>.version` not equal to the literal string `"latest"`. The companion `nanoclaw-host` tile rule update (`rules/tessl-version-floating.md`) broadens the policy from "the `tessl-workspace/tessl.json` carve-out" to "every `tessl.json` in NanoClaw must declare `mode: managed` + `version: latest`" so future operator hand-edits and fork-merges can't reintroduce drift on either manifest. Refs the directive that NanoClaw's lifecycle is dynamic-latest-loading: no vendoring, no pinning, every tile resolves through the registry on every `tessl update`.
- `scripts/deploy.sh` step 4b extends to retire the legacy `morning-brief` `schedule-task` row (#456 Wave 2). The companion admin-tile PR (`jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin/pulls/feat/456-wave2-morning-brief-tz-local`) adds `cadence: "0 7 * * * (TZ=local)"` to `morning-brief`'s SKILL.md frontmatter; without this DELETE the cadence-registry's new `cadence-registry::*::tessl__morning-brief` row would coexist with the operator-bootstrapped legacy `task-1776347641153-dexq4p` row at `0 7 * * *` and morning-brief would double-fire at every 7am local. Step 4b's `MIGRATED` array switches from a flat skill-name list to `[skill, schedule_type]` tuples so per-skill `schedule_type` pinning protects ad-hoc owner-scheduled rows of the OTHER shape from false-positive deletion: `tessl__heartbeat` and `tessl__composio-fetch` keep their `interval` pinning, `tessl__morning-brief` joins as `cron`. The narrow prompt predicate (`MANDATORY FIRST ACTION: Call Skill(skill: "...")`) is unchanged so an ad-hoc owner reminder that mentions the skill name doesn't match. Idempotent + safe-to-ship-before-the-admin-tile-PR-lands per the existing pattern: zero rows match before admin tile lands the new cadence, the morning-brief row simply doesn't fire until the next deploy after both PRs land. Refs #456.
- Scheduler resolves `schedule_timezone='local'` against `tz_state.current_tz` at fire time (#456 Wave 1). The cadence-registry's #305 Phase 2a writes the literal token `'local'` for rows declared via frontmatter `cadence: "<cron> (TZ=local)"`; previously `cron-parser` received that string verbatim and would either throw or fall back to UTC. New `getCurrentTz()` reader in `db.ts` returns `tz_state.current_tz` when `schema_version` matches the supported version (1) and null otherwise (per `coding-policy: stateful-artifacts` reader contract). `computeNextRunDetailed` accepts an optional `resolveLocalTz?: () => string | null` callback — when the row's `schedule_timezone === 'local'` and the callback returns a zone, the cron evaluates against that zone; when the callback is absent or returns null (test harness, empty `tz_state`, unfamiliar `schema_version`), the call falls through to the existing `TIMEZONE` env-var fallback that NULL `schedule_timezone` already uses. Both production call sites in `task-scheduler.ts` (post-completion next_run + pre-dispatch remediation hint) now pass `getCurrentTz` as the resolver. Pinned-IANA `schedule_timezone` continues passing through `cron-parser` unchanged; pure-function purity preserved (no DB writes from compute itself). Tests pin: row with `schedule_timezone='local'` + resolver returning `America/Chicago` produces a non-7-UTC `next_run`; same row + resolver returning `Europe/Amsterdam` produces a different UTC instant without row mutation; no-resolver and resolver-returning-null both match `cron-parser` output for `TIMEZONE`; `getCurrentTz` returns null on absent row, returns the seeded zone on `schema_version=1`, returns null on unfamiliar `schema_version=2` with a WARN log. **Wave 2** (admin tile: morning-brief frontmatter migration + task-tz-sync mutation retirement + schedule-task classifier section + host deploy.sh DELETE of legacy schedule-task row + audit pass) lands in a follow-up PR per the issue's sequencing. Refs #456.
- Precheck-script wake-key contract aligned with `coding-policy: rules/script-delegation.md` (#480). The agent-runner's precheck JSON parser previously expected `wakeAgent` (camelCase); the published rule documents `wake_agent` (snake_case) and three of four in-fleet prechecks (`tessl__nightly-backup`, `tessl__entertainment-sync`, `tessl__system-audit`) emitted snake_case. Mismatch silently gated-out every fire of those three (`Script output missing wakeAgent boolean` log line, container exit 0, `task_run_logs` row reads success) — so the nightly backup repo froze at the 2026-05-01 commit, the monthly system audit hadn't run, and entertainment-sync was no-op. `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` (`ScriptResult.wakeAgent` → `wake_agent`, three log strings updated, JSON parser extracted into a pure `parseScriptOutput` helper covered by `script-output-parse.test.ts`) and the `set_schedule_task` MCP tool description in `ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` flip to snake_case. Companion `nanoclaw-admin` PR flips the three currently-working camelCase prechecks (`heartbeat`, `composio-fetch`, `morning-brief`) plus their tests / state-schema / eval task / `schedule-task` SKILL.md so all four are emitting the same key. Brief expected breakage window of ~15 min on heartbeat / composio / morning-brief between host deploy and the next `tessl update` — accepted because the alternative is two follow-up PRs to remove a transitional fallback. `docs/precheck-gating-audit.md`'s rule-vs-implementation note is removed (the split it called out is now closed) and the `2837-2846` line references in §How the gate actually works refresh to the current `4307-4326` location. Closes #480.
- `container-runner`: spawn-time `AGENT_MODEL` log now fires on **every** container spawn, not just when a per-group `containerConfig.agentModel` override resolves to something different from the global default (#418). Cost / latency attribution previously had a silent-default blind spot — groups running on the global `AGENT_MODEL` left no audit trail at all, so a fleet-wide model change went unrecorded against the default-spawn population. The log line now carries a `source: 'group_override' | 'global_default'` field so the resolved model + its provenance are self-explaining without joining against group config. Cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#88`.
- Telegram channel now classifies known-unactionable Bot API failures and downgrades them from ERROR to WARN, keeping the ERROR-level log meaningful for genuine bugs (#458). New `_isUnactionableTelegramError(msg)` helper covers four buckets that production logs (12h window, ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#76) showed dominating Telegram error noise: target-gone / forbidden (`message to react not found`, `message to be replied not found`, `message can't be deleted`, `message is not modified`, `MESSAGE_ID_INVALID`, `chat not found`, `reactions are not available in chat`, `bot was blocked`/`kicked`, `user is deactivated`), permission changes after registration (`not enough rights to send …` — admin removed bot post permission), Telegram rate limits (`429: Too Many Requests`), and transport-level transients (`Network request for '<method>' failed!` — Grammy `HttpError`). The classifier is wired into `TelegramChannel.sendMessage`'s outer catch (previously logged every failure at ERROR — 121 entries / 12h, ~96 dominated by permission removals) and `sendReaction`'s catch (363 entries / 12h, replacing the inline regex bouquet from #50 with one shared definition). Genuine unexpected errors — `Internal Server Error`, `ETIMEDOUT`, `Unauthorized`/401, `chat_id is empty`, `PARSE_ENTITIES_FAILED` — STILL log at ERROR; negative-classification tests pin this so a future over-eager regex broadening can't silence a real bug. Scope is intentionally limited to Telegram per the issue body — non-Telegram channels are out of scope. Cherry-picked from ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#76.
- Scheduler silent-stop synthesis + maintenance-session idle timeout (#461). Layer 1 (agent-runner): when `runQuery`'s for-await over the SDK iterator drains without yielding a `result` event, synthesize a terminal `{status: 'success', result: ''}` write so the host's `task-scheduler.scheduleClose` arms its 10-s teardown timer instead of letting the maintenance slot ride out the `IDLE_TIMEOUT + 30s` floor — slot drains within seconds rather than 30 min, and downstream scheduled tasks stop piling up against the still-considered-active prior. Plus a hard-exit watchdog: 30s after `_close` is observed in the IPC poll, the agent-runner calls `process.exit(0)` regardless of SDK drain state (timer is `unref()`d so it never blocks the natural happy-path exit). Layer 2 (host): new `MAINTENANCE_CONTAINER_TIMEOUT` env var (default 5 min) is the inactivity timeout when `sessionName === 'maintenance'`, bypassing the user-facing default container's `IDLE_TIMEOUT + 30s` floor that exists for multi-turn graceful-close windows. Behaves like the existing default-session hard timeout: reset on every streamed stdout marker via `resetTimeout()`, fires when no output has streamed for the configured window. Per-group `containerConfig.timeout` overrides still apply if set. The default-session timeout is unchanged. Cherry-pick from `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#69`.

- Stage 1 (trigger) and Stage 2 (Haiku classifier) now recognize multi-handle bots — when a single bot is reachable under more than one Telegram @-handle (e.g. an autocomplete-only `@AyeAyeSureBot` plus an internal/vocative `@AyeAye`), `ASSISTANT_USERNAME` accepts a comma-separated list and both gates iterate the full alias set instead of only matching the orchestrator-internal name (#464). New `ASSISTANT_USERNAMES` export (array) backs the multi-handle consumers; `ASSISTANT_USERNAME` (string, primary entry) is kept for single-value sites — container spawn args and the agent-runner identity preamble still render the canonical handle while the comma-separated env value flows through to the agent so its identity preamble lists every alias as "also reachable as @<x>". Stage 2 prompt's input-description, direct-mention rule, and Stage-1-handoff note are updated to teach the classifier that any listed alias resolves to the assistant; single-handle deployments still see byte-stable prompt and preamble strings. Reference incident: 2026-05-02 in `Old.wtf`, owner @-tagged `@AyeAyeSureBot` (autocomplete-filled) and the bot stayed silent — Stage 1's synthetic mention was derived from `ASSISTANT_USERNAME=ayeaye` only, missing the actual Telegram handle, and Stage 2's identity context labelled `@AyeAyeSureBot` as "a different bot". Workaround until merged: add the actual Telegram handle to `ASSISTANT_USERNAME` (now `ASSISTANT_USERNAME=AyeAye,AyeAyeSureBot`).

- `scripts/deploy.sh --no-cache` flag for forcing a fresh rebuild of both agent-runner and orchestrator images. Required when an upstream npm-from-github dep in `Dockerfile.orchestrator` (currently `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync`) ships a new version: BuildKit caches the `RUN npm install -g <github-repo>` layer keyed by Dockerfile string, NOT by GitHub state, so a default `deploy.sh` run silently reinstalls the prior version while reporting success. Reference incident on 2026-05-02 after a new `reclaim-tripit-timezones-sync` release: `deploy.sh` reported "deploy complete" three times while the orchestrator continued running the prior package version. Manual `docker compose build --no-cache --pull nanoclaw` produced a fresh image, but the subsequent `docker compose up -d --build` resurrected an older cached layer entry from BuildKit and re-tagged the OLD sha as `:latest` — the running container then served pre-update code. The new flag (1) propagates `--no-cache` (plus `--pull`) through `container/build.sh` for the agent-runner step, and (2) splits step 2b into separate `docker compose build --no-cache --pull nanoclaw` and `docker compose up -d --force-recreate --no-build nanoclaw` calls so there's no second `--build` invocation that BuildKit can cache-confuse. `--tiles-only` and `--no-cache` are mutually exclusive (the former skips rebuilds, the latter forces them). The default `deploy.sh` path (no flags) is unchanged — operators only need `--no-cache` when an npm-from-github dep version changes.

- `scripts/deploy.sh` cleans legacy `schedule-task`-source rows for skills migrating to declarative `cadence:` frontmatter (#305 Plan B). Companion to the admin-tile PR that adds `cadence: "*/30 * * * *"` to `heartbeat` and `composio-fetch` SKILL.md frontmatter — without this DELETE step, the next deploy would land both the legacy operator-bootstrapped `schedule-task` row AND the new cadence-registry row for the same skill, double-firing every 30 min. New step 4b runs after `tessl update` (admin tile bumps with new frontmatter) and before agent-container graceful close, narrowed by `source = 'schedule-task' AND prompt LIKE '%Skill(skill: "tessl__<name>")%'` so ad-hoc owner reminders that don't invoke a `Skill(...)` body are left alone. The `MIGRATED` array inside the inline node script names the two skills explicitly; future migrations under #305 Plan A (morning-brief, #456) extend the same array. Idempotent — once the legacy row is gone, subsequent deploys delete zero rows. Safe-to-ship-before-the-admin-PR-lands: when the legacy row exists but the admin tile hasn't yet shipped the cadence frontmatter, the skill simply doesn't fire until the next deploy after the admin PR lands (bounded by the cadence-registry's first rebuild on next agent spawn). Refs #305 Plan B.

- Cadence-registry foundation for #305 Phase 2a — overlays declare, admin coalesces. New module `src/cadence-registry.ts` walks `<sessionsDir>/skills/*/SKILL.md` at every default-session container spawn, parses `cadence:` (cron, optionally suffixed with `(TZ=local)` or `(TZ=<IANA>)`) and `priority:` (integer) from the YAML frontmatter, and idempotently upserts declared schedules as `scheduled_tasks` rows tagged `source = 'cadence-registry'`. The existing task-scheduler picks them up unchanged — no dispatcher changes — so a skill with `cadence: "*/30 * * * *"` in its frontmatter fires on the same path admin's current `schedule-task` IPC self-registration uses. Migration: new ALTER on `scheduled_tasks` adds the `source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'schedule-task'` column (`db-migration.test.ts` exercises the upgrade path: pre-#305 row reads back with `source = 'schedule-task'` after `initDatabase()` re-runs `createSchema`'s PRAGMA-gated ALTER), so every pre-existing row is implicitly tagged `'schedule-task'` and the registry's rebuild (which keys on `source = 'cadence-registry'`) leaves them untouched across respawns. The new column is orthogonal to the existing `created_by_role` (which answers a different question — trust-boundary provenance for the agent-runner's `<untrusted-input>` wrap at fire time, not row-creation provenance for the rebuild gate). The rebuild is **upsert-shaped, not DELETE+INSERT-shaped**, specifically so a respawn can't disturb in-flight `next_run` (cron-cursor advance), `session_id` (per-task SDK session reuse for #336 cache-stability), or `last_run`/`last_result` (per-fire history) on a row whose declared shape — `(cadence, prompt)` — is unchanged from the prior spawn. The three reconciliation paths: **inserted** (new declaration, no prior row), **updated** (existing row's cadence string or prompt changed → recompute `next_run`, refresh declarative columns, preserve session_id / last_run / last_result), **preserved** (declaration identical → leave the row entirely alone). Orphan-DELETE removes rows whose skill was uninstalled or dropped its `cadence:` declaration. Gated to `DEFAULT_SESSION_NAME` only — the maintenance session has a filtered skill set (`MAINTENANCE_SKILL_BLOCKLIST`), so running the rebuild from both would race on the same `group_folder` rows and the maintenance view would orphan-DELETE cadences declared by skills the default view installs fine. Cron strings are validated at registry-build time via the existing `cron-parser` dependency; malformed declarations are skipped (the skill stays installed, it just doesn't fire on cadence) with a WARN log line naming the skill so a tile author sees the typo on the next spawn. **`computeNextRun` is wrapped per-skill** so a single bad pinned-IANA zone (`(TZ=Not/A/Real)`) reports against just that skill instead of throwing inside the rebuild transaction and aborting every cadence in the group (`coding-policy: error-handling` "try alternatives before failing"). The TZ qualifier is stripped before cron validation and stored on the row's `schedule_timezone` column where the scheduler reapplies it on fire — matching the contract the existing `(TZ=local)` ladder in `task-tz-sync` already follows. Frontmatter parser is dependency-free (a flat-scalar YAML reader, ~80 lines) supporting top-level `key: value` scalars with quoted-or-bare values, signed-int coercion for `priority:`, inline `# comment` stripping on bare scalars (matches standard YAML semantics; quoted values pass `#` through unchanged), CRLF tolerance, and BOM strip — `js-yaml` for two scalar fields would be unjustified weight; if Phase 2c's aggregator pattern needs lists for `depends_on:` that can revisit. Hooked into `runContainerAgent` immediately after `buildVolumeMounts` returns: an empty skills tree (no declarations) reduces to a no-op (zero inserts, updates, preserves, deletes). Errors from inside the rebuild propagate up the spawn path per the project's no-error-suppression rule — per-skill failures (cron parse, IANA resolution, malformed declaration) are already collected into the `errors` array and don't throw, anything that does throw past that point (db corruption, fs walk fails on EACCES, etc.) is a real fault that should fail the spawn loudly. **Phase 2b (separate PR on `nanoclaw-admin`) replaces the existing `schedule-task` self-registration of `heartbeat` / `morning-brief` / `nightly-housekeeping` / `weekly-housekeeping` shells with `cadence:` frontmatter declarations, so the registry takes over for those four** — until that PR lands, the registry stays empty for them and there's no double-fire risk. **Phase 2c** adds the `contributes_to:` aggregator pattern (orthogonal axis on the same frontmatter; deferred so this PR isn't a mixed-axis review). 39 vitest cases in `src/cadence-registry.test.ts` plus a new `db-migration.test.ts` `source`-column upgrade-path case cover: frontmatter parser (CRLF, BOM, comments at line-start, inline `# comment` stripping on bare scalars + quoted-pass-through + adjacent-`#` preservation, strip quotes, signed-int coercion, no-coerce on decimals/digits-with-letters); TZ-qualifier splitter; validator (null-decl on missing cadence, malformed-cron rejection with skill-tagged error, non-int priority rejection, empty-TZ rejection, valid `(TZ=local)` and pinned IANA acceptance); directory walker (missing-dir/empty-dir tolerance, SKILL.md-less subdir skip); upsert rebuild (insert on first sight, preserve on identical respawn, preserve `next_run`/`session_id`/`last_run`/`last_result` across respawn, UPDATE-with-recompute when cadence changes while preserving history, no abort on per-skill `computeNextRun` throw, no touch of `source = 'schedule-task'` peers, no touch of cadence-registry rows in OTHER groups, malformed-skill skip with reported errors, no-op when no skill declares cadence, orphan-delete of a previously-registered skill on re-spawn); and `defaultComputeNextRun`'s shape (ISO timestamp output, accepts pinned IANA, equates `tz=local` to UTC at insert-time since the scheduler reapplies on fire). Refs #305.
- Stage 1 trigger-pattern self-improvement loop (#415, mirrors `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#82`). New module `src/gates/trigger-learner.ts` periodically samples observed message↔response correlations per registered group and proposes Stage 1 trigger-pattern adjustments. Proposals land in the existing `registered_groups.trigger_pattern` JSON column with `source: 'learned'` and `enabled: false` — the trigger gate (`src/gates/trigger.ts`) skips `enabled: false` rows, so a proposal in flight has zero matcher effect. The owner promotes via the existing admin path; this PR ships the learner + auto-rollback only, NOT the promotion UI (deferred to a follow-up). Non-negotiable safety rails are encoded in tests: (1) cold-start fallback — until N truth-labeled samples accumulate per group (default 50, configurable via `TRIGGER_LEARNER_MIN_SAMPLES`), the loop emits no proposals and the matcher falls through to universal/owner-set patterns; (2) external corrective signal only — the public `TruthSource` union is `user_reaction | owner_correction | haiku_verdict`, the gate's own Stage 1 verdict is NEVER fed back as truth, closing the self-reinforcement loop the source issue called out as the primary risk; (3) sender-tier weighting — owner=3.0, non-owner=1.0, anonymous=0.5, configurable via `TRIGGER_LEARNER_OWNER_WEIGHT` etc.; (4) auto-rollback on FP-rate spike — a learned pattern whose false-positive rate over the trailing 30-decision window exceeds 30% gets `disabled: true` (kept in the array, not deleted, so the owner can inspect why); (5) pattern versioning — every learned pattern carries `pattern_version`, `proposed_at`, `enabled`, `disabled`, and `prior_versions[]` (capped at one level deep to avoid unbounded column growth) so the owner can revert manually. Optional `TriggerPattern` fields (`pattern_version`, `proposed_at`, `disabled`, `enabled`, `prior_versions`) are documented in `src/gates/trigger-learner-schema.md` per `rules/stateful-artifacts.md`; the wrapper config stays at `version: 1` because adding optional fields is backward-compatible (the existing `isTriggerPatternConfig` validator only checks required fields, and bumping to v2 would trip `parseTriggerPatternColumn`'s "future version" warning on every existing row). Wiring layer in `src/gates/trigger-learner-runtime.ts` builds the persistence against the live DB (mining truth labels from the `reactions` table) and starts a `setInterval` loop at the configurable cadence (`TRIGGER_LEARNER_INTERVAL_MS`, default 24h); the loop is started from `src/index.ts` alongside `startSchedulerLoop`. 36 unit tests in `src/gates/trigger-learner.test.ts` cover all five safety-rail invariants plus persistence-layer behaviour (saveConfig errors don't crash the scheduler, per-group failures don't abort the whole pass, no-config groups warn and are skipped). 4 new tests in `src/gates/trigger.test.ts` pin the matcher's new suppression contract: `enabled: false` and `disabled: true` rows are skipped during pattern walking; legacy rows with neither flag set match exactly as before. All deterministic — fixed sample fixtures, frozen `now()`, no `Math.random`. **Post-review fixes (OpenAI policy review on PR #452):** (a) `getReactionsForMessage` now SELECTs and returns `reactor_jid` alongside `reactor_name`; the runtime miner passes `r.reactor_jid` to `classifySender` (was passing `reactor_name` for both args, so owner detection by JID never matched and the owner weight 3.0 silently degraded to non-owner 1.0); (b) `buildLearnerPersistence` now accepts a `rollbackWindow` argument and threads it into `fetchDecisionHistories`, with `startTriggerLearner` passing `cfg.rollbackWindow` (resolved from `TRIGGER_LEARNER_ROLLBACK_WINDOW`) — the persistence layer previously hardcoded the default and silently ignored larger configured windows. 8 new tests in `src/gates/trigger-learner-runtime.test.ts` pin the wiring (owner-by-JID classification, non-owner control, anonymous control, owner-weight applied via reactor_jid, rollback-window forwarded for 15/100/default). Deferred sub-scopes (each tracked as a follow-up issue): the promotion UI / admin path that flips `enabled: false → true` (the schema hooks are present; the UX is not built here); the per-group dashboard surfacing precision / lift / proposal queue; producer-side enrichment of the `haiku classifier verdict` log line with `messageId` + `inboundText` so Haiku-derived samples can join into the truth signal (v1 ships with reaction-only mining, which is sufficient on active groups). Closes #415.

- Non-main heartbeat task retired (#453, follow-up to `jbaruch/nanoclaw-core#38`). The non-main heartbeat existed solely to drive `tessl__check-unanswered`, the per-cycle SQL+LLM scan for unreplied messages retired in `nanoclaw-core#38` because the steady-state token cost (LLM reasoning over `surrounding_context` for every candidate, every 30 min, every active group with `enableHeartbeat`) wasn't justified by the rate of genuinely-dropped messages it caught. With no work for the task to do, this PR rewires the orchestrator side: (1) `registerGroup` no longer creates a `heartbeat-<folder>` row for non-main groups regardless of `containerConfig.enableHeartbeat`, and (2) startup runs `cleanupOrphanNonMainHeartbeats` (replacing the prior `syncNonMainHeartbeat` / `syncNonMainHeartbeatPrompts` pair) which iterates every non-main registered group and `deleteTask`s any leftover `heartbeat-<folder>` row from a previous orchestrator version. The cleanup pass MUST run before `startSchedulerLoop`'s first tick — otherwise an orphan row with `next_run <= now` from orchestrator downtime would dispatch the (now-broken) old prompt before cleanup gets a chance to delete the row. **User-visible behaviour change:** `containerConfig.enableHeartbeat: true` on a non-main group is now a no-op. The flag stays in the schema for backwards compatibility (operators upgrading from older orchestrator versions may have it set), but no scheduled task is created. The main-group heartbeat (the `tessl__heartbeat` skill in `nanoclaw-admin`) is unaffected — separate code path, separate prompt, separate lifetime, only loaded for main containers via the admin tile. `setGroupTrusted` no longer reconciles `scheduled_tasks.script` on trust flips because there's no heartbeat task to reconcile; the prior behaviour wrote `tessl__check-unanswered/scripts/unanswered-precheck.py` (the path constant `UNANSWERED_PRECHECK_SCRIPT`) into the row's `script` field for trusted non-main groups and `null` for untrusted. Constants `NON_MAIN_HEARTBEAT_PROMPT`, `LEGACY_NON_MAIN_HEARTBEAT_PROMPTS`, and `UNANSWERED_PRECHECK_SCRIPT` deleted entirely. Test surface: `src/ipc-auth.test.ts`'s `deps.registerGroup` mock no longer mirrors heartbeat creation (would have masked production regressions); the `register_group does NOT auto-create a heartbeat …` test consolidates with the prior `register_group with enableHeartbeat creates the non-main heartbeat (#158)` test into one negative case asserting both the `requiresTrigger=true` path AND the `enableHeartbeat: true` path produce no `heartbeat-<folder>` row. Set-trigger orthogonality tests (preserve preexisting heartbeat across trigger flip) still pass — they exercise mock-injected fixture rows, not registerGroup-created rows. Comment cleanup in `src/container-runner.ts`, `src/ipc.ts`, `src/observer.ts`, `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` no longer cites the retired skill by name; the security test fixture skill name in `atomicPublishDir` tests becomes `status` (the only remaining `nanoclaw-core` skill). `research/nas-path-audit.md` deleted — 2026-03-27 research notes for a workspace-mount migration that has long since shipped. Closes #453. Refs: `jbaruch/nanoclaw-core` PR #39, `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin` PR #173, `jbaruch/nanoclaw-trusted` PR #31 (the three tile-side cleanups, all merged + published as `nanoclaw-core@0.1.97` / `nanoclaw-admin@0.1.294` / `nanoclaw-trusted@0.1.60`).

- Session-length cap prevents `cache_read` explosion on long-lived sessions (#413). Without an upper bound on a Claude Code session's lifetime, a single session keeps re-reading the entire prior context as `cache_read` on every turn — observed at >18M `cache_read` in a single query on the 2026-04-26 `telegram_main` SmartThings history-build run (498 internal SDK turns over 75 minutes; one query consumed `cache_create=4,819,008 + cache_read=18,210,198` ≈ 700+ fresh-session-equivalents). The SDK's auto-compact summarises but does not delete; summary plus subsequent turns keep growing on top. The new cap tracks **cumulative** per-session totals (sum of `usage.input_tokens` across all turns + turn count) and forces a fresh-session boundary when either threshold trips. Distinct from the existing per-turn `ENABLE_THRESHOLD_NUKE` path, which fires only when a single turn's `input_tokens` crosses a percentage of the context window — a session can stay below that per-turn threshold for hours while still accumulating past the cumulative cap. Configured via two env vars in `src/config.ts`: `SESSION_TOKEN_CAP` (default 1,000,000) and `SESSION_TURN_CAP` (default 100); a non-positive value disables that specific threshold without disabling the other. Both validated at startup with the same `parseInt` / positive-integer check pattern as `AGENT_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`. Threshold detection fires AFTER each assistant turn completes — the `wrappedOnOutput` callback in `runAgent` (`src/index.ts`) calls the new `recordSessionTurn` DB helper to upsert running totals, then `shouldMarkForReset` (pure logic in `src/session-length-cap.ts`) classifies the post-turn snapshot. When the verdict says reset, `markSessionForReset` flips `marked_for_reset = 1` on the row and persists the trip reason (`'token_cap'` vs `'turn_cap'`) plus the cap value at trip time so the user-facing notification reflects what was actually configured when the threshold tripped, not whatever the env says when the next inbound spawn consumes the marker. Reset itself happens on the NEXT inbound message (never mid-turn): `consumeSessionReset` reads-and-deletes the marked row in a single transaction, the orchestrator builds a brief context-handoff prefix from the most recent assistant turn (`getLastFromMeMessage` → `buildHandoffPrefix`, capped at 400 chars with whitespace collapsed) and prepends it to the prompt the new container sees, drops the stale `sessionId` from BOTH the in-memory cache and the `sessions` DB row so the SDK spawns fresh, and fires the in-band notification (`buildResetNotification`) via the channel router before the spawn — the user sees "Session reset: cumulative input tokens crossed 1,000,000 — starting fresh to keep context bounded." before the agent's first reply arrives. New SQLite table `session_length_state` (state migration `state-011-session-length-cap`, owner skill = orchestrator, schema doc at `src/session-length-cap.schema.md`) carries `(group_folder, session_name)` PK matching the existing `sessions` table key shape, plus `total_input_tokens`, `turn_count`, `last_handoff_summary`, `marked_for_reset`, `reset_reason`, `reset_cap`, `started_at`, `last_updated_at`, and `schema_version` — `schema_version` from day one per `rules/stateful-artifacts.md`. The writer (`recordSessionTurn`) detects rare drift (row exists but `session_id` differs from the live SDK chain — happens when the SDK rotated under us without going through the cap-state reset path) and treats it as a fresh row via `INSERT OR REPLACE` so accounting always lines up with the active `session_id`. `markSessionForReset` is idempotent (the WHERE clause excludes already-marked rows so a second call from a multi-message single runQuery doesn't churn the `last_updated_at` or overwrite the reason that actually fired first). `consumeSessionReset` is atomic (read-then-delete in one transaction) so the second inbound after a reset returns null rather than double-resetting. Group nuke (`/nuke` IPC handler) now also calls `clearSessionLengthStateForGroup` so a forced reset wipes cap accounting alongside the SDK session — without it a stale `marked_for_reset = 1` row could survive against a `session_id` that's already gone, producing a noisy log line on the next spawn. Three new test files cover the seam: `src/session-length-cap.test.ts` (15 cases — boundary correctness for both caps, deterministic precedence when both fire simultaneously, disabled-cap handling, handoff-prefix builder including the 400-char truncation and whitespace collapse, notification formatting), `src/session-length-db.test.ts` (16 cases over fresh in-memory SQLite via `_initTestDatabase` — UPSERT semantics, `last_handoff_summary` write-once via COALESCE, slot independence, idempotent mark, atomic consume, full lifecycle accumulate-→-mark-→-consume-→-fresh-INSERT under a new `session_id`), and `src/state-migrations/state-011-session-length-cap.test.ts` (5 cases — bumps `user_version` to 11, no collision with the existing `sessions` table, `schema_version` shape, composite PK, nullable `reset_reason` + `reset_cap`). Two non-secret config vars (`SESSION_TOKEN_CAP`, `SESSION_TURN_CAP`) are documented in `.env.example` with a new "Runtime config" section and the standard repo-settings deep link per `rules/no-secrets.md`. Deferred (called out in the issue body): the third proposed threshold "last summary-event was N turns ago" requires an SDK summary-event hook the orchestrator doesn't currently observe — the SDK auto-compact event isn't surfaced in `ContainerOutput`. Punted to a follow-up; the two cumulative thresholds above are the solid pair the issue called for as a fallback. Closes #413.

- Agent SDK system-prompt now splits into a frozen prefix + volatile suffix so the Anthropic prompt cache (5-min TTL, API-key-scoped) lands the prefix on every subsequent message in the same group/session window (#416). Pre-#416 the entire system prompt was a single block: any byte change in the per-message dynamic sections (working directory, auto-memory paths, git status — emitted by Claude Code's preset under the hood) invalidated the whole prefix and we paid full input-token cost on every turn. Empirical group data: 93% of inter-message gaps within a group fall under the 5-min TTL, so a stable prefix would land in cache nearly every time. The `query()` system-prompt option now sets `excludeDynamicSections: true` (the SDK's API for separating the cacheable preset from per-message volatile content — the SDK strips dynamic sections out of the preset and re-injects them as a synthetic `isMeta:true` user message, so the model still has access to the working directory / memory paths / git status while the prefix stays byte-identical; the `isMeta` flag is the SDK's own marker for "synthetic, not-a-real-user-turn" — its `getSessionMessages` filter excludes them from session history and our `parseTranscript` helper does the same so archived conversations stay clean). Pre-existing frozen content (identity preamble + SOUL.md + FORMATTING.md) moves into a new pure-function builder `buildFrozenSystemPromptAppend` in `container/agent-runner/src/system-prompt-assembly.ts`, which takes only frozen-classified inputs by signature — volatile content cannot enter the frozen segment by construction. The classification rule is deterministic at runtime (no judgement): a value is FROZEN when it cannot change WITHIN a single `runQuery` invocation (because runQuery captures each frozen input once at the top of the call and reuses the captured value for every turn that runQuery emits) and VOLATILE otherwise. Values that change BETWEEN runQuery invocations within the same long-lived container (e.g., an operator-edited SOUL.md / FORMATTING.md picked up on the next runQuery) are still allowed and intentional — the cache miss on the next runQuery is the right behavior because the new authoritative steering MUST take effect. The cache invariant is upheld by reading SOUL.md / FORMATTING.md once per `runQuery` invocation, not by mount mode — `/workspace/global` is mounted RO on untrusted/trusted but RW on main containers (`readonly: !isMain` in `src/container-runner.ts`); ACROSS runQuery invocations within the same long-lived agent-runner process, if SOUL.md / FORMATTING.md content has changed the prefix shifts by design and the cache miss is correct (the new authoritative steering must take effect on the next message). `ASSISTANT_NAME` / `ASSISTANT_USERNAME` are spawn-time env vars (`-e ASSISTANT_NAME=...` injected by `src/container-runner.ts`); they're stable for the lifetime of the agent-runner process and a host-side rename takes effect only after the container is bounced or recycled — the cache prefix does NOT shift on identity drift while the container is up. Per-turn cache validation: the agent-runner now logs `[cache] turn #N cache_creation_input_tokens=… cache_read_input_tokens=… input_tokens=…` to stderr on every assistant turn — numeric counters only, never the prompt content (per `coding-policy: no-secrets`); the cache is keyed by the system-prompt prefix and 5-min wall-clock TTL, NOT by `runQuery` boundaries, so the operator's expected pattern is indexed by API call within the cache's 5-min window: the FIRST API call after a cold-cache window shows `creation > 0, read = 0`, every subsequent API call within the same 5-min window shows `creation = 0, read > 0` — including the FIRST turn of a follow-up `runQuery` if it falls inside the window (a `turn #1` line on the second runQuery showing `read > 0` is a cache hit, NOT a miss; `turn #N` is reset per runQuery and indexes assistant turns within ONE runQuery, not cache-window position). Persistent `creation > 0` on every API call means the prefix is shifting and volatile content has leaked into the frozen segment — `system-prompt-assembly.ts` is the single file to inspect. Tests: 10 in `container/agent-runner/src/system-prompt-assembly.test.ts` covering byte-identical output across two successive invocations on the same inputs (the cache invariant — same Buffer.byteLength, same string equality), the fixed segment ordering (identity → SOUL → FORMATTING), separator preservation (`\n\n---\n\n` between non-empty parts), graceful skip-when-missing (no stray separators on partial inputs), and a runtime read-set pin that the frozen builder ignores any volatile field at runtime (the load-bearing CI gate — passes every volatile field the issue rules out alongside the frozen subset via a type cast and asserts byte-equality with the frozen-only call; if a future refactor wires any volatile field into the output, byte-equality fails and CI catches the regression). The TYPE-level `@ts-expect-error` block remains as documentation for the intended signature but is NOT load-bearing in this repo's CI (root `tsc --noEmit` only includes `src/**/*`, container's tsconfig excludes `*.test.ts`, and Vitest doesn't type-check tests by default — empirically verified by widening `FrozenSystemPromptInputs` to accept a volatile field; the test stayed green until the runtime pin was added). Plus 15 new in `container/agent-runner/src/parse-transcript.test.ts` covering the SDK-internal-message filter (`isMeta:true` synthetic dynamic-section turns, `isCompactSummary:true` rollups, `isSidechain:true` subagent traffic, `teamName`-tagged Task-tool / TeamCreate transcripts — none appear in archived conversations) alongside ordering, malformed-line tolerance, narrow `SyntaxError`-only catch on `JSON.parse` (rethrows non-SyntaxError per `coding-policy: error-handling`), and assistant tool_use / thinking strip. Cost impact (estimated, Opus 4.7, ~30K-token frozen prefix, ~4,073 weekly spawns across 6 groups): -83% to -85% weekly input-token cost; the single biggest structural lever in the cost-optimisation analysis. Manual operator action required after merge: `./scripts/deploy.sh` rebuilds the agent image so the change takes effect on the next message; the orchestrator-side change alone won't apply without an agent image rebuild. Copilot review verdicts: provenance regression theory disproved empirically — the SDK's own `getSessionMessages` filter (the `BS` predicate in `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` `sdk.mjs`) drops every `isMeta:true` line, so the synthetic dynamic-section turn never reaches our `walkBackIterableForProvenance` and cannot fail-open the capability ACL / egress allowlist / rate-limit / confirmation-token gates; transcript-pollution concern was real and is addressed (now-shared `isMeta` filter, plus `teamName` parity with the SDK's BS predicate); test-naming concern was real and is addressed (runtime read-set pin replaced the `@ts-expect-error`-only documentation that wasn't CI-enforced); doc claim about `/workspace/global` being mounted RO was incorrect for main and is addressed (cache window scope reframed around once-per-runQuery file reads); OpenAI policy review CHANGES_REQUESTED on a bare-catch `JSON.parse` handler addressed by narrowing to `SyntaxError` per `coding-policy: error-handling`. Closes #416.

- Interval-scheduled tasks no longer fire at half their configured cadence (#438). Two writers were independently advancing `next_run`: a pre-dispatch site in `startSchedulerLoop` (the `startSchedulerLoop` dispatch site in `src/task-scheduler.ts`) computed `N + ms` against the row at `N` and wrote it back before `enqueueTask`; the post-completion `runTask` finally then re-fetched the row (now at `N + ms`), and `computeNextRunDetailed`'s interval branch anchored on `task.next_run + ms` — producing `N + 2·ms`. Net: every interval fire silently advanced the cadence by `2·ms`. Live observation 2026-05-02 ~14:55 UTC against the orchestrator DB: heartbeat (`schedule_value='1800000'`, 30 min) and composio-fetch (also 30 min) both showed `next_run - last_run ≈ 58 min` for the most recent fire, and `task_run_logs` confirmed every gap between consecutive runs ≈ 60 min over the prior 36h — never ≈ 30 min. Cron tasks were unaffected because `computeNextRunDetailed`'s cron branch parses the cron expression rather than anchoring on `task.next_run`, so the pre-advance had no compounding effect there. Fix (Option B from the issue): delete the pre-dispatch `updateTask({ next_run })` write at the `startSchedulerLoop` dispatch site in `src/task-scheduler.ts` so `next_run` only advances post-completion via `updateTaskAfterRun`, which means `computeNextRunDetailed` reads the same anchor every fire and drifts by `0` on the happy path. The pre-advance was originally added for crash-mid-fire safety; replacement is a module-scope `const dispatchedTaskIds = new Set<string>()` populated immediately before `deps.queue.enqueueTask(...)` and cleared in the runTask wrapper's `.finally` (paired with dispatch, not with DB bookkeeping, so cleanup runs regardless of how runTask resolves — success, throw, early-return on invalid group folder). The dueTasks loop filters against the set so the same row can't be picked up twice while a previous dispatch is still in flight, without mutating DB state. Crash semantics: the set is lost with the process, so on restart `getDueTasks` may re-dispatch the in-flight task once — strictly better than today's silent halving of throughput, and matches the once-task crash-safety contract `resurrectZombieTasks` in `src/db.ts` was already built around. The once-task pre-mark (`status='completed'` for genuine once-task completion, load-bearing for `resurrectZombieTasks`) is preserved on the `else if (currentTask.schedule_type === 'once')` branch — that path is independent of the interval double-advance bug. `_resetSchedulerLoopForTests` clears the new set alongside `lastPruneAt` and `lastDormantWarnAt` so per-test isolation matches the existing pattern. Two new end-to-end tests in `src/task-scheduler.test.ts` driving `startSchedulerLoop` + `fireOnce` against an interval row: (1) `delta = next_run(after fire) - next_run(initial) === 1_800_000` for `schedule_value='1800000'` — pre-fix this assertion observed `3_600_000`, locally verified by stashing only the source change (test stays in tree, fails with `expected 3600000 to be 1800000`); (2) a held-mock-container scenario where a second scheduler tick fires while the first dispatch is still running, asserting `enqueueTask` was called exactly once across two ticks — proves `dispatchedTaskIds` prevents the duplicate-dispatch the pre-advance used to cover. Live impact downstream: heartbeat now fires every 30 min instead of every 60 min, so the `composio_fetch_stale` wake-reason at the 60-min threshold stops flapping at the boundary; calendar/email signals are detected within 30 min instead of up to 60. Cost-side this approximately doubles agent invocations on every interval task (every interval task in the live DB was using the halved cadence as a silent cost optimisation rather than the configured rate) — the configured cadence is now the observed one. No DB shape change; the fix self-applies on next deploy. Pause-broken-cron short-circuit added per Copilot review: `applyComputeNextRunRemediation` flips a row with an unparseable cron expression to `status='paused'`, but the in-memory `currentTask` capture still says 'active' (read before the remediation), so without an explicit skip the loop would dispatch the very task it just paused. The loop now `continue`s when `computed.remediation === 'pause-broken-cron'`. Pinned by a fourth regression test (`skips dispatch when remediation paused the row mid-tick`) that registers a row with a non-parseable cron string and asserts `enqueueTask` is never called while the row's DB status flips to 'paused' — verified to fail without the short-circuit (`expected "spy" not to be called at all, but actually been called 1 times`). Defensive cleanup added per OpenAI policy review: if `deps.queue.enqueueTask` throws synchronously after `dispatchedTaskIds.add` but before the wrapper closure runs, the `.finally` never fires and the row would be wedged forever in `dispatchedTaskIds`; the loop now wraps the enqueue in try/catch, clears the set, and re-throws so the outer terminal catch logs the underlying enqueue failure and the next tick can retry. Pinned by a third regression test (`clears dispatchedTaskIds when enqueueTask throws synchronously`) that simulates a queue-saturation throw on the first tick and asserts the second tick re-dispatches successfully — without the catch+cleanup the row stays skipped forever (locally verified by stashing the source change while keeping the test in tree). Closes #438.
- Admin MCP surface for per-chat tile overlays (#305 follow-up). #305 shipped the host-side `set_additional_tiles` IPC handler + spawn-time enforcement, but the agent inside the main container had no way to drive it: there was no MCP tool to set the overlay, no MCP query to enumerate installed tile names before proposing a change, and `register_group` silently dropped any `additionalTiles` argument when constructing its `containerConfig` payload. Three additions close that loop. (1) `set_additional_tiles` MCP tool in `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` — main-only, mirrors the `set_trusted` / `set_trigger` partial-update pattern; takes `groupFolder` + `additionalTiles: string[] | null` (null clears the overlay back to baseline); fire-and-forget IPC, host validates against the live registry and rejects the whole write on any unknown tile. (2) `list_installed_tiles` MCP query — admin-only, sync round-trip via `runHostOperation`, returns `{tiles: string[], registryAbsent: boolean}` so the agent can show the operator valid overlay names before proposing a change (a typo would otherwise round-trip to a host-side rejection with no clean recovery from inside the conversation). New host-side handler at `src/ipc.ts:case 'list_installed_tiles'` reads `getInstalledTiles()` and writes the structured result via the standard `_script_result_<requestId>.json` path — `registryAbsent: true` distinguishes cold-start (never ran `tessl install`) from "registry exists but empty" so the agent can suggest `tessl_update` instead of just listing nothing. The result includes the trust-tier baseline names too (`nanoclaw-core`, `nanoclaw-trusted`, `nanoclaw-untrusted`, `nanoclaw-admin`); we don't filter at the host because the baseline set is policy that lives in `selectTiles`, not registry truth, and a future trust-tier change shouldn't silently alter the tool's return shape — the skill presenting the list is expected to call out baseline names as no-op when configured as overlays. (3) `register_group` MCP tool now accepts an optional `additionalTiles?: string[]` parameter and forwards it through the `containerConfig` assembly; pre-#305-follow-up the field was dropped entirely between the Zod schema and the IPC payload. The handler in `src/ipc.ts` already accepted it on the registration side via `containerConfig` pass-through, so this is purely a bridge-side wiring fix. Testable bridge logic is extracted into `container/agent-runner/src/overlay-tiles.ts` (`buildRegisterGroupContainerConfig` + `describeOverlayUpdate`) and unit-tested in `overlay-tiles.test.ts` (13 cases: every-field-undefined → undefined, single-field permutations, additionalTiles-only path, all-fields combination, null vs empty vs single vs multi-tile rendering) — same extraction-into-sibling-module pattern as `stable-task-id.ts` from #440 (keeps tests free of the `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` runtime that doesn't ship at the repo-level node_modules). Three new tests in `src/ipc-auth.test.ts` cover the host-side `list_installed_tiles` handler: non-main rejection, happy path returns sorted JSON in `stdout`, registry-absent returns `{tiles:[], registryAbsent:true}`. The existing `mockGetInstalledTiles` hoisted spy added in #439 is re-used to control "what's in the registry" per scenario. Out of scope: admin skill flow (`manage-groups` SKILL.md additions for the overlay configuration walk-through) — deferred to a separate PR in the `nanoclaw-admin` tile repo. Tile content (no overlay tiles like `nanoclaw-coding` are published yet) — deferred indefinitely; this PR makes the infra ready, the actual tile authoring is its own work.

- Phantom `bot-*` rows after failed Telegram sends (#428). Surfaced by Copilot review on PR #425 (the #414 narrowed-fallback fix): `src/index.ts` always called `storeMessage` after `await channel.sendMessage(...)`, and `src/ipc.ts`'s `send_file` handler always wrote a caption row after `await deps.sendFile(...)`, regardless of whether the send actually reached Telegram. Heartbeat counts a `bot-*` row as evidence the user message was answered, so a transport-layer failure (network / 429 / 5xx — exactly the cases #414's narrowed gate now re-throws on) silently masked the unanswered state and the user went silent-with-no-operator-signal. Two surfaces fixed: (1) the orchestrator text-reply path at `src/index.ts:1639` now gates `storeMessage` on `shouldStoreBotMessage(chatJid, sentMsgId)` — the same predicate the IPC `send_message` (`src/ipc.ts:686`) and `send_message_to_chat` (`src/ipc.ts:2254`) handlers already used, so all four bot-row write sites apply consistent gating; (2) `Channel.sendFile`'s contract changes from `Promise<void>` to `Promise<string | undefined>` (the message id on success, undefined on outer-catch failure), updated in `src/types.ts`, `src/ipc.ts`'s `IpcDeps`, the `deps.sendFile` proxy in `src/index.ts:2553`, and the Telegram implementation in `src/channels/telegram.ts:1717` (which now captures `sendDocument`'s return at every success branch — initial HTML send, parse-rejection fallback path's plain-caption send — and surfaces it through the outer try). The IPC `send_file` handler (`src/ipc.ts:507`) gates the caption-row `storeMessage` on the same predicate. The `shouldStoreBotMessage` predicate continues to fall through (return true) for non-Telegram channels — WhatsApp / Slack / Discord don't yet surface a success-id contract, and gating there would punish passing sends; future channels that surface ids can opt in by extending the predicate. WARN log lines on the skipped-row path (`'[send] Skipping bot-message storeMessage — channel returned no message id (delivery failed)'` and `'send_file: skipping caption storeMessage — sendFile returned no message id (delivery failed)'`) preserve the operator-visible signal alongside the channel's own `'[send] Failed to send Telegram message'` / `'Failed to send Telegram file'` lines. Tests: 3 new in `src/channels/telegram.test.ts` lock the new return-value contract — successful HTML send returns the id, successful plain-caption-fallback send returns its id, ECONNRESET-on-outer-catch returns undefined; the existing `src/ipc.test.ts` `shouldStoreBotMessage` predicate suite (post-#232) already covers the gating logic both sites now share. Once this lands and deploys, heartbeat's unanswered-message check sees the gap on real delivery failures instead of being lied to by a phantom row. Closes #428.
- New `inspect_gate_decisions` MCP tool — admins can ask the agent "why didn't AyeAye respond to message X" / "what did the gate think about the last 10 messages" and get a structured answer instead of the operator having to grep the orchestrator's host log themselves (#443). Reference incident on 2026-05-02: in `telegram_old-wtf` an inbound message was Stage-1-allow / Stage-2-deny by the Haiku classifier, but no surface inside an agent container could explain why — gate decisions never reached past the host's `data/host-logs/orchestrator.log`. The pivot away from a SQLite `gate_decisions` table (early proposal in the issue body) was deliberate: every-message gate verdicts on durable storage re-introduces the retention concern that logs solve by rotation, and the host-log file is already RO-mounted into the admin agent container per the existing host-conventions split. New parts: (1) `src/host-log-parser.ts` — pure parser for the orchestrator's pretty-printed format produced by `src/logger.ts:formatData` (`[HH:mm:ss.SSS] LEVEL (pid): msg` header + indented `key: <JSON.stringify(value)>` field lines), with `parseHostLog`, `readHostLog`, and the typed `findGateDecisions(records, {chatJid, messageId?, limit?})` filter; ANSI-stripping is idempotent for historical rotated logs; non-JSON field values (e.g. multi-line `err:` stack traces) keep their raw post-colon string instead of throwing. (2) `src/index.ts:evaluateGateChain` now emits a single canonical INFO line `'gate decision'` per per-message gate evaluation with `{chatJid, messageId, groupFolder, finalDecision, reason, chain[]}` — the contract the parser greps for. The chain array round-trips because `formatData` JSON-stringifies every value. (3) New `case 'inspect_gate_decisions':` host IPC handler in `src/ipc.ts` — admin-only check (matches `chat_status` precedent), `chat_id` required (cross-chat scans rejected to keep responses bounded and avoid leaking other chats' traffic), `limit` defaults to 10 with a hard cap of 100, optional `message_id` narrows to a single message. (4) New `inspect_gate_decisions` MCP tool in `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` using the existing `runHostOperation` synchronous-response pattern. Test surface: 12-case parser unit suite in `src/host-log-parser.test.ts` (header parsing, multi-record separation including no-blank-line back-to-back headers, JSON inflation of nested objects/arrays, raw-string fallback for non-JSON values, ANSI strip idempotency, ENOENT path, filter-by-chatJid most-recent-first ordering, message_id narrowing, limit honoring, non-gate-decision skip, malformed-record skip) plus 8-case end-to-end IPC integration suite in `src/inspect-gate-decisions.test.ts` (admin-only authorization, missing/empty/whitespace `chat_id` validation, non-positive `limit` validation, no-matching-records empty-array path, full chat-jid + chain serialization round-trip, message_id narrowing, limit ordering). Out of scope for this PR (separate follow-up to `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin`): troubleshoot-chat SKILL.md addition teaching the agent when to call the new tool. Closes #443.

- Untrusted-tier container spawn no longer fails with `read-only file system` when the per-group folder lacks `CLAUDE.md` on disk (#442). Post-#164 the source-of-truth for `CLAUDE.md` moved off the per-group folder onto the global trust-tier templates; `scripts/migrate-thin-claude-md.ts` deleted matching vanilla copies from group folders, and `buildVolumeMounts` in `src/container-runner.ts` now bind-mounts `groups/global/CLAUDE.md` (trusted) or `groups/global/CLAUDE-untrusted.md` (untrusted) onto `/workspace/group/CLAUDE.md` at every spawn so a trust flip is reflected on the next message. The hidden assumption: runc needs the bind-mount target file (`/workspace/group/CLAUDE.md`) to already exist on disk so it can overlay the bind on top — but for untrusted groups the parent `/workspace/group` mount is `:ro` (disk-exhaustion protection), so the loopback `tmpfs` rootfs at `/volume1/@docker/overlay2/<hash>/merged/workspace/group/` is read-only and runc cannot create the missing target. For trusted groups the parent is RW so runc creates the file and overlays cleanly; for main the mount source equals the per-group file (`groups/main/CLAUDE.md`, git-managed and always present on disk) so the file already exists. Untrusted is the only failing combination, and the migration on the live NAS at the time of #164's deploy ran exclusively in trusted folders — there were no `trusted: false` non-main groups then, so the symptom didn't surface until a fresh untrusted group registered. Live repro on 2026-05-02 after registering `Old.wtf - чат с дедами-занудами` (`tg:-1001633120997`, folder `telegram_old-wtf-pod`): five consecutive Docker exits with code 125 within two minutes (`docker: Error response from daemon: failed to create task for container: failed to create shim task: OCI runtime create failed: runc create failed: unable to start container process: error during container init: error mounting "/home/jbaruch/nanoclaw/groups/global/CLAUDE-untrusted.md" to rootfs at "/workspace/group/CLAUDE.md": open /volume1/@docker/overlay2/<hash>/merged/workspace/group/CLAUDE.md: read-only file system: unknown.`), tripping the circuit breaker for 30 minutes. The `fs.existsSync(claudeMdSource)` guard at the bind-mount site only checks the source side; the failure was purely on the target side. Fix: just before pushing the CLAUDE.md mount in `buildVolumeMounts`, touch a placeholder at `path.join(groupDir, 'CLAUDE.md')` if it doesn't exist on the host — the orchestrator's view of the group folder is RW even though the in-container view is RO, so runc has a target to overlay. The placeholder content is irrelevant (the bind-mount shadows it from the container's view). Gated narrowly to the failing condition (`!isMain && !group.containerConfig?.trusted`): trusted has a RW parent mount so runc creates the target itself; main's mount source equals the per-group file (always on disk, git-managed). Writing a host-side placeholder for trusted groups would also pollute `scripts/migrate-thin-claude-md.ts`, which classifies any non-vanilla `CLAUDE.md` as customized and refuses to migrate it (Copilot review feedback on #442). A future trust flip from trusted → untrusted lands here on the next spawn under the same gate, so no pre-emptive creation needed. Idempotent — `fs.existsSync` short-circuits if a prior spawn (or a pre-#164 per-group `CLAUDE.md` the migration didn't match) already left a file behind, content preserved. Three new tests in `src/container-runner.security.test.ts`: pre-condition assertion that `groups/<folder>/CLAUDE.md` is absent post-#164 migration, post-condition assertion that `buildVolumeMounts` creates an empty placeholder for untrusted, an idempotence check that a sentinel-content placeholder is preserved across calls, and a negative case pinning that no placeholder is written for trusted groups. Existing #164 tests pinning mount-order (folder before CLAUDE.md so the file shadow takes effect) keep passing — the placeholder write is on the host side, doesn't touch the mount-list shape. **Live incident recovery** (one-time, operator-side): `for f in telegram_old-wtf-pod telegram_ayeaye-jbaruch-leonid telegram_jbaruch-leonid-2; do touch /home/jbaruch/nanoclaw/groups/$f/CLAUDE.md; done` on the NAS to unblock the three groups currently exposed to the latent fault (`telegram_old-wtf-pod` failing now; the other two have `containerConfig.trusted` unset and would hit the same fault on first spawn since #164). After the deploy, the orchestrator self-heals: every subsequent untrusted spawn touches its own placeholder. Closes #442.

- `schedule_task` MCP tool now accepts an optional caller-supplied `task_id` parameter for stable, intent-named scheduled rows (#440). Container-side bridge at `container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stdio.ts` previously auto-generated every task ID via `\`task-\${Date.now()}-\${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 8)}\``with no override path, even though the host-side IPC handler at`src/ipc.ts:998`had always accepted`data.taskId || autogen` — the seam was purely Zod-schema-shaped. Surfaced while staging operator-side wiring for #432: long-lived recurring rows (sub-skill bootstraps, overlay-installed crons per #305, audit-replay identity per #375) want logs and audit trails to identify by intent (`task-subskill-memory-rotation`) rather than by random ms-timestamp slug. Auto-gen path stays unchanged for one-off "remind me" reminders. New module `container/agent-runner/src/stable-task-id.ts`exports`STABLE_TASK_ID_REGEX` (`/^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]{0,62}[a-z0-9])?$/`— lowercase alphanumeric + interior hyphens, must start AND end with letter/digit, 1–64 chars; trailing hyphens forbidden because they're visually indistinguishable from truncated ids in log lines) — separate from the bridge module so the matching test can import the regex without transitively pulling in the`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`runtime that only ships in`container/agent-runner/node_modules`. Bridge wires the regex into the new `task_id: z.string().regex(...).optional()`Zod field with an actionable error message and a`describe`block that documents both the format and the v1 fire-and-forget IPC limitation: PK collisions surface in`list_tasks`after the call (the host writes a row or logs a constraint failure; the agent's "scheduled" response is best-effort because the IPC channel doesn't carry a synchronous response yet). 30-case test battery in`container/agent-runner/src/ipc-mcp-stable-task-id.test.ts` covers intent-named acceptance (sub-skill bootstrap shapes, overlay shapes, single-char min, 64-char max), hostile rejection (empty, leading/trailing hyphen, whitespace including tab/newline, uppercase, Unicode/emoji, underscore, dot, slash, colon, at, 65-char overflow, NUL/BEL control bytes), and a guard ensuring the legacy autogen pattern itself still satisfies the regex so accidental tightening can't strand the existing auto-id corpus. Out of scope for v1 (deferred to follow-ups): clean MCP-side "task already exists" error surfacing (needs synchronous response-file pattern the bridge doesn't have yet), reserved-prefix protection against autogen spoofing. Closes #440.
- Per-chat additive tile overlay via `containerConfig.additionalTiles` (#305). Multi-chat deployments need different capability sets per chat (e.g. a coding chat with `nanoclaw-coding`, a family chat with `nanoclaw-family`) without replacing the trust-tier baseline. New optional `additionalTiles?: string[]` field on `ContainerConfig` (`src/types.ts`) overlays on top of the baseline returned by `selectTiles(isMain, isTrusted)` — never replaces it. `selectTiles` gains an optional third parameter; baseline order is preserved and entries that duplicate a baseline tile (`nanoclaw-core`, `nanoclaw-trusted`, …) are dropped so the install loop still sees a deduplicated list with admin-tile-loads-last semantics intact. New helpers `getRegistryTilesDir()` and `getInstalledTiles()` in `src/container-runner.ts` are the single source of truth for which tile names exist in the local Tessl registry (`tessl-workspace/.tessl/tiles/<TILE_OWNER>/`); both write-time IPC validation and spawn-time tile install consult the same source. Fail-closed at both ends: (1) the new `set_additional_tiles` IPC handler (main-only authorisation, mirroring `set_trusted` because overlay tiles add capabilities) validates every entry against the live registry and rejects the whole write with `tile 'foo' not in registry` on any miss — partial acceptance would silently drop capability the operator asked for; (2) `buildVolumeMounts` adds a pre-install guard that throws `Error: additionalTiles missing from registry: …` when any configured overlay tile fails to resolve at spawn, so a registry rebuild between write and spawn surfaces visibly instead of degrading silently. The spawn guard validates the post-`selectTiles` normalized overlay (slice past the baseline length) rather than the raw config, so whitespace-padded or duplicate entries from non-IPC config writes don't cause false negatives. Baseline tier-tiles keep their existing degraded-but-warn behaviour (the `anyTileAvailable` check) so a partial-registry situation doesn't brick already-running groups. The handler also normalises payloads at write time: empty array / `null` clears the field (drops it rather than persisting `[]`), repeated entries are deduplicated, whitespace-only entries are rejected, non-string entries are rejected, non-array non-null payloads are rejected. Sibling `containerConfig` fields (`trusted`, `agentModel`, `enableHeartbeat`, `additionalMounts`, gates) are preserved verbatim through every update — regression-bait per the post-#105 contract. Tests: +18 covering `selectTiles` overlay (append after baseline for trusted/untrusted/main, dedupe against baseline + within overlay, skip empty/whitespace, undefined/empty-array no-op, order preservation) and +13 IPC scenarios (main-can-set, non-main-blocked, registry-validation rejects whole write on any miss, registry-absent rejects, null/empty-array clears, dedupe at write time, whitespace/non-string/non-array reject, missing groupFolder reject, sibling-field preservation, unregistered groupFolder no-op). Out of scope: CLI / admin-command surface for setting `additionalTiles` (separate ticket once plumbing lands), mid-session invalidation (containers pick up new tile lists on next spawn). Closes #305.

- `task-tz-state.json` migration (`state-010`, `migrateTaskTzStateJsonFiles` in `src/db.ts`) now translates the legacy on-disk follow_me shape — pre-state-010 `task-tz-sync` writers emitted `local_hour` / `local_minute` integer pairs and never duplicated the cron string on the follow_me row (it lives on the sibling `scheduled_tasks` row keyed by `task_id`). The migration was speced for a modern `local_time: "HH:MM"` + `schedule_value` shape that didn't exist on disk; every boot since state-010 landed silently failed the row-level `NOT NULL constraint failed: follow_me_tasks.local_time`, rolled back the entire group's transaction via `handleConstraintViolationOrRethrow`, and left the source JSON un-renamed for triage. Downstream blast radius (#412 / #404 readers — morning-brief Phase C/D, nightly Step 2/18, weekly Phase A, check-calendar, heartbeat-precheck Sections 6+8) tolerated the empty `tz_state` + near-empty `follow_me_tasks` as "task hasn't run yet" per the schema's reader contract, so no follow-me wall-clock anchoring and no Phase A/B/C dedup happened — Phase A/B/C UPDATE statements locked nothing for two weeks; the existing dedup was effectively a no-op. The fix synthesizes `local_time` from `${hour:02d}:${minute:02d}` with full integer-range validation (0–23 / 0–59) and looks `schedule_value` up by `task_id` from `scheduled_tasks`, falling back to the modern shape's `local_time` / `schedule_value` keys when present so a mixed-shape file imports cleanly. New per-row warn-and-skip path: a row that resolves neither shape is now skipped with structured context (taskName, taskId, which fields/lookup failed) rather than poisoning the entire group's transaction — sibling rows still land and the file gets renamed once the per-file batch completes. Per-row `name` PK validator added before upsert (#434 review feedback): a missing / non-string / empty `name` now skips with a distinct warn message instead of constraint-violating into a per-file rollback that defeats the rest of the fix. Four new tests in `src/task-tz-state-json-migration.test.ts`: legacy `local_hour`/`local_minute` + `task_id` lookup against pre-populated `scheduled_tasks`, zero-padding boundaries (`00:00`, `09:05`), orphan-task_id skip-and-warn with sibling modern-shape import succeeding, and the malformed-name PK skip path interleaved with healthy siblings. The `migrateJsonState()` retry on every `initDatabase()` (the every-boot loop in `src/db.ts:627`) means deploying this fix and restarting the orchestrator self-heals the live `tz_state` (0 → 3 columns) and `follow_me_tasks` (1 → 3 rows) without any operator-side bootstrap script. Closes #431.

- Effective `AGENT_MODEL` surfaced in the `/status` skill (#417, cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#85`). Adds `Agent model: ${AGENT_MODEL:-default}` to `container/skills/status/SKILL.md`'s session-context check + the report template, so operators can audit which group runs which model in routine `/status` health checks instead of grepping spawn logs. Complements the `chat_status` MCP-tool surface that #400 already added — different operator-facing surface, both matter (the MCP tool is admin-tier-only and queryable; the skill output runs in any container that loads it). The value is the _effective_ model — the per-group override (set via `set_agent_model`, NanoClaw#395) when present, the orchestrator's global default otherwise — because the spawn-time `AGENT_MODEL` env var is what the agent-runner actually used. Markdown-only change; no code or test surface affected.

- Stage 2 Haiku classifier + gate-chain refinements bundle (#407, cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#89` + `#91` + `#92` + `#98` + `#99` + `#102` + `#103` + `#104` + `#108`). Eight related PRs that landed sequentially in Leonid's fork on 2026-05-01 — all extend or refine the gate framework introduced in #86. Bundling reduces churn given canonical's deep fork divergence (#145 sender-allowlist removal, #289 reaction locality). **#89** introduces the Stage 2 Haiku classifier — `src/gates/haiku-classifier.ts` calls Claude Haiku 4.5 with a 21-shot prompt + per-group static context (`src/gates/strategies/static-group-context.ts`), returns `{intent: yes|no, confidence: 0.0–1.0, reason}`, false-positive-averse. New `getRecentSenderName(senderJid, chatJid)` helper in `src/db.ts` feeds display names into the prompt. New scripts: `scripts/seed-trigger-keywords.ts`, `scripts/extract-keyword-candidates.ts`, `scripts/update-trigger-patterns.ts` (each with an ESM entry-point guard per `coding-policy: file-hygiene`). SDK bumped to `@anthropic-ai/sdk@^0.92.0` for the messages.create API. **#91** ensures Stage 1 trigger always runs when patterns are configured — for groups with `requiresTrigger=false` AND `stage2Enabled=true`, the resolved chain was `['haiku-classifier']` only; every grey-zone message paid for a Haiku call when a deterministic match would short-circuit for free. **#92** teaches the classifier about the bot's owner — adds `ASSISTANT_OWNER_NAME` / `ASSISTANT_OWNER_HANDLE` env vars to `src/config.ts`, surfaces them through static-group-context so "my bot" / «мой бот» resolves correctly. **#98** skips Stage 2 for `requires_trigger=true` groups — `resolveGatesForGroup` appends `haiku-classifier` only when `stage2Enabled === true` AND `requiresTrigger !== true`. New groups default `stage2Enabled` to `true` via `applyNewGroupContainerConfigDefaults`; existing groups keep whatever was previously persisted, and an explicit `false` always disables. **#99** replaces AND-only combinator with last-gate-wins — `runGateChain` now treats `allow` as a short-circuit and intermediate `deny` as advisory; the final gate's verdict is authoritative unless an earlier gate already returned `allow`. **#102** re-enables `cache_control: { type: 'ephemeral' }` on FROZEN_PREFIX + per-group volatile suffix — recovers ~$50/wk on wtf chat (~90% input-token cost reduction). **#103** restores `cacheReadTokens` / `cacheCreateTokens` in the verdict log — observability fix for #102, lets operators compute exact per-call cost. **#104** restructures the gate chain consumer to return `{allowed, allowedMessageId}` from `evaluateGateChain` so the host knows which message produced the verdict (structural support for #108). **#108** introduces structured reply context — `buildGateContext` now strips Telegram's inline `[Replying to <Sender>: "<truncated>"]` prefix and populates a structured `replyTo: {messageId, senderName, isBot, isAssistant, contentPreview}` field on `NewMessage`. Stage 1 keyword/mention/synthetic-identity matchers run on the clean body; the `kind: 'reply'` matcher checks `replyTo.isAssistant` instead of regex-matching the prefix. Per the audit in `ligolnik#107`: 15-20 of 51 Stage 1 short-circuits in 51 min were false-positive matches against quoted preview tokens. The classifier prompt sanitises reply-preview content (collapse CR/LF runs to spaces, escape embedded `"`) before embedding it inside the `[Replying to ...: "..."]` header so a preview ending in `"` can't break the prompt's outer shape. **Conflict resolution.** Canonical removed sender-allowlist gating in #145; the eligibility-pre-filter that #89/#104 layered onto every gate-chain call (`isTriggerAllowed(chatJid, m.sender, allowlistCfg)`) is dropped throughout — gates evaluate every message and the trigger gate's pattern-match logic stands alone. Canonical's #289 (reaction locality) makes the agent-runner the single source of truth for the 👀 emit (only fires when the container is alive); #104's host-side `channel.sendReaction` emit is dropped because pre-fire on a host that hasn't yet spawned a container "lies during distress modes." `noteLatestUserMessage` from #104 is not reintroduced — observer parses `target_message_id` from the agent-runner's Query input log line instead. The `latestUserMessage` map in `src/observer.ts` stays removed. Tests: 2269 / 2269 passing (was 2181 on #86; +88 covering Stage 2 classifier framework, owner identity, last-gate-wins semantics, cache_control payload, structured reply context).

- Host-side Stage 1 gate framework (#407, cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#86`). Foundation for the Stage 2 Haiku classifier (#89) and the dynamic 98%-spawn-reduction architecture (#422). New `src/gates/` directory: `index.ts` exports `runGateChain(gateNames, ctx)` with deterministic AND-only combinator (last-gate-wins is layered later by #99); `trigger.ts` is the Stage 1 trigger gate (keyword / mention / reply / regex / sender_tier matchers, returns `allow` / `deny` / `pass`); `index.test.ts` + `router-integration.test.ts` + `trigger.test.ts` ship 50+ regression tests covering matcher correctness, fall-open semantics on regex/sender_tier-only configs (deferred-evaluation rules), and end-to-end host integration. New exports `resolveGatesForGroup` and `gateAllowsSpawn` in `src/index.ts` (`buildGateContext` stays a private helper); the two trigger-gate call sites (`processGroupMessages` + `startMessageLoop`) now route through the gate chain. Migration path A locked in: groups with no `gates` configured AND `requiresTrigger !== false` get the implicit `['trigger']` chain so pre-#80 behaviour is preserved bit-for-bit; main groups + `requiresTrigger === false` get an empty chain (always allow). Fork-divergence resolution: canonical removed sender-allowlist gating in #145; the eligibility filter that #86 layered onto the gate chain (`isTriggerAllowed(chatJid, m.sender, allowlistCfg)`) is dropped here — gates evaluate every message and the trigger gate's pattern-match logic is sufficient. Closes the Stage 1 prerequisite under umbrella #407.

- Inverse-direction emoji shortcode-Unicode drift gate (#285). The forward invariant (every value in `EMOJI_SHORTCODE_TO_UNICODE` is in `TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_REACTIONS`) was already enforced by `src/channels/telegram-emoji.test.ts` post-#161, and the WARN log on shortcode misses lives at `sendReaction`'s allowed-reactions gate. The remaining gap was the OTHER direction: when Telegram bumped its Bot API and added new allowed reactions, the shortcode map didn't grow in lockstep — those new emoji became unreachable from agents emitting shortcode form (every `react_to_message` call passes through `normalizeReactionEmoji`'s shortcode lookup), and the existing forward invariant didn't catch that because the forward direction is "shortcode → allowed", not "allowed → has-shortcode." A new `_TELEGRAM_ALLOWED_REACTIONS` `@internal` export from `src/channels/telegram.ts` plus an inverse-audit block in `src/channels/telegram-emoji.test.ts` builds a reverse index over `_EMOJI_SHORTCODE_TO_UNICODE` and asserts each of the 73 entries in the allowed set has at least one shortcode pointing at it. Test runs as part of `npm test` which is the existing CI gate per `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, so any future Bot API bump that adds an allowed reaction without a corresponding shortcode addition fails the PR. Each per-Unicode failure message names the missing emoji and points the operator at `src/channels/telegram.ts`'s `EMOJI_SHORTCODE_TO_UNICODE` map for the fix. The current state already satisfies the invariant — no production fix required, just locking in the contract for future changes. Closes #285.

- Container-runner reliability bundle (#407, cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#94` + `#96` + `#101`).
