Three sequential reliability fixes that landed on Leonid's main on 2026-05-01 — all tighten the orchestrator's filesystem and SQLite handling against partial-state failures. **#94** makes `createFilteredDb` atomic via temp+rename: writing the schema directly to the canonical path means any interruption (SIGTERM, OOM, fs hiccup, throw mid ATTACH/CTAS) leaves a 0-byte or partial-header file there; subsequent spawns hit `disk I/O error` and the group permanently wedges behind the circuit breaker. The fix writes to a sibling temp file and `renameSync`s on success — POSIX-atomic within the same filesystem; the canonical path flips from `absent or stale` → `complete` with no observable midpoint. Inner `try { ATTACH+CTAS+DETACH } finally { dst.close() }` ensures the connection always closes even on partial failure; outer try cleans up the partial temp file before rethrowing. **#96** generalises the same atomicity to `groupTesslDir` publishing — new `atomicPublishDir(srcDir, dstDir)` in `src/container-runner.ts` does temp+swap-rename: rename `dstDir` → `dstDir.bak.<pid>.<ts>`, rename `srcDir` → `dstDir`, then `rmBestEffort(backup)`. `RACE_CODES = new Set(['EEXIST', 'ENOTEMPTY', 'EPERM', 'EACCES', 'ENOENT'])` and `rmBestEffort` are lifted to module scope so spawnContainer's per-group tessl publish path can call them. ENOENT is in the race-code set because two concurrent callers can race on the `existsSync` → `renameSync` window: the loser's check sees `dstDir` exists, but its rename hits ENOENT because the winner already moved it; same race as EEXIST/ENOTEMPTY, just sampled at a finer-grained tick. Per `coding-policy: error-handling`, `rmBestEffort` and the restore-from-backup path use typed `NodeJS.ErrnoException` catches and WARN-log unexpected failures so real filesystem drift surfaces instead of being silently swallowed. **#101** adds source-WAL auto-recovery on top of #94 — when ATTACH/CTAS throws `disk I/O error` (the symptom of a degraded source `-shm` region per `ligolnik#100`), the orchestrator opens a fresh connection to the source `messages.db`, runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` (re-establishes the SHM region), and retries `attemptCreate()` exactly once. New helpers `recoverSourceWalState(srcDb)` and `isDiskIoError(err)` (matches both typed `SqliteError` and generic `Error.message.includes('disk I/O error')` defensively). Non-disk-I/O errors propagate immediately — checkpoints can't fix a schema bug. Retry-collision defeated by `randomBytes(4).toString('hex')` suffix on the temp path. New `synchronous = NORMAL` pragma on the snapshot connection. **Conflict resolution.** All three PRs build on the same `createFilteredDb` body; canonical's pre-#287 `-wal`/`-shm` sidecar wipe loop, `srcHasReactions` explicit `sqlite_master` probe (instead of bare `try/catch`), and the `MAINTENANCE_CONTAINER_TIMEOUT` / `readEnvFileAll` / `DEFAULT_AGENT_MODEL` / `usage` / `addressedToUs` / `createdByRole` features are all preserved. Helper functions are added at module scope without disturbing existing exports. Tests: 2116 / 2116 passing host-side (was 2055 on `main`; +4 covering the disk-I/O retry happy path, retry succeeds, retry-also-fails propagation, non-disk-I/O propagates).

- Quarterly precheck-gating audit replay procedure + deterministic snapshotter (#375). Closes the #338 audit's "snapshot-in-time" weakness: new recurring tasks added between audits could slip in without prechecks (5/6 tasks were missing one at first audit, the failure mode that motivated #338 in the first place). New `src/audit-precheck-gating.ts` exports `runAuditSnapshot({ dbPath, windowDays?, now? })` — selects every `scheduled_tasks` row with `status='active' AND schedule_type IN ('cron','interval')`, joins each with `task_run_logs` stats over a window ending now and starting `windowDays` back (default 90 — quarterly cadence: cron-weekly tasks need ≥4 fires for empirical confidence on duration drift). Returns a deterministic JSON snapshot with `{snapshot_at, window_start, window_end, window_days, tasks: [{task_id, group_folder, schedule_type, schedule_value, has_precheck, script, fires, gated_likely, avg_duration_s, min_duration_s, max_duration_s}, ...]}`. `gated_likely` is a heuristic populated from a 10-second `duration_ms` threshold — NOT ground truth (real gated runs share the short-duration signature with quick-failing runs); the replay agent applies judgment when diffing. `runAuditSnapshot` opens the DB read-only, throws actionable errors on missing path / non-positive `windowDays`, and orders tasks deterministically by `group_folder`/`schedule_type`/`task_id` so quarter-over-quarter diffs don't churn on row order. CLI shim at `scripts/audit-precheck-gating.ts` (thin wrapper, `--db` and `--days` flags) so the operator can reproduce the snapshot from the NAS via `npx tsx scripts/audit-precheck-gating.ts`. New `docs/precheck-gating-audit-replay.md` documents the six-step procedure for the scheduled agent: snapshot → diff against committed audit doc → pick output mode by diff size (0 changes silent, 1–2 in-place PR, ≥3 new dated doc) → file follow-up issues for any new task without a precheck → open PR → finish. Test surface: `src/audit-precheck-gating.test.ts` (9 tests, tmpdir + better-sqlite3 fixtures) covers the SQL filter (cancelled/once excluded), `has_precheck` reflection, in-window vs out-of-window aggregation, zero-fire window, `--days` override, ISO-8601 second-precision timestamps, missing-DB error path, non-positive `windowDays` validation, and deterministic ordering. The audit-replay agent itself is registered separately via `/schedule cron='0 6 1 1,4,7,10 *' prompt='follow docs/precheck-gating-audit-replay.md, open a PR'` — keeps the cron prompt one short instruction so methodology refinements ride normal in-tree PR review instead of re-registering the agent. The /schedule registration step is operator-side because the skill requires claude.ai (not API) authentication. Closes #375 (the snapshotter + procedure half); `/schedule` registration is a one-liner the operator runs once.

- `registered_groups.trigger_pattern` migrated to JSON `TriggerPatternConfig` schema (#407, cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#84` + `#87` follow-up). Foundation for the Stage 1 host-side gate framework (#86) and the Stage 2 Haiku classifier (#89). Adds `TriggerPattern` / `TriggerPatternConfig` / `TriggerPatternSource` / `TriggerPatternKind` types in `src/types.ts`; idempotent backfill migration in `createSchema` converts every legacy string row into a single-element config with smart kind-classification (`@bare` → `mention` with `@` stripped; everything else stays `keyword` verbatim) and `source: "owner-set"`. Dual-mode read path: `parseTriggerPatternColumn` accepts both shapes so a rollback to a pre-#84 binary then forward again re-converges cleanly. Malformed JSON falls back to treating the raw string as a literal keyword (warn-log + continue). A future-version payload (`{"version": 2, ...}` read by a v1 binary) is handled distinctly: warn-log loudly with `jid + foundVersion + expectedVersion` and return `config: null, trigger: null` — the gate combinator falls open at that boundary instead of silently re-interpreting the JSON as a literal keyword. Either way, startup cannot crash on a single bad row. `RegisteredGroup.trigger` is now `string | null` with locked-down derivation semantics in `deriveTriggerString`: first keyword-kind entry → first mention-kind entry with `@` re-prepended → `null`. Four call sites (`processGroupMessages`, `canSenderInteract`, `startMessageLoop`, `triggerHit` for media + voice) coerce `null` to `undefined` so `getTriggerPattern` falls back to the global `@<assistant>` default — locked-in behaviour for #82-style mention-only / regex-only configs. Wrong-version rows warn-log loudly with jid + foundVersion + expectedVersion and return `config: null, trigger: null` (loud-but-non-fatal — the trigger gate falls open at the combinator instead of "row matches nothing forever until re-rolled-forward"). New helpers `getTriggerPatterns(jid)` / `setTriggerPatterns(jid, config)` are the surface #82's self-improvement loop will read/write through. Conflict resolution: canonical's `telegram_message_id` migration (#159) and `requiresTrigger === undefined` NULL semantics (#105) are preserved alongside the JSON migration; the auto-react host-side reaction logic that #87 modified was already removed in canonical (#289 reaction-locality), so the parts of #87 touching `telegram.ts` voice/text auto-react paths drop cleanly. The sender-allowlist clause that #87 reintroduced in `index.ts` was removed in canonical (#145); kept the `?? undefined` coerce for compatibility with the new nullable `trigger` field. +25 tests covering migration, idempotence, dual-mode read, malformed-JSON fallback, helper round-trip, write-while-other-columns-untouched, deriveTriggerString matrix (keyword-only, mention-only, mention-with-leading-@, regex-only, sender_tier-only, mixed combinations), end-to-end mention-only round trip, version 2/3 row handling. Behaviour-equivalent at the matcher today; eliminates a retroactive reclassification step for the self-improvement loop.

- Identity preamble injection for agent containers (#407, cherry-pick of `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public#90` + cross-fork config-export port). The `nanoclaw-core` 0.1.94 tile bump introduced `@AyeAye` / `@AyeAyeSureBot` as the canonical example for the dual-handle pattern. In untrusted-tier deployments where the bot's persona files don't explicitly state its identity, the agent has been observed templating itself from the example handles — claiming `@AyeAye` as its own identity instead of its configured one. Live repro: a LoMBot deployment (`@limlombot`) reading the canonical example as gospel. Two-part structural fix: (1) `src/container-runner.ts` forwards `ASSISTANT_NAME` and `ASSISTANT_USERNAME` to every agent container via `-e` (alongside the existing `AGENT_MODEL` / `AGENT_EFFORT` — these are not secrets, no env-file plumbing needed); (2) `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts` exports two pure functions, `buildIdentityPreamble(name, username)` (renders an authoritative identity preamble that explicitly marks `@AyeAye` / `@AyeAyeSureBot` as `FICTIONAL EXAMPLE` and frames itself as authoritative-by-orchestrator so it outranks tile-rule examples) and `resolveIdentityPreamble(name, username)` (env-var-aware wrapper, returns `undefined` when either input is missing or empty so the caller can log a skip notice — emitting a half-formed preamble like "You are **undefined**" would be worse than none). The preamble is prepended to `appendParts` BEFORE `SOUL.md` and global `CLAUDE.md` so it sits at the top of the appended system prompt. `src/config.ts` adds the `ASSISTANT_USERNAME` export (read from `process.env.ASSISTANT_USERNAME` → `envConfig.ASSISTANT_USERNAME` → `ASSISTANT_NAME.toLowerCase()` as the safe default) and registers it in the `readEnvFile([…])` whitelist; this was a config-export divergence from `ligolnik/nanoclaw-public/main` that needed to be ported alongside the cherry-pick. `src/container-runner.auto-compact.test.ts`'s `vi.mock('./config.js', …)` adds the two new mock fields so the threshold-nuke flag-on tests don't fail with `No "ASSISTANT_NAME" export is defined on the "./config.js" mock`. Test coverage: 12 tests across `container/agent-runner/src/index.identity-preamble.test.ts` (10 in the source PR — substitution correctness, FICTIONAL EXAMPLE framing, authoritative/orchestrator framing, cross-test name leak isolation, every missing/empty env-var permutation; +1 added in this PR addressing open-note #1 from #407 — anchors the "or any other bot name" generalization so future tile-rule introductions of new fictional handles are still treated as substitute-mentally examples) plus a spawn-args assertion in `src/container-runner.test.ts` (matching mock fixtures `LoMBot` / `limlombot`, the live repro from the source chat). Manual operator action required after merge: `./scripts/deploy.sh` rebuilds the agent image so the preamble actually injects on next message; the orchestrator change alone won't take effect without an agent image rebuild. Closes #407.

- Telegram sanitizer Phase 1b stray-tag regex widened to cover namespaced, dotted, Unicode-letter, and digit-leading tag-shaped tokens (#284). Pre-fix char class `[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9_-]*` rejected `:`, `.`, every non-ASCII letter, and any digit-first token, so shapes like `<svg:rect>`, `<x.y>`, Cyrillic `<язык>`, and `<3D>` slipped past Phase 1b entirely, reached Telegram unescaped, and triggered a 400 + plain-text fallback (the user saw the raw `<…>` markers and any Markdown formatting on the same message was lost). Post-fix class is `[\p{L}\p{N}][\p{L}\p{N}_.:-]*` with the `u` flag — same `protectStray` + Phase-3 escape path as the underscored-tag handling already in place. Five new tests in `src/channels/telegram-sanitize.test.ts`: four positive cases (`<svg:rect>`, `<x.y>`, `<язык>`, and `<3D>` each escape to entities) and one negative case (math expression `<a < b>` correctly escapes brackets without over-protecting — the existing `(?:\s[^>]*)?` attribute-blob clause swallows the inner `<`, which is the desired outcome regardless of whether we call it a tag, since unescaped `<`/`>` would confuse Telegram's HTML parser anyway). Out of scope for this PR and not handled here: leading-punctuation shapes (`<!DOCTYPE>`, `<?xml ?>`, `<-2>`) — these slip past for the same reason a bare `<` does and warrant a follow-up audit, not a mid-PR scope expansion. Closes #284.

- `sqlite3` CLI added to the orchestrator image. `src/db-dump.ts` (dump logic originally introduced in #398 as `scripts/dump-state-tables.ts`, then moved into `src/db-dump.ts` and integrated into the `github_backup` IPC handler in #397) shells out to `sqlite3 .dump <table>` per state-table; `Dockerfile.orchestrator` was installing build tools for the `better-sqlite3` native binding (`python3 make g++`) but never the CLI binary itself, so every nightly housekeeping run that reached `runDumpPlan` threw `dump-state-tables: sqlite3 CLI not on PATH` at the preflight, the IPC handler's `case 'github_backup'` failed closed with `{ stage: 'sync' }`, and the `backup-repo` `git diff --cached --quiet` plumbing never ran — pipeline still silently no-op'd post-#397, just at a different layer of the stack. One-line fix: append `sqlite3` to the existing `apt-get install -y` list in the Docker image. Same `apt-get update` / `rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*` block as the other system deps so no extra image layers and no measurable image-size growth. Once this image is rebuilt and `./scripts/deploy.sh` re-runs, the next `mcp__nanoclaw__github_backup` IPC call goes green: `runDumpPlan` writes `<table>.sql` per state-table, `syncBackupRepo` reports non-zero `dumped` / `copied` counts, the bash-driven `git add -A && git diff --cached --quiet` finds non-empty diffs, and the commit-and-push cycle runs as designed.

- backup-repo origin-URL cleanup + GITHUB*TOKEN documented in `.env.example` (#399). New `scripts/clean-backup-repo-origins.sh` walks `groups/*/backup-repo`and rewrites only origin URLs that carry an embedded credential — matched via`://[^/@]+:[^/@]+@`— to the clean form`https://github.com/jbaruch/nanoclaw.git`, counting `changed` / `already_clean` / `no_remote` / `no_repo` / `unexpected_non_clean` outcomes per group. Narrow on purpose: a non-credential URL that happens to differ from the clean form (e.g. an operator-set SSH remote `git@github.com:jbaruch/nanoclaw.git`, or a fork mirror) is reported as `unexpected_non_clean` and LEFT ALONE — the script's intent is to scrub embedded credentials, not to enforce a single canonical URL as policy. The token only needs to exist at runtime now — the IPC handler's `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0` `insteadOf` injection (preserved from the original handler) substitutes it ephemerally for the duration of the `git push`, so no group's `.git/config` carries the secret on disk anymore. Idempotent (re-runs on a clean tree are no-ops), with `--dry-run` for previewing; arg parsing is strict (rejects unknown flags and extra positional args with exit 2) so a typoed `--dryrun` doesn't silently fall through to a real rewrite. The current URL is printed with the password component redacted (`https://x-access-token:<redacted>@...`) per `jbaruch/coding-policy: no-secrets` so a tmux scrollback or terminal log of this script's output never carries a live token. Acceptance per the issue body: post-run, `git -C groups/<group>/backup-repo remote -v` returns the clean URL with no token, and `grep -rn "github_pat*" /home/jbaruch/nanoclaw`only hits`.env`. `.env.example`now splits into a CI-only-secrets block (the existing`OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`TESSL_TOKEN`consumed only by`.github/workflows/`) and a runtime-secrets block; `GITHUB_TOKEN` joins the runtime block with a fine-grained-PAT scope hint (`Contents: write`on`jbaruch/nanoclaw`only) and a`https://github.com/settings/personal-access-tokens/new` creation link. The runtime / CI partition makes the security model legible for a new maintainer: anything in the CI block flows into GitHub Actions secrets via the link in the file header; anything in the runtime block lives only in `.env` on the NAS and the IPC handler that reads it. **Manual operator action required**: rotate the broken PAT on github.com → Personal access tokens (the current token started returning `Invalid username or token` on `git fetch --dry-run` per the issue body, so backup pushes have been failing in addition to the sync gap fixed by #397), put the new value into `.env` on the NAS, and re-deploy via `./scripts/deploy.sh`. The cleanup script and the rotation are both prerequisites for the github_backup pipeline returning to health: #397's sync produces non-empty diffs again, then a fresh PAT lets the push land. Closes #399; with #397 and #398 merged, the github_backup pipeline is fully back online once the operator rotates the token.

- Source-data sync into `backup-repo/` before commit + structured `github_backup` result envelope (#397). New `src/backup-sync.ts` exports `syncBackupRepo({ groupDir, backupDir, dbPath })` which copies live group state into the backup tree before the existing `git add -A && git diff --cached --quiet` plumbing gets a chance to declare "nothing to commit." Concretely it (a) overwrites `backup-repo/MEMORY.md` and `backup-repo/daily_discoveries.md` from their group-folder counterparts when the source files exist, (b) mirrors `groups/<group>/memory/` into `backup-repo/memory/` with delete-on-missing semantics so files that no longer exist in the source are removed from the dest (git history retains the older state — the working tree should match the live one), preserving nested subdirectories, and (c) calls `runDumpPlan` from the relocated `src/db-dump.ts` to write `<table>.sql` for every table in `STATE_TABLES` that's present in the DB. Source `memory/` missing leaves the dest untouched (don't blow away historical content just because the source disappeared on the wrong day); the source/backup-repo dirs missing throws an actionable error naming the missing path. The IPC handler at `src/ipc.ts` (`case 'github_backup'`) now invokes `syncBackupRepo` BEFORE the bash-driven git plumbing, fails closed if sync throws (writes `{ error, stage: 'sync' }` to the result file and skips the git step rather than committing a half-synced tree), and merges the `SyncResult` into the success envelope as `sync_summary: { copied, removed, dumped, skipped }` so downstream consumers (the nightly-housekeeping skill specifically) can detect anomalies without parsing prose. The bash command's emitted JSON now also carries an explicit `committed: boolean` field — `{"committed":false,"stdout":"Nothing to commit."}` vs `{"committed":true,"stdout":"Committed and pushed."}` — so a future "warn on N consecutive empty commits" pass in Step 19 of nightly-housekeeping has a structured signal to gate on instead of regex-matching the stdout text. The `stage: 'sync' | 'git'` field on error envelopes makes it unambiguous which side of the pipeline failed (sync = file copy / dump; git = stage / commit / push); the OneCLI-driven `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0` token injection from the original handler is preserved unchanged. The structured-logging change at the top of the handler now logs `copied`/`removed`/`dumped`/`skipped` counts at INFO before kicking off git so the operator-visible diagnostic for a quiet run ("synced 0 files, 0 dumps, but committed=false") has signal in `task_run_logs` even before the skill-side warning lands. Refactor: `runDumpPlan` and `STATE_TABLES` moved from `scripts/dump-state-tables.ts` into `src/db-dump.ts` so production code can import them — `tsconfig.json`'s `rootDir: ./src` forbade the script-to-src direction. `scripts/dump-state-tables.ts` is now a thin CLI wrapper around `src/db-dump.ts`, preserving the operator-side ad-hoc invocation path; tests moved from `scripts/dump-state-tables.test.ts` to `src/db-dump.test.ts` with the import retargeted. New `src/backup-sync.test.ts` exercises the sync surface with tmpdir fixtures and a `better-sqlite3`-populated DB: single-file copy + overwrite, single-file source-missing skip, `memory/` mirror with orphan removal, nested-subdirectory preservation, source `memory/` missing leaves dest alone, dumped/skipped table accounting, and the two missing-dir error paths. The Step 19 "warn on consecutive empty commits" heuristic itself lives in the nightly-housekeeping tile-skill (separate repo, separate PR); this change is the structured signal that makes that warning implementable. Origin-URL cleanup and PAT rotation for the broken token live in #399. Closes the source-data-sync half of #397; the github_backup pipeline now stops being a silent no-op the moment this lands.

- SQLite state-table dumps for the github*backup pipeline (#398). New `scripts/dump-state-tables.ts` exports a `STATE_TABLES` constant (the irreplaceable queue/state surface called out in #398 — `orders`, `email_feedback`, `scheduled_reminders`, `email_state`, `email_seen_ids`, `resumable_cycles`, `pending*\_`, `calendar\__`, `phase*completions`, `tz_state`, `follow_me_tasks`, `scheduled_tasks`, `task_run_logs`, plus the trusted-session and orders-metadata tables) plus a `runDumpPlan({ dbPath, outDir, tables? })`entry point that calls`sqlite3 <db> .dump <table>`per table and atomic-writes the resulting SQL text to`<outDir>/<table>.sql`. Atomic-write uses `<file>.tmp`+`renameSync`so a concurrent reader (or a crash mid-write) never observes a half-written dump.`tableExists`short-circuits per-table — tables not present in the DB land in`report.skipped`and write nothing rather than producing empty-shell`.sql`files (typical when a group hasn't run a particular state-NNN migration yet).`.dump` is text-form SQL (`CREATE TABLE`+`INSERT`statements) so the existing`backup-repo` `git add -A && git diff --cached --quiet` pipeline keeps producing meaningful per-day diffs, and a destructive-test replay (`cat <table>.sql | sqlite3 fresh.db`) reconstitutes the exact rows — covered by an explicit round-trip test in `dump-state-tables.test.ts`. Bulk-cache tables (`messages`, `chats`, `reactions`, `sessions`, `smart_home_events`) are intentionally excluded from the default — they're recoverable from upstream sources (Telegram, the SDK runtime, Hubitat) and large enough that buffered `.dump`output through`maxBuffer`would OOM and produce dumps that don't diff usefully; pass`--tables messages`etc. to dump them explicitly. Operator-supplied`--tables`values are validated against`/^[A-Za-z*][A-Za-z0-9_]\*$/`so a pasted-in value containing`;`or`'`aborts the run rather than getting interpreted by the`sqlite3` CLI's command parser. Sqlite3 CLI preflight throws an actionable error message naming the install path per package manager (`apt: sqlite3`, `brew: sqlite`, `Synology: SynoCommunity sqlite3`) when the binary isn't on PATH, rather than letting `spawn ENOENT`leak through. The script lives in`scripts/`alongside`migrate-thin-claude-md.ts`and follows the same shape: typed exports, entry-point guard via`import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(path.resolve(process.argv[1])).href`(the`path.resolve`matters — relative`argv[1]`would otherwise crash`pathToFileURL`), vitest tests with tmpdir fixtures populated via `better-sqlite3`(the project's existing dep) so fixtures are programmatic per the`testing-standards`rule. The IPC handler integration that calls this script before`git add -A` is a separate change (#397) — landing the dump script first decouples review of "does the dump produce restorable SQL" from "does the IPC handler invoke it correctly," and the script is independently useful from the CLI for ad-hoc state extraction. STATE_TABLES is the single point of truth for the irreplaceable surface; future migrations adding new tables append here in the same declaration order as the migration file. Closes #398.

- Two-context split for `Read` of external file paths (#392). New `external-file-summary.ts` in the agent-runner + a `createExternalFileSummaryHook` PreToolUse hook on `Read`. When `SUMMARISE_EXTERNAL_FILES=1` AND the resolved path is outside the workspace mount roots (`/workspace/{group,state,trusted,global,store,ipc}/`), the hook reads the file in the hook process, routes the bytes through `extractStructuredSummary` (the no-tools sub-agent shipped in #367), and DENIES the original `Read` with a multi-line `permissionDecisionReason` carrying the canonical Encoding-B sentinel (so #322's capability-ACL walk-back keeps gating on `file:<path>`) plus the structured digest. The parent agent's transcript carries the digest, never the raw bytes — closing the laundering channel where a poisoned external file (NAS share, user-shared mount, `/tmp` payload) carries hidden prompt-injection text that lands in context even with the destructive-op gates closed. Schema is generic external-file shape: `{ content_kind ('text' | 'code' | 'data' | 'log' | 'binary' | 'other'), detected_format, summary, extracted_fields, contains_links, contains_imperatives }` — every field required so the parent transcript shape is stable; the sub-agent emits empty / false defaults when a field is unknown. The `contains_imperatives` flag surfaces injection-shaped content to the parent without requiring the verbatim text. Path classification reuses #321's `classifyReadPath` (same traversal-defeated normalization) so `/workspace/group/../etc/x` collapses to `/workspace/etc/x` and is correctly classified as external — same definition the existing PostToolUse provenance-sentinel emit uses, so internal/external classification stays in lockstep. Spec correction (recorded in the module header): #392 originally proposed a PostToolUse hook layered on top of the sentinel emit, but the SDK exposes no `tool_response` mutation surface for built-in tools (verified at #321 PR 4) — `additionalContext` adds to the raw output rather than replacing it. PreToolUse interception is the only way to satisfy the issue's acceptance ("the injection string does NOT appear verbatim"). File-read errors (`ENOENT` / `EACCES` / `EISDIR` / `ENOTDIR` / `EPERM` / `ELOOP` / `ENAMETOOLONG`) and summariser failures (timeout, refusal, API error, etc.) pass through — the original Read runs, the SDK produces its canonical error in the first case, and the marker-based ACL gate is the active defence in the second. Unexpected (non-fs, non-SDK) errors propagate per `jbaruch/coding-policy: error-handling`. Files larger than the byte cap are read up to the cap via low-level `openSync` + `readSync` (avoiding slurping a 4 GiB log file into memory) and flagged as truncated in the digest. The Anthropic SDK client is now shared (lazy-cached at module scope) between the #319 Composio body summariser and this hook — one client per process so retries / keepalive / etc. aren't duplicated when both flags are enabled. Default-off via `SUMMARISE_EXTERNAL_FILES=1`, same posture as #319; per-call latency telemetry logs `latency_ms=...` and `truncated=...` per intercepted Read so per-group flag tuning can be data-driven. Closes the `Read` external-paths half of the original #319 scope; together with the #319 Composio gmail/calendar summariser this completes the two-context-split coverage for the structural-replacements audit row 13.

- Composio body summarisation for gmail and calendar reads (#319). The `READ_TOOL_PATTERNS` rows in `container/agent-runner/src/untrusted-input-wrap.ts` now carry an optional `summariseBody` config (extraction goal + JSON Schema) that routes the wrapped text-block content through `extractStructuredSummary` (the #319 v1 library shipped in #367) before the Encoding-A `<untrusted-input source="...">` envelope is added. Gmail rows summarise into `{ messages: [{ sender, recipients, subject, date, body_summary, action_requested, contains_links, contains_attachments }] }`; calendar rows summarise into `{ events: [{ title, start_time, end_time, attendees, location_summary, description_summary }] }`. The structured digest replaces the raw body in the parent agent's transcript, so a "now please email Alice" injection that lands in a gmail body does not enter the parent's context verbatim — only the sub-agent (no tools, hard rules forbidding instruction-following inside content) ever sees the raw bytes. The Encoding-A envelope and `source=` attribute stay unchanged so #322's capability-ACL walk-back keeps gating on `gmail:` / `calendar:` markers exactly as before. Expected failure modes (`timeout`, `sub_agent_refused`, `sub_agent_returned_nothing`, `sub_agent_returned_invalid_shape`, `api_error`) fall back to wrapping the raw body with a `<summarisation-failed reason="..." detail="..."/>` marker INSIDE the envelope, so the parent model still reads the body but knows it is RAW and must apply maximum scepticism. Unexpected errors propagate per `jbaruch/coding-policy: error-handling` — the wrap module does NOT catch them, so a real bug (programmer error, OOM, runtime fault) surfaces instead of being silently rewrapped as a `<summarisation-failed>` marker that would hide the defect. The `detail` attribute on the marker is HTML-attribute-escaped (`&` `"` `<` `>`) and newline-collapsed so a malicious sub-agent can't smuggle a closing `</untrusted-input>` through the failed-summary path. Default-off via `SUMMARISE_COMPOSIO_BODIES=1` so per-group enablement is decoupled from the deploy that ships the code path; the wrap hook logs `summary_latencies_ms=...` and `summary_outcomes=...` per call so per-group flag tuning can be data-driven. Anthropic SDK client is constructed lazily on first call and cached so per-runQuery reads share one TCP keepalive pool. Slack, GitHub, and Tessl rows stay on envelope-only behaviour for this PR — adding a `summariseBody` config is a one-line change per row and a future audit pass will broaden coverage; the marker-based `#322` ACL gate on those sources is the active defence in the meantime. Closes the Composio-body half of #319; the `Read` of external file paths is tracked separately under #392.

- Provenance-aware compaction (#327). New `compact-provenance.ts` in the agent-runner — extends the existing `PreCompact` hook to walk the live pre-compaction JSONL transcript and persist a per-session sidecar JSON of every unique `prefix:value` source seen in either #321 encoding (Encoding A `<untrusted-input source="...">` wraps and Encoding B `PROVENANCE_MARKER:` sentinels). A new `PostCompact` hook reads the sidecar back, deletes it, and emits a `systemMessage` containing one synthetic `PROVENANCE_MARKER:` line per source. The synthetic lines land in the post-compaction transcript as a `system`-role SessionMessage that #322's `capability-acl` walk-back picks up exactly like the canonical pre-compaction sentinels — closing the laundering channel where the SDK summariser strips `<untrusted-input>` wraps and the gates re-open. Boundary semantics preserved: when the operator types a fresh prompt after compaction, the walk-back stops at that boundary and our injected markers are NOT scanned (correct reset — the operator has had a chance to read the summary and sanitise their request); when the model continues autonomously across the boundary without a fresh operator turn, the walk-back scans backward through the synthetic markers and the ACL intersection holds. Sidecar discipline: one file per session id under `/workspace/state/compact-provenance/<sessionId>.json`, written 0600, schema-versioned per `rules/stateful-artifacts.md` (parser returns `null` on shape mismatch, caller treats as "no usable prior state"); PreCompact overwrites, PostCompact deletes after reading, mismatched session ids leave the file in place (defence in depth — the path is keyed on session id, but a stranded file from another session must never be deleted by the wrong owner). Marker extractor walks every text-bearing slot in the JSONL (string `entry.message.content`, `content[N].text` blocks, nested `tool_result.content` arrays of text blocks) via a generic flatten so adding a new content-block type with a `text`-like field requires no change here. Source-cap of 256 unique entries (insertion order — earliest sources, typically the ones the model is most likely to act on, are preserved when a long session blows past the cap; one preserved marker is enough to lock the ACL intersection down). Per-source byte cap of 2 KiB rejects malformed emitters and injection-driven oversized `source=` attributes. Sidecar persistence runs as a separate try block from the archive flow so a corrupt-transcript / archive failure can't cascade into losing the provenance signal (and vice versa). Logging emits source counts only, never source values — per `rules/no-secrets.md` the values can carry signed-URL tokens, gmail msg ids, and file paths. PostCompact failure modes (missing sidecar, corrupt JSON, wrong schema_version, mismatched session id) all return an empty payload silently — the SDK's compaction proceeds normally, just without the post-compaction defence; the gate is a defence-in-depth layer over #322's existing walk-back, not a gate the SDK depends on. Closes the post-compaction half of #318's structural-defense umbrella alongside #322's walk-back; the pre-compaction half is already gated by #321's emitters and #322's ACL.

- Memory read-time staleness reminder (#387). New `memory-staleness-reminder.ts` + PostToolUse hook on `Read` matched by the `^(WebFetch|WebSearch|Read|Bash)$` family. When the resolved target is under `/workspace/trusted/` (excluding the carved-out `quarantine/` subtree), the hook injects a `systemMessage` via `additionalContext` reminding the model that the bytes are a last-seen snapshot, not ground truth — any state-mutating decision (sending messages, scheduling tasks, calling Composio sinks) needs verification against the live source. The reminder rides at the same salience tier as the data, rather than relying on the model recalling the global `RULES.md` _Memory verification_ rule that may have been compacted away or drowned by injected text. Path classification mirrors #321's traversal-defeated normalization so `/workspace/trusted/../../etc/x` doesn't accidentally trip the gate. Complementary to #325's write-time quarantine: #325 prevents a session that touched external content from laundering injected data INTO `/workspace/trusted/`; #387 prevents the model from acting on a `/workspace/trusted/` snapshot AS IF it were authoritative when it reads it. Out of scope for v1: excluding internal harness reads (session-reentry / status reports — both legitimately read MEMORY.md) since PostToolUse has no skill-context awareness; if the reminder proves noisy on the recovery path, a follow-up tightens. `Grep` / `Glob` extension also deferred — those return path/name hits, not content; the staleness vector is content-based.

- Structural-replacements audit row 2 redirected from #386 to #104. Investigation while preparing #386 (post-compaction skill-block reframe) found that the proposed system-reminder rendering hook isn't viable — the Claude Agent SDK doesn't expose a hook surface for mutating injected system-reminders, and reliable detection of compaction-summary skill-block formats from a UserPromptSubmit hook is heuristic-at-best. The actual structural fix is the in-flight `#104 (kill auto-compaction)` work: replacing auto-compaction with threshold-triggered nuke + `## Facts` / `## Reasoning` checkpoint + `session-reentry` on the next turn structurally eliminates the "stale skill block re-executed as a new task" class of incident, because a nuked session has no system-reminders carrying stale skill blocks. #386 is closed as superseded; row 2 of `docs/security/structural-replacements.md` now points at #104.

- Structural-replacements audit at `docs/security/structural-replacements.md` (#328). Catalogs every behavioral injection-defense rule in the harness against its structural backstop — 13 rows mapping `RULES.md`-level prose to PreToolUse hooks, marker emission, ACLs, quarantine paths, and so on. 9 rows DONE (#320, #321 PRs 1–4 + #382, #322, #323, #324, #325, #326), 3 rows OPEN tracked by existing umbrella issues #319 (library-only-pending-consumers), #327 (compaction-summary-provenance), and the kill-auto-compaction work under #104; one newly-filed follow-up #387 for memory read-time staleness reminder; 1 row accepted as residual risk (default-silence inside a triggered group — host-side `requires_trigger` is the structural part; the in-container "model decides not to answer" surface is operator-tuning, not security). The doc closes #328, the structural-replacements audit deliverable under the broader #318 structural-defense umbrella: every behavioral-only rule now has either a structural replacement landed, a tracked open issue, or an explicit residual-risk decision with rationale. Future incidents that name a behavioral rule the model didn't honor under load look up the rule here and either find the structural backstop (then ask "did the gate fire? if not, why not?") or pick up the linked follow-up issue.

- Memory write quarantine for sessions that touched external content (#325). New `memory-quarantine.ts` + 3 hook factories + wiring in `index.ts`. A per-`runQuery` `processedExternalContent` flag flips when (a) the prompt body carries a `<untrusted-input source="...">` wrap (UserPromptSubmit), or (b) any PostToolUse hook emits a #321 marker — the flag-flip reuses #321's existing classifiers (`inferReadSource` for MCP read tools, `inferSentinelSource` for built-ins) so a future emitter prefix flips the flag automatically. PreToolUse on `Write` / `Edit` checks the flag when the target resolves under `/workspace/trusted/` (excluding the carved-out `quarantine/` subtree). When set, `Write` is intercepted: the hook itself persists the model's content to `/workspace/trusted/quarantine/<session-id>/<rel-path>` (sessionId sanitized — alphanumerics + hyphen + underscore only; path-traversal chars collapse to `_`) and denies the original call — the redirect is structural, not dependent on agent cooperation. `Edit` denies hard with a redirect-explainer reason, since surgical-edit semantics don't survive a redirect to a fresh quarantine snapshot. Redirect uses atomic write (temp + rename) so a concurrent promote operation can't observe partial JSON. Failure to write the quarantine target fails closed — original `/workspace/trusted/` write is denied to keep the trust boundary intact, and the operator is asked to fix subtree permissions. Path resolution mirrors #321's traversal-defeated normalization so `/workspace/trusted/../../etc/x` doesn't accidentally trip the gate (path falls outside the prefix; #322 handles it). Out of scope for v1: host-side notification when a quarantine record lands; `nightly-housekeeping` exclusion from weekly summaries; compaction-summary exclusion (rolls under #327). Closes the laundering channel called out in #325's body — webpage → daily log → weekly summary → indistinguishable from operator-typed fact.

- Rate limits on `Task` (sub-agent spawn) and `mcp__nanoclaw__schedule_task` (#323). New PreToolUse hook in the agent-runner — `rate-limits.ts` + wiring in `index.ts` — applies provenance-conditional per-hour caps. Walk-back reuses #322's `walkBackIterableForProvenance`, classifying the call into one of five rows: operator-trusted (50 spawns/hr, 30 schedule_task/hr — audit-log only, never denies), operator-untrusted (5/3), untrusted-source (web/email/scraped → 2/1), cross-group from non-owner (2/1), or mixed chain (any untrusted link → 2/1). The `__unknown__` sentinel from #322's classifier collapses to `untrusted-source` so a future emitter prefix can't silently bypass the gate. Counter file at `/workspace/state/rate-limit-counters.json` is group-scoped — an injection in one chat can't poison another chat's counters; rolling-window pruning bounds file size by the highest cap (operator-trusted: 50 spawns/hr). Atomic write (temp-file + `renameSync`) keeps concurrent readers from observing partial JSON. Loader fails open on validation errors (denying every spawn from a corrupt file is worse than losing the counter; per-hour cap means the new window starts immediately). Optional operator-tightening file at `/workspace/trusted/rate-limit-overrides.json` lets the operator lower any row's caps or flip the trusted-operator row from audit-only to enforced; non-trusted rows can't be flipped TO audit-only via override (would defeat the gate), and negative cap values clamp to the default rather than silently disabling. Override file is host-only-writable, mirroring #320 / #324. Out of scope for v1: recursion-depth tracking on Agent spawn (needs SDK-level depth propagation), global ceilings across groups (host-side coordination), per-chat active-task and cron-recurring caps (per-hour rate caps already gate the fork-bomb case).

- Composio outbound argument validator (#326). New PreToolUse hook in the agent-runner — `composio-arg-validator.ts` + wiring in `index.ts` — gates `mcp__composio__gmail_(send|reply)*` and `mcp__composio__slack_(post|send)*` on per-field shape constraints before bytes leave the container. Header fields (`subject`, `to`, `cc`, `bcc`, `recipient_email` for gmail; `channel`, `user` for slack) reject `\r`/`\n` and cap at the RFC 5322 998-byte line limit so a model can't synthesize header injection (`subject = "Hi\n\nBcc: attacker@evil.com"`). Body fields (`body` for gmail, `text` for slack) cap at 100 KB measured as UTF-8 bytes (not chars — multi-byte content can't slip past a chars-based limit) and reject ASCII control chars except `\t` and `\n`; `\r` is in the reject set because CRLF in a body is the common header-spoof pattern. Array variants of `to`/`cc`/`bcc` are validated element-by-element so an injection can't ride in via the array shape when the scalar shape is gated. Wrong-type values (number where string expected) deny with `wrong_type`. Tool-only — does not look at provenance, since header injection and oversize bodies are wrong regardless of who issued the call (operator pays the same gate; the operator can never legitimately produce a `\n` in `subject`). Runs BEFORE #320's egress allowlist so a malformed arg is rejected without paying the transcript-walk cost. Logged-deny field is the field name only, never the value, per `no-secrets` (body / recipient strings carry tokens or PII). Composio read tools, github mutations, and calendar mutations pass through unchanged — adding a row to `TOOL_RULES` is a one-line change when a new outbound surface appears. Symmetric counterpart to #117's incoming-result sanitizer.

- Provenance wrap coverage extended to `WebSearch` and Tessl registry read tools (#321). The Encoding B sentinel in `provenance-sentinel.ts` now emits `web:<query>` for `WebSearch` (search results are external web bytes — same prefix and #322 ACL row as `WebFetch`), and the PostToolUse matcher in `index.ts` includes `WebSearch` alongside `WebFetch | Read | Bash`. The Encoding A read-tool allowlist in `untrusted-input-wrap.ts` now wraps `mcp__tessl__(search|outdated|query_library_docs)` results with a `tessl:<action>` source — registry tile search, remote version checks, and library-doc queries return external bytes that need to flow through #322's walk-back the same way Composio Gmail/Calendar/Slack/GitHub reads do. The mcp prefix-strip helper in `inferReadSource` now generalizes to `^mcp__[^_]+__` so server-specific prefixes are removed regardless of MCP server, while the emitted Tessl `source=` value remains `tessl:<action>` (for example, `tessl:search`). Tessl mutating verbs (`install`, `login`, `update`, `uninstall`, `new_tile`) and the local-only `status` stay un-wrapped by omission. Closes the third-party-MCP and built-in-search gaps called out in #321's body.

- Destructive-op confirmation tokens (#324). New PreToolUse hook in the agent-runner — `confirmation-tokens.ts` + wiring in `index.ts` — gates destructive NanoClaw MCP tools (`nuke_session`, `nuke_chat`, `github_backup`, `set_trusted`, `set_trigger`, `register_group`, `unregister_group`, `promote_staging`, `push_staged_to_branch`, `tessl_update`) on a host-issued OOB token. Provenance-conditional per #318: operator-originated trusted chains pass with the existing in-chat confirmation; chains carrying any untrusted-provenance link require an unspent, scope-matching, unexpired token consumed atomically on use. Tokens are minted by a host-side `scripts/aye-confirm.ts` CLI (`--scope <scope> --reason <text> --ttl 5m`), single-use, default 5-minute TTL, stored at `/workspace/trusted/aye_confirm_tokens.json`. The token file is host-only — a companion PreToolUse hook denies Write/Edit/Bash mutations of the path so an injection-driven chain in trusted/main can't forge tokens; legitimate consumption writes bypass via an env-var sentinel set only for the duration of the hook's own atomic save. Atomic write (temp-file + `renameSync`) keeps concurrent readers from observing partial JSON. Loader validates every token record's shape (required fields, types, parseable ISO timestamps) so a manually-edited `expires_at: 'zzzz'` can't lexicographically authorize a destructive op — invalid records throw hard, gate fails closed. Failure posture for destructive ops is fail-CLOSED: transcript-read errors (`ENOENT` / `SyntaxError` during pre-init / mid-flush) deny with a "retry once readable" reason rather than allowing, since allowing every destructive call during transcript-read races would be a bypass. Token logging emits scope + expires_at only, never the token value (per `no-secrets`).
- Precheck-gating audit landed at `docs/precheck-gating-audit.md` (#338). Catalogues every active recurring scheduled task in production against a 14-day window (`[2026-04-17T02:00Z, 2026-05-01T02:00Z)`) and finds that of six recurring tasks, five have no precheck script at all and the sixth (heartbeat) has one whose gate was disabled in-place on 2026-04-19 for email-latency reasons — so effectively zero recurring fires are currently being gated out. The doc walks through the gate mechanism (script content is written to `/tmp/task-script.sh` inside the container and run via `bash`; the agent-runner short-circuits at `container/agent-runner/src/index.ts:2837-2846` both on `wakeAgent === false` AND when the script errors / produces no parsable JSON; gated runs still write a `task_run_logs` row with short `duration_ms`), gives per-task analysis with concrete recommended gate inputs (e.g. for morning-brief: gate on calendar / email / pending-undated / cleanup / decisions counts), and proposes architectural alternatives for the heartbeat (split Composio fetch from the gateable conditions; or fetch Composio inline in the precheck and write events to a state file before gating out) so email latency and precheck gating can both win. Follow-up issues are tracked separately for each tightening; this PR ships the audit document only — implementation lands per-task in subsequent PRs.
- Egress allowlist for outbound communication (#320). New PreToolUse hook in the agent-runner — `egress-allowlist.ts` + wiring in `index.ts` — gates Composio `gmail.send` / `slack.post` and `mcp__nanoclaw__send_message_to_chat` against an operator-managed allowlist at `/workspace/trusted/egress_allowlist.json`. Provenance-conditional per #318: operator-originated trusted chains bypass by default (the in-chat confirmation IS the confirmation); chains carrying any untrusted-provenance link (web/email/scraped/cross-group, per #321 markers walked by #322) require the destination to match an allowlist entry. Multi-recipient gmail calls require every recipient to pass; domain match is suffix-style on `@<domain>` and case-insensitive. Operator opt-in tightening via `enforce_for_operator: true` enforces the allowlist even on operator-originated chains. The capability ACL from #322 was adjusted in lockstep — outbound sinks are now structurally permitted on every untrusted-source row except `file:` (which keeps no-outbound posture), so the destination filter can take over rather than blanket-deny everywhere. Companion PreToolUse hook denies Write/Edit/Bash mutations of the allowlist file path so an injection-driven chain in trusted/main can't add an attacker-controlled destination before sending; mutations are operator-only via host-side direct edit (and per #324 will require the OOB confirmation token once that lands). Allowlist loader validates per-field shapes at load time — operator typos like `allowed_channels: '#general'` (string instead of array) drop with `console.warn` rather than crashing every outbound call. Failure posture is provenance-aware: `ENOENT` / `SyntaxError` on transcript-read or allowlist-load passes through under operator-trusted (with log) but fails CLOSED under untrusted-provenance — a corrupt allowlist under attack conditions never silently widens the gate.
- Two-context split library scaffolding (#319 v1, library-only). New `structured-summary.ts` in the agent-runner exports `extractStructuredSummary(req)` — runs raw external bytes through a no-tools sub-agent and returns ONLY a structured-JSON summary, so the parent agent never sees the raw text. Sub-agent contract enforced: no tools beyond the wrapper's `emit_summary` tool_use, schema gets `additionalProperties: false` by default (recursively, on nested object subschemas / array items / anyOf-oneOf-allOf variants), system prompt explicitly forbids following instructions in the content / echoing identifiers / adding fields outside the schema. The free-form `source.identifier` is moved out of the system prompt into the user message labeled "data only" and sanitized (newlines collapsed, length-capped at 256 chars) so an attacker-controlled URL or message id can't influence the highest-priority prompt layer. Default model: Haiku 4.5. Default timeout: 30s. Default input cap: 200 KB (trimmed by exact byte count via `Buffer.subarray`, not a chars/4 heuristic). Failure surface returns structured `{ kind: 'error', reason }` for `timeout` / `sub_agent_returned_nothing` / `sub_agent_returned_invalid_shape` / `sub_agent_refused` / `api_error`; the catch is narrowed to expected SDK error shapes (`Anthropic.APIError` + `{ status: number, error }` duck-type for cross-module-boundary cases) and `AbortError` — anything else propagates per `error-handling` policy. The helper returns audit metadata (`inputBytes`, `truncated`) but does NOT log on its own; callers manage logging and must keep raw bytes out of logs (`no-secrets` — external content can carry tokens). `SummarySourceKind` derives from #321's `SourcePrefix` taxonomy via `Exclude<…, 'untrusted-container' | 'cross-group'>` so the type stays in sync. Added `@anthropic-ai/sdk` as an explicit direct dep with the lockfile updated. NOT YET INTEGRATED — agent-browser, check-email, check-calendar refactor lands in follow-up PRs because (a) agent-browser is a Bash subcommand and the SDK exposes no `tool_response` mutation surface for built-in tools (verified at #321 PR 2 / PR 4), and (b) check-email / check-calendar are not currently discrete skills in `container/skills/` — their flows go through Composio MCP tools whose results are wrapped by #321 PR 2, and integrating the summarizer means modifying the existing PostToolUse wrap hook to optionally summarize before wrapping, which is a meaningful behavior change worth its own PR.
- Recurring scheduled tasks (cron / interval) now reuse the same SDK `session_id` across fires (#336). Cross-window cost analysis traced ~76% of total `cache_create` tokens (~27M of ~35M across 119 queries) to fresh-session-per-fire — every heartbeat, housekeeping, and morning-brief landed a full system-prompt cache_create even though the message-history prefix could have been API-cached. Anthropic's prompt cache has a 5-minute TTL, so a 15-30-minute heartbeat cadence always misses it; the win comes from the API's per-session message-history caching, which persists across that window when the session id is reused — `cache_create` becomes incremental rather than full-prefix. Implementation: new nullable `scheduled_tasks.session_id` column populated by `runTask` on first fire, passed as `resume:` on every subsequent fire (eligibility: `schedule_type !== 'once'` — one-shot reminders stay fresh-per-fire and are out of scope per #336). The #193 cross-task bleed concern doesn't reappear because persistence is keyed on `task_id` rather than the shared `MAINTENANCE_SESSION_NAME` slot cache: two distinct tasks land in two distinct rows hence two distinct SDK sessions on disk; nothing aliases. Disk hygiene is preserved: the post-run `wipeSessionJsonl` finally now keeps the live session id (next fire needs it) but still wipes orphans (any id the SDK rotated through mid-run plus the pre-existing column value when a rotation happened). `nukeSession` extends to clear `session_id` for every scheduled task in the affected group when the maintenance (or `'all'`) slot is wiped — without this the next fire would `resume:` an id whose JSONL no longer exists and the SDK would 404 / start fresh anyway, just noisily. The schema's pre-existing `context_mode` column stays inert (per #193's note "retained on the schema for future use"); #336's gating is on `schedule_type` instead. Acceptance target from the issue: 3-5x reduction in per-fire `cache_create` for recurring tasks; verifiable via the `session_tokens` telemetry shipped in #349 by aggregating `cache_creation` per `taskSkill` over a 24h before/after window.
- Per-turn `session_tokens` telemetry now emits from the scheduled-task path, not just inbound-message processing (#349, follows #344 / #337). The shared emitter lives in `src/usage-telemetry.ts → emitSessionTokens(...)` and preserves the inbound-side log key (`session_tokens` / `session_tokens_warn` / `session_tokens_nuke`), `threshold_state` classification, percent-of-window math, and field shape verbatim. `src/task-scheduler.ts:runTask` now calls the helper from its `onOutput` callback so every assistant-turn `usage` payload from a cron / interval / once fire lands in host-logs alongside inbound data. The scheduled-task variant carries `taskId`, `scheduleType` (`cron` / `interval` / `once`), and `taskSkill` (parsed from the prompt's `Skill(skill: "tessl__X")` invocation when present, undefined for raw-text reminders). The raw prompt text is intentionally NOT logged — scheduled-task prompts can include user-authored reminders containing PII, so the telemetry sticks to fixed identifiers (`taskId` for bucketing, `taskSkill` for human-readable bucket labels on maintenance fires); operators who need to see what a row's prompt actually was can join against `scheduled_tasks` by `taskId`. `session` is intentionally undefined on the scheduled-task variant (#193: scheduled fires never persist a sessionId). The inbound path's `thresholdReached` latch + post-run nuke handshake stays unchanged — scheduled fires don't trigger that path because each fire is already a single-turn discardable session by #193.
- Silence the observer's progress ladder on non-addressed inbounds (#289 follow-up to #310 / #313). Post-deploy regression in `Old.wtf` (multi-bot, `requires_trigger=false`): 👀 stayed silent on bystander chatter as intended, but the _observer's_ commit-gated `🤔 / ⚡ / ✍ / 🤝` ladder still fired the moment the agent reasoned and committed (which it does for every inbound in a `requires_trigger=false` room). The user clarified the goal was "reactions in untriggered groups limited to relevant discussions" — meaning the entire reaction trail, not just the 👀 ack. Fix: agent-runner now emits `addressed=true|false|-` on the `Query input:` log line (resolved from per-pipe `latestPipedAddressedToUs ?? containerInput.addressedToUs`); observer.ts parses it into `QueryState.addressed` and short-circuits `updateReaction` when explicitly `false`, plus skips `startWatchdog` so the blink-emoji interval doesn't even arm for non-addressed queries. `undefined` (legacy log lines / scheduled tasks) falls through to the existing engagement-gate alone, preserving backward compat. Cleanup: with the observer pinning to `target_message_id` from every Query input log line, the host-side `noteLatestUserMessage` seeding in `src/channels/telegram.ts` and the matching `latestUserMessage` fallback map in `src/observer.ts` were strictly redundant — both removed (host-side telegram message handlers now do nothing extra; observer's `updateReaction` reads `state?.targetMessageId` only). Resulting net effect on `requires_trigger=false` multi-bot rooms: zero reactions on bystander messages (👀 + progress ladder all silent); addressed messages get the full sequence as before.
- React-first per-pipe addressedness (#289 follow-up to #310). After the pivot landed, post-deploy testing in `Old.wtf` showed an `@AyeAye` reply (msg 3425) failed to receive 👀 even though the agent engaged. Root cause: the orchestrator pipes follow-up inbounds into already-running containers via `queue.sendMessage`, but the agent-runner's react-first hook was reading the spawn-time `containerInput.addressedToUs` flag — which was set from the message that originally spawned the container (a non-addressed bystander message in `requires_trigger=false` chats), not the new piped message. So a fresh addressed reply landing on a busy container inherited the stale "not-addressed" flag and got skipped. Fix: extend `queue.sendMessage` to carry per-pipe `addressedToUs` (computed via the same `isAddressedToUs` helper as the spawn path), persist it as `addressedToUs` on the IPC payload alongside `replyToMessageId`, and have `drainIpcInput` in the agent-runner update module-scoped `latestPipedAddressedToUs` / `latestPipedReplyTo` from each pipe. The hook reads the per-pipe values when present (latest-wins), falling back to the spawn-time `containerInput.addressedToUs` for the first prompt of the spawn. The pinned `messageId` on the IPC payload also tracks per-pipe — without that, the host's reaction would land on the spawn-time message instead of the addressed pipe.
- React-first 👀 source moves back inside the container (#289, follow-up to #291 / #306). The host-side 👀 emit those PRs added gated correctly on "addressed to us" but turned the reaction into a "channel received the bytes" claim — it fired before the container existed, so distress modes (stuck container, wedged IPC, hung SDK) left a 👀 promising engagement that never landed. The original #136 contract was specifically that 👀 distinguishes "alive and reading" from "container down"; pre-firing from the host violates that. The pivot: drop host-side `sendReaction('👀')` from `src/channels/telegram.ts` (text + voice handlers), keep `evaluateTriggerGate` narrowed to `recordObserver` only (host bookkeeping for `noteLatestUserMessage`, no liveness claim). Add `ContainerInput.addressedToUs: boolean` resolved orchestrator-side via a new `isAddressedToUs(group, chatJid, messages)` helper — `isMain || (chats.is_group=0 — 1:1 DM) || trigger-match || reply-to-our-bot`, intentionally independent of `requires_trigger` (which governs answering mode, not addressed-ness). `decideReactFirst` in `container/agent-runner/src/react-first.ts` honors `addressedToUs` with a new `'not-addressed'` skip reason; the hook fires 👀 only when the orchestrator marked the inbound addressed AND the container is genuinely processing the prompt — distress modes now surface as visible silence, exactly the property #136 wanted. Hook also stamps the IPC payload with `containerInput.replyToMessageId` so the host reacts to the triggering inbound specifically rather than `reactToLatestMessage`'s chat-latest fallback (slow spawn + newer inbound between routing and hook fire would otherwise mark the wrong message). New `getChatByJid` helper in `src/db.ts` for the addressed-ness gate's `is_group` lookup. User-visible behavior: in `requires_trigger=false` multi-bot rooms (e.g. the `Old.wtf` reproduction chat) AyeAye no longer 👀-reacts to bystander chatter; in 1:1 DMs every inbound still gets a 👀 once the container engages; addressed messages on stuck containers stay un-reacted (the silence is the alarm).
- Telegram sanitizer chain convergence — second slice (#280): #282 + #286, closing the umbrella. `TelegramChannel.sendMessage` and `sendPoolMessage` now sanitize the whole message ONCE before chunking via `splitMessage` (#282). Pre-fix the order was split-then-sanitize, which let `splitMessage` cut mid-construct (between `]` and `(` of a markdown link, mid-word inside `**bold**`, mid-fence inside ` ` ```) and handed each chunk to the sanitizer as half a construct — neither half matched the sanitizer's regex, so the user saw raw markdown markers across chunks. Sanitizing first produces explicit `<a>...</a>`, `<b>...</b>`, `<pre>...</pre>`tag boundaries;`sendTelegramMessage`learns a`preSanitized: true`flag (stripped from the API payload before reaching Telegram) so chunks aren't double-sanitized and the existing single-message code path stays unchanged.`splitMessage`is now HTML-aware (#286): a new`buildSafeSplitMap`walks the input once, marking each position as safe iff splitting there leaves both halves at zero paired-tag depth and not inside a`<...>`tag. The priority ladder (code-fence boundary →`</pre>`boundary → paragraph → newline → space → latest-safe-position → hard-cut) now validates each candidate against the safety map. Paragraph and newline thresholds drop from 30% to 5% of MAX_LENGTH — pre-fix a clean`\n\n`at byte 1100 of a 4096-char chunk was rejected (below 30% = 1228) and fell through to a mid-sentence space cut or hard cut at 4096; post-fix it's accepted. Hard-cut at MAX_LENGTH stays as last resort, only firing when even no safe position exists ≤ MAX_LENGTH (e.g. a single`<pre>...</pre>`block longer than the limit — produces a chunk with an orphan tag and the sanitizer's plain-text fallback handles it, strictly less bad than a "fallback also failed" lost message). Fallback path gets a new`htmlToPlainText`helper to recover readable plain text when shipping pre-sanitized HTML through the no-parse-mode plain-text retry — pre-fix the fallback's`text`parameter was raw markdown so shipping it as plain was readable; post-fix`text`is sanitized HTML and a verbatim ship would render`<i>great</i>`as literal-tag text to the user. Tile-side`sanitize-html.py`/`sanitize-email-body.py`/`sanitize-wrapper.py`deletion still tracked separately (must flow through`staging → promote-from-host.sh`per`host-conventions.md`); the umbrella's "all sub-issues land" closure criterion is met by this PR for code-side scope.
- Telegram sanitizer chain convergence — first slice of the audit umbrella (#280): #279, #283, #281 (host-side coverage), #278. Phase 2a (link) and Phase 2b (inline-code) outputs now emit through `protect()` placeholders so a wrapping bold/italic/heading capture can no longer HTML-escape the freshly-emitted `<a>` / `<code>` tags — pre-fix `**[#271](https://x/271)**` shipped to Telegram as `<b>&lt;a href=...&gt;#271&lt;/a&gt;</b>` and rendered as literal-tag text (msg 6231 evidence in #279). Phase 3's protect-restore is now recursive so nested placeholders (a Phase 1c URL placeholder inside a Phase 2a link placeholder) expand all the way out instead of leaving raw NUL bytes in the user-visible text. Round-trip idempotency tests added against 17 fixtures spanning every Phase + broken-HTML inputs (#283), plus explicit regression coverage for the four divergence cases the Python skill sanitizers used to handle (underscored stray tags, `&apos;`/`&quot;` decode, nested protected `<code>`, top-level stray-tag escape) so host TS stays a strict superset of the Python paths and they can be retired tile-side via the staging-promote pipeline (#281). Both `sendTelegramMessage` and `sendFile` caption fallbacks now log full sanitizer input + output on HTML 400 (pre-fix the previews were truncated at 200 chars, below the typical 400-byte-offset boundary, which made post-mortem repro impossible — msg 6240 self-referential evidence in #278), prefix the degraded fallback with `⚠️ formatting failed; raw text below` / `⚠️ formatting failed; raw caption below` so the user knows they're seeing a fallback rendering rather than the agent's intent, and short-circuit the fallback entirely when `DEV_NO_HTML_FALLBACK=1` so dev/CI sees the real 400 instead of a successful degraded send masking the bug. Out of scope for this PR and tracked separately: tile-repo deletion of `sanitize-html.py` / `sanitize-email-body.py` / `sanitize-wrapper.py` (must flow through `staging → promote-from-host.sh` per `host-conventions.md`); `splitMessage` ordering and HTML-aware boundaries (#282, #286).
- `scripts/deploy.sh` step 5 now emits a window-pair marker per force-kill burst to `data/host-logs/deploy-kills.log` (#249). Captures `DEPLOY_KILL_START` before sending `_close`, appends `<start_iso>\t<end_iso>` (UTC, ms ISO 8601 produced via `python3` to dodge GNU-only `date '+%3N'`) after `xargs docker kill` returns AND only when stdout shows a container was actually killed — a holdout that exited gracefully in the race window between `docker ps` and `docker kill` doesn't produce a 137 and so doesn't justify a marker row that would otherwise false-suppress a genuine OOM 137 landing inside the fictitious window. `host-logs/` is mounted read-only into the **main/admin** agent container only — see the `isMain` block in `src/container-runner.ts::buildVolumeMounts` (host-logs are inherently cross-chat and must not leak to trusted/untrusted tiles), and the admin-tile heartbeat skill in `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin` runs there, so it reads the same file and drops 137 `task_run_logs` rows whose `run_at` falls inside any window. Trusted/untrusted agents see no `/workspace/host-logs/` and never run the suppression code path. Marker writes are best-effort with a stderr WARNING rather than `set -e`-fatal: if `data/host-logs/` is unwritable the deploy keeps going (loudly), heartbeat suppression fails open and 137s surface as before — strictly worse than suppression but observable, vs. a wedged deploy that's worse on every axis. Pre-fix every deploy that hit a stuck-agent holdout produced false-positive task-failure alerts in `system_health.issues`; deploys now emit zero spurious 137 alerts while genuine OOM 137s keep flowing through. The original "host supervisor SIGTERMing the orchestrator" framing in #213 was the wrong premise — the 137 source is deploy.sh step 5 itself, doing exactly what it advertises (forced cleanup of holdouts after the 30 s grace); docker-events evidence is in the comment thread on #249.
- New `send_message_to_chat` MCP tool — main-group only host tool that posts a plain text message into another registered chat without spawning a container there (#234, #245). Replaces the `schedule_task + once: now+5s + target_group_jid` kludge for one-shot cross-chat broadcasts. Resolves `chat_id` OR `chat_name` (mutually exclusive) against `registered_groups`; ambiguous names return candidate JIDs so the agent can disambiguate. Supports `pin: true` (direct path only — silently ignored on the bot-pool path because pool sends do not expose a pin hook) and `sender: "<name>"` (Telegram bot pool, named identity). `<internal>` tags are stripped from `text` for parity with the regular `send_message` flow. The #232 phantom-row guard carries through: a swallowed Telegram send (no `sentMsgId` returned) skips the `bot-…` row write into the target chat's `messages.db` AND surfaces a top-level `error` so the calling agent sees `isError: true` rather than a misleading "successful" response — critical for a tool whose entire purpose is sending into chats the agent isn't watching, where a phantom row would silence that chat's heartbeat / unanswered-message detection. Sender is trimmed at both the MCP boundary and the host so a whitespace-only value collapses to `undefined` instead of binding a pool bot to a blank identity. The DB row records `ASSISTANT_NAME` whenever the actual outbound went via the direct path (sender only takes effect on the pool path), so heartbeat / unanswered-cron / future audits never see a persona that didn't reach the wire. `reply_to` is deliberately out of scope (foreign chat message ids aren't reachable from main; Telegram message ids are per-chat so guessing collides). File send is a separate, not-yet-built tool. Companion admin-tile skill (`broadcast-to-chat`) ships in `jbaruch/nanoclaw-admin#93` and depends on this PR landing first.
- Installed tile content (`/home/node/.claude/skills/` and `/home/node/.claude/.tessl/`) is now mounted read-only into agent containers (#247). Pre-fix, an agent could `Write`/`Edit` over its own installed `SKILL.md` / `RULES.md` / per-tile rules from inside the container; the changes didn't survive container restart (the host-side cpSync at the top of every spawn rebuilt them) but were live for the current container's lifetime — sometimes minutes-to-hours of monkey-patched behaviour before reset. Two readonly bind-mounts now layer on top of the existing writable `/home/node/.claude` mount so the kernel rejects any write to those subdirs with `EROFS`. The parent stays writable for SDK transcript writes (`projects/<slug>/`), debug logs, todos, telemetry, session-env, and the auto-memory overlay. `tessl update` is unaffected — it runs in the orchestrator container against `/app/tessl-workspace/.tessl/tiles/`, a completely different filesystem path the agent never sees. The per-spawn cpSync into `<groupSessionsDir>` still runs host-side before the agent container starts, so the readonly overlay is not in effect during the copy. Modifications to installed tile content must go through staging → `promote_to_tile_repo` → publish → `tessl update`.
- `AGENT_MODEL` is now overridable via the orchestrator's environment (#225, cherry-picks public #56). Default unchanged at `claude-opus-4-7[1m]` so existing deployments see no behavior change; operators can set `AGENT_MODEL=opus` / `AGENT_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-6[1m]` / etc. at deploy time without editing source. Mirrors the existing `AGENT_EFFORT` env override on the agent-runner. Resolution lives in a new `resolveAgentModel()` helper in `src/container-runner.ts`: trims whitespace (so `AGENT_MODEL="  "` falls back to default rather than passing two spaces to the SDK), and warns at startup via `logger.warn` when the value doesn't begin with `claude|opus|sonnet|haiku` — surfaces typos like `claud-opus-4-7` at boot instead of as an opaque error deep in the first `query()` call. The prefix check is loose-by-design: the SDK accepts both aliases (`opus`, `sonnet[1m]`) and full IDs, the set churns with each model release, and a missed-name whitelist would block legit upgrades. `DEFAULT_AGENT_MODEL` is exported `@internal` so the unit tests can pin against the same literal the helper returns instead of duplicating the string.
- `scripts/promote-to-tile-repo.sh` now prepends an `**Author-Model:** $AUTHOR_MODEL` line to the PR body it opens against tile repos (#217). Without the declaration the gh-aw policy review pair (`review-anthropic.lock.yml` / `review-openai.lock.yml`) self-skipped promote PRs because `jbaruch/coding-policy:author-model-declaration` requires the signal — observed on tile PRs #78/#79 which never received cross-family review. `AUTHOR_MODEL` defaults to `claude-opus-4-7` and is overridable via the env var the IPC handler already passes through to the spawned bash (`...process.env`), so a non-Claude promoter can override without code changes.
- New canonical writable per-group state mount at `/workspace/state/` — sourced from `data/state/<folder>/` on the host, mounted into every container regardless of trust tier (#99 Cat 4). Solves the silent-EACCES failure mode that motivated the original audit: skills writing to `/workspace/group/` for cross-run state worked on trusted/main but silently broke on untrusted (read-only mount), and the only existing workaround was `tessl__check-unanswered`'s ad-hoc routing through `/home/node/.claude/nanoclaw-state/`. Per-group scoping (not per-session) so a scheduled task and a user-facing turn in the same group can read each other's state. Mount is always writable — `data/state/<folder>/` is `mkdir -p`'d at spawn and `chown`ed to the container user. Tier-uniform availability is the contract: `container-runner.test.ts` asserts the mount is present without `:ro` for main, trusted, and untrusted, and that the host path is `<DATA_DIR>/state/<folder>`. `docs/SPEC.md` adds a workspace-layout subsection documenting the choice between `/workspace/group/` (operator-readable, trust-tier-conditional readonly) and `/workspace/state/` (agent's persisted state, always writable). Tile-side migrations of `tessl__check-unanswered` and `tessl__brief-cleanup` ship as separate issues on `nanoclaw-core` and `nanoclaw-admin`.
- `nuke_session` MCP tool gains a `skipReentry` argument (#127, follow-up to #104). When `skipReentry: true`, the orchestrator deletes the per-group checkpoint pair (`<groupDir>/.checkpoints/default.md` + `previous.md`) after the standard wipe steps so the next container spawn has no Facts/Reasoning to load via the reentry skill. Default behaviour (false / omitted) preserves the checkpoint, since the standard nuke is "fresh session, but reentry continues to work". `skipReentry` is for the case where the checkpoint itself is suspect (poisoned plan, stale do-not-re-execute list); without it the operator's only workaround was a manual `rm` from the host. Implemented via a new `clearCheckpoints(groupDir)` helper exported from `src/checkpoint.ts` (idempotent: ENOENT on either file returns the count of files actually unlinked). Strict boolean check on the IPC payload — anything non-true falls back to preserving the checkpoint, so a malformed payload can't accidentally erase reentry state.
- Kill-auto-compaction Phases 1–4 land behind `ENABLE_THRESHOLD_NUKE` (#125, #122, #123, #128, tracks #104; design at `docs/proposals/kill-auto-compaction.md`). Token-usage telemetry is **always-on**: every per-turn `usage` payload from the SDK's `stream-json` output (`input_tokens` / `output_tokens` / cache fields) is logged via `logger.info({…}, 'session_tokens')`, with WARN at the warn threshold and ERROR at the nuke threshold. Threshold formula is `min(70% of context, context-200K)` warn / `min(80%, context-100K)` nuke; for Opus 4.7 1M that's 700K warn / 800K nuke. The `## Facts` checkpoint writer fires at threshold-cross (always-on, validates the file format on real transcripts before the destructive flip relies on it), rotating `default.md → previous.md` first then writing the orchestrator-deterministic Facts section to `<group>/.checkpoints/default.md` — mutating-tool taxonomy filter (Write, Edit, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit, Skill, every `mcp__nanoclaw__*` mutator, Task, TeamCreate/Delete) plus a Bash read-only argv allowlist (cat, ls, grep, find, head, tail, wc, sort, uniq, pwd, echo, file, stat, date, plus `git status/log/diff/show/rev-parse`) keeps the "do NOT re-execute" list focused on real state changes. The agent-runner's existing SessionStart auto-context hook now ALSO loads `/workspace/group/.checkpoints/default.md` as a CHECKPOINT section (last, closest to the prompt for max salience) so the next session after a nuke picks up the prior facts deterministically. A user-invocable `/session-reentry` skill ships as the manual fallback. **Behaviorally inert by default** — `ENABLE_THRESHOLD_NUKE=0` keeps SDK auto-compaction on and the orchestrator's `nuke_session` call gated off; the orchestrator only logs `threshold_nuke_inert (ENABLE_THRESHOLD_NUKE=0)` at warn-level when the threshold crosses. When the operator flips `ENABLE_THRESHOLD_NUKE=1`, `DISABLE_COMPACT=1` is set on every container spawn AND the threshold-cross handler calls `nuke_session` for the default slot after the current turn completes — the next inbound spawns a fresh container which auto-loads the checkpoint via the SessionStart hook. The flag-off period is the data-collection window: ssh nas + grep `'session_tokens|threshold_nuke'` on host-logs to curve real-session usage distributions before flipping.
- `wipeSessionJsonl` now also removes the sibling per-session tool-results directory at `<slug>/<sessionId>/` alongside the JSONL transcript (#202, follow-up to #193). The SDK writes tool-call result snapshots (image attachments, search outputs) into that directory; pre-fix it was orphaned by every scheduled-task run and by every `nukeSession` call (since the helper is shared). Implemented via a new `removeToolResultsDirInSlug` helper that mirrors `unlinkJsonlInSlug`'s lstat → branch on type → realpath-containment discipline: a symlink at the dir path is unlinked as a link (target preserved), a regular file is left alone (logs warn — not the SDK's shape), and a real directory is removed via `fs.rmSync({ recursive: true })` with `ENOENT` handled explicitly in the catch so the returned delete count stays accurate (a concurrent cleanup that vanishes the path between lstat and rm returns 0, not 1). Node's `rmSync` does not follow symlinks during recursion, so a compromised container that scattered host-pointing symlinks inside its own tool-results dir cannot redirect the wipe outward. The `wipeSessionJsonl` return count is now up to 2 per slug (1 transcript + 1 tool-results dir) rather than 0–1.
- Added `unregister_group` MCP tool — main-group only inverse of `register_group` (#159). Removes the `registered_groups` row, cascade-deletes scheduled_tasks tied to the unregistered folder, and refreshes `available_groups.json` in one call; refuses to touch the main-group registration; leaves the on-disk `groups/<folder>/` directory intact (operators delete that manually). Companion one-shot cleanup at `initDatabase()` drops the dormant `tg:1698969` / `telegram_main` row that lingered because there was no inverse path until now. Companion drift detector logs (does not auto-delete) any `registered_groups` row whose JID has no matching `chats` row at startup, so future drift surfaces immediately.
- [BREAKING] Removed the auto-create heartbeat rule for non-main groups (#158). Heartbeat is now opt-in via `containerConfig.enableHeartbeat` instead of being implicit on `requiresTrigger !== false` (a flag no group ever actually had set). Existing heartbeat rows are preserved (the startup prompt-migration still rewrites legacy prompts on any non-main row that exists), and the main-group heartbeat is unchanged. `setGroupTrigger` no longer touches heartbeat lifecycle — trigger config and heartbeat opt-in are now orthogonal. The `register_group` MCP tool exposes the new `enableHeartbeat` parameter so opt-in flows through the same channel as other registration config.
